# Multi-team audit — 2026-07-29

Six review teams (architecture, roadmap, process, tests, economy, devil's-advocate) were
run against this repo, their top proposals adversarially verified (three were refuted or
corrected — including one fabricated roadmap citation the verify pass caught), then
synthesized. The devil's-advocate team was lost to an infrastructure error; the synthesis
rests on the other five. This document records the FINDINGS; none of the proposals below
are decisions until acted on.

## Verdict

Micro-level engineering is genuinely strong: differential live gates with honest verdicts
(losses and retractions reported as-is), provenance-wiped economics, a fail-closed
capability authority boundary, 1327 tests in under 4 seconds. What is not going well is
structural: the project keeps paying for ONE defect class — a single world-fact defined in
two places (decide rule vs capability gate; runner N vs runner N+1) synchronized only by
memory. Six live failures trace to it, and **the audit found a seventh, latent today**:
`mage_life.py`'s `fetch_gold` rule ignores the `PICKUP_RADIUS` and pack-cap its gate
enforces (the carpenter's fix was never back-ported). Meanwhile the flagship "Lives" run
on scripted cognition (`_LifeClient` echoes `decide_mode`'s choice as fake LLM JSON) with
no Phase-6 persistence wired in — so the AI-native showcase is currently a hand-written
FSM village whose live hours accumulate no society state — and both deployed craft loops
destroy value at vendor prices (throne −33g; dagger batch −25g once staged iron runs out;
an Ettin pays ~157g vs +10g for the best craft trip).

## Top risks

1. **The dominant failure class does not converge under the current process** — six
   incidents fixed one at a time; the seventh (mage fetch_gold) already exists.
2. **Lives bypass the LLM brain and Phase-6 persistence** — the differentiators sit idle
   while deterministic rules do everything.
3. **Craft loops are value-destroying by construction** — a society scaled on them decays
   to bankruptcy, and gold-per-life fitness would rank crafters below doing nothing.
4. **Resumability contract broken** — CLAUDE.md's "Next:" (~90 commits stale) contradicts
   AUTONOMY-ROADMAP.md §E; this session's live proofs live in uncommitted scratchpad
   scripts.
5. **Scaling cliff at ~3–4 concurrent agents** — hand-tuned pump budgets, insertion-order
   capability priority, string-key contracts across two memories per agent.

## Proposals (verified, ranked by leverage)

| # | Proposal | Effort |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Rule↔gate concordance suite**: for every Life, generate observations across the drift axes (worn/packed/ground tools, radius edges, threshold edges) and assert `decide_mode`'s pick is always in `ready_capability_ids` — plus a reverse gate-ready-but-never-wanted check. Fails on the mage drift on day one. | small |
| 2 | **Runtime disagreement detector in `WarriorLife.tick`** (~15 lines, inherited by all Lives): rule wants a cap, no goal admitted, cap absent from ready for ~10 ticks → loud self-labelled line. Goal-stack guard is mandatory (gates de-assert mid-transaction). | small |
| 3 | **LifeRunner harness**: extract the five verbatim runner blocks (staging+readback, provenance wipe, telemetry, leash-on-both-memories, monitor) so profession N+1 inherits every lesson **by construction** — omission becomes a TypeError, not a live rediscovery. | medium |
| 4 | **Reconcile the two roadmaps in writing**: fix CLAUDE.md's stale "Next:", quote AUTONOMY-ROADMAP §E's actual criterion verbatim, trim the 37KB phase essay to a <150-line entry point. Cheapest insurance against the one multi-hour live-budget mistake a stale pointer can cause. | small |
| 5 | **Life thresholds → constructor parameters**, each routed through its single source (memory key / capability attr) so decide and gate cannot diverge — the prerequisite for genome axes, bandit tuning, and slow-loop steering to touch them. | small |
| 6 | **Commit the economy to its researched profitable configuration**: net-gold/hour on the monitor line; flagship chain moved to a positive-margin pair (miner→tinker tongs, 7g/ingot); lumberjack→carpenter marked "mechanism-proof, economically frozen"; a narrowed price-table tripwire test. | medium |
| 7 | **Fix MockBody's PickUp/Drop and build the closed-loop two-Life simulation** — the only offline test kind that catches the cross-agent/cross-time class (one-memory leash, budget-exhausted worker). Currently `PickUp` deletes the item and `Drop` is a no-op, so the supply-chain mechanism is unrepresentable offline. | medium |
| 8 | **Wire Phase-6 persistence into Life runners, then one real LLM-steering slice in MageLife** (LLM chooses only when 2+ branches are simultaneously admissible; code composes the candidate set; differential scripted-vs-LLM gate). Depends on #3. | medium |

Refuted by verification and dropped/corrected: the original roadmap-reconcile text (it
quoted a criterion that appears nowhere in the repo), the broad economy-table test (the
era-drift claim was mechanically false; narrowed), and `sell_ingots` for the miner (not a
capability-registry profession; inert as specced).

## Stop doing

- Copy-pasting runner #6 from runner #5 — no new profession until the harness exists.
- Writing rule-side copies of gate predicates.
- Citing uncommitted scratchpad scripts as live proof.
- Scaling/showcasing the lumberjack→carpenter pair as an economy.
- Appending full phase narrative to CLAUDE.md.
- Running Phase 7 item 2 before the roadmap reconciliation lands.

## Keep doing

Differential live gates and as-is verdicts; provenance wipes; agreement-pinning postmortem
tests; ServUO-source-cited economics BEFORE building loops; root-cause commit essays; the
want/admitted/ready telemetry philosophy; the <4s offline suite.

## Implementation record (2026-07-29/30)

All eight proposals were implemented in order, each with its own commit and evidence:

1. **Concordance suite** — failed on the latent mage drift on its first run, exactly as
   predicted; fixed (`FETCH_GOLD_PACK_CAP` single-sourced). Later caught a genuinely
   dead `bank_gold` branch in the brand-new TinkerLife BEFORE its first live run.
2. **Runtime detector** — `WarriorLife.rule_gate_disagreement`, inherited by all
   Lives; no-goal guard pinned against mid-transaction false fires; stayed silent
   through a healthy live smoke (zero false alarms) — the other half of correctness.
3. **LifeRunner harness** — village.py runners de-duplicated (~230 lines removed);
   `LifeSpec` makes omission a TypeError. Live smoke: the harnessed carpenter repeated
   its full buy->craft->sell cycle identically to the pre-refactor runner.
4. **Roadmaps reconciled** — CLAUDE.md is an 83-line entry point; §E quoted verbatim;
   the 37KB narrative moved verbatim to docs/HISTORY.md; DESIGN.md §10 carries the
   autonomy thread and the economically-frozen note.
5. **Thresholds → constructor knobs** — `bank_reserve`/`econ_grace` params;
   `tick()` hands decide the econ agent's OWN memory, so the old drift avenue is
   structurally impossible; fixed the warrior's and mage's latent
   threshold-vs-reserve conflations (reserves now derived: 376g re-arm kit / 180g =
   the fetch cap).
6. **Economy committed** — price-table tripwire (keyed by GRAPHIC after its own first
   draft false-fired on a multi-art entry); net g/h meter; **TinkerLife + fetch_iron +
   the forge pair**: live, both agents provenance-broke, the chain ran
   deliver->fetch->craft->sell to **+140g at ~+2000g/h** — against the frozen
   carpenter chain's −33g/throne. The one anomaly — an admitted `bank_gold` goal that
   did not progress in that first run — was closed by a dedicated forced-state gate
   (`live_tinker_bank_gate.py`): the same Life, spots, NPCs and 140g deposited
   flawlessly in 12 ticks (popup→settle→deposit, pack settling at exactly the 82g
   reserve), so the FSM is exonerated and the wedge stands as a single unreproduced
   mid-run state interaction — with the bank-FSM stage telemetry now resident in the
   pair runner to self-diagnose any recurrence.
7. **Mock item physics + the two-Life sim** — PickUp/Drop/Equip with conservation;
   the REAL WoodsmanLife delivered to the REAL CarpenterLife entirely offline with
   four per-tick oracles. Equip earned its place honestly: the first Life ever ticked
   over the mock wedged on the axe-equip two-step.
8. **LLM steering + persistence** — BOTH live gates passed. Steering
   (`live_steering_gate.py`, forced two-candidate overlap after the natural-overlap
   run logged zero consults): `['buy_reagent','fetch_gold'] -> fetch_gold (LLM)` and
   the agent admitted exactly that goal — a real model overrode the rule's first
   choice, inside the closed vocabulary. Persistence: run A persisted "chop has paid
   off: +0.9 reward over 9 turns." and run B — a new OS process — resumed it verbatim
   at construction. CORRECTION (post-hoc forensics): that insight's text exactly
   matches the HeuristicReflection template (cognition.py:418), so its AUTHOR was the
   heuristic fallback, not the LLM — LLM traffic flowed in the window (with two fast
   provider failures) but LLM authorship of the persisted insight is NOT established.
   The persistence MECHANISM (write + verbatim cross-process resume) stands proven;
   the earlier commit's "reflected through the real tiered LLM" overstated. The
   audit's identity risk is answered by the STEERING gate regardless — there the LLM's
   choice demonstrably changed the agent's behavior.


## Post-implementation health check (2026-07-30)

A four-team verification workflow + one live run, after all eight proposals landed.
Static layer: fully green (suite, imports, CLI, invariants held structurally, docs
verbatim-checked). Live: a fresh supply-pair run completed its handover cleanly with
zero warnings. The forensics team's raw verdict of "broken" over-read several
deliberately-killed runs as silent deaths, but four of its findings are real:

1. **Harvest relocation never fires in the forge runner** — Grimm has no
   nodes/spot-pool wired, so a drained vein ends the economy (forge2's dead run,
   forge3's zero-reward tail). Fix: wire a spot pool like foundry/eval.py's.
2. **Steering has no ledger role** — consults log as 'chatter'; add a 'steering' role
   so the flagship feature leaves an auditable trace.
3. **The persistence insight was heuristic-authored** — corrected above.
4. **Pipeline monitor starvation — ROOT-CAUSED and fixed.** Not GIL, not process
   priority: the steering-evidence block had been inserted one indent level too
   shallow, which silently moved every status print OUT of the while loop — the loop
   ran (and slept) for four hours printing nothing, then the dedented prints ran once
   at exit. The "5x tick slowdown" claim dissolves with it (it compared a full run's
   duration against another run's observed sample window). Fixes: correct indentation
   with a load-bearing comment; the loop now prints FIRST and sleeps after; every
   block carries `[mon#N +gap T+elapsed]` so cadence anomalies are measurable in-log;
   `_ThrottledAgent` yields with a 10ms scheduling pause instead of a GIL-hot spin;
   worker status lines carry a tick counter.

Also worth adopting from the same report: a liveness line for NON-Life agents (the
forge miner died silently — `out+0.0 steps=0` for 72 samples with nothing flagging
it), and noting that stale finished-goals (want=None with an admitted goal lingering)
are a distinct shape the disagreement detector deliberately does not cover.

*(**ADOPTED 2026-08-03 — after the same failure happened a SECOND time.** "Worth adopting"
sat here unlanded for four days; on 2026-08-03 the forge miner died silently again, with the
same signature on the same runner (§6.5, follow-up 17), and only then was the line built:
§8.1 has the alarm, the measured 240-tick threshold and the false-positive class that nearly
made it useless. Recorded as a PROCESS finding, not a code one — one sighting was treated as
an anecdote and it took two. **Half of the item is still open:** the alarm says an agent
STOPPED PRODUCING; it still does not say whether the agent is ALIVE, because
`run_forge_pair`'s status line calls `hp_readout` for neither agent. That half is now
follow-up 18, and it is the third time this document has named it.)*

*(Updated 2026-08-03. That last clause is still true of the DETECTOR and is still
deliberate — `_detect_disagreement` requires an empty goal stack — but it no longer means
the shape is unreported. It was first observed live on 2026-08-03, root-caused as a
different mechanism than the phrase implies (the frame's owner agent stops being ticked,
upstream of the finished-goal case), and fixed offline the same day: see the 2026-08-03
entry §3 and §5, and follow-up 10. `want=None` with an admitted goal is now the NORMAL
shape of an owed transaction and the telemetry names which one it is (`+hold` vs
`!frozen` vs `!overdue`); the residue the detector cannot see gets its own report,
`frame_overdue`.)*


## The natural-deposit campaign (2026-07-30, commit a1b0609)

The request "run the forge pair until banking happens NATURALLY" turned into a
five-defect excavation — and a correction to finding #1 above. The full narrative
lives in a1b0609's commit message; the record here keeps the audit honest:

- **Finding #1's diagnosis was wrong twice.** "No spot pool wired" was not the
  hole: relocation IS inherited through the forge chain. The real blockers were
  (a) **tool exhaustion** — both staged pickaxes wore out (ServUO Pickaxe = 50
  uses) and a toolless harvester makes no swings, so the relocation window can
  never fill (forge4: GM-adding one pick revived the miner instantly, 30.3 ->
  122.3); and (b) **window dilution** — a live probe watched invalid-target
  replies ("You can't mine that", ~half the trade spot's probe ring) count as
  "not stuck", so a fully dead bank rated ~0.5 and needed 142 ticks to confess.
  Relocation sampling is now outcome-only (probe verdict: fires at t=48), tools
  scale with the budget and confess when gone, and completed relocations re-home
  the delivery return spot.
- **The bank starvation was a RULE property, not an FSM bug** (the forced-state
  gate had already exonerated the FSM): the patient bank branch below craft only
  fires in a supply gap. A pockets-full band (reserve + one restock) now makes
  banking urgent above 157g — concordance-pinned on both edges.
- **The review pass earned its cost**: five confirmed defects fixed before any
  live budget was spent, including the bank_reserve raw-vs-clamped read that
  recreated the rule-vs-gate drift class through the tuning knob itself.
- **forge8, everything landed**: four sales to 140g, a genuine supply gap, and
  the patient branch deposited **58g on its own** — bank(stage=settle), pack
  settling at exactly the 82g reserve, followed unprompted by a 75g iron
  restock and the next craft batch. Deposit -> working capital -> reinvestment,
  provenance-clean. The forge1 "admitted bank_gold that did not progress"
  anomaly did not recur.
- **Infra footnote**: 1123 auto-created accounts finally crossed ServUO's
  AccountsPerIp=999 — every fresh-account run had begun rolling dice on login
  (forge7's "sell wedge" day and two aborted launches trace here). Raised to
  99999; the restart also reset the runtime ore banks.

**Addendum (same day, commits `f0b7a9~..`)**: the relocation window stayed empty in
the RUNNER even after the sampler fixes — the final thief was `_run_worker` itself,
which called `body.observe()` after every tick for its status pulse. `new_journal`
is since-last-observe: a second consumer on one body steals the batch, so every
journal-borne signal (relocation verdicts, tool-gone clilocs) silently vanished for
every PLAIN agent in every pair runner since the runner was written — the Lives were
immune only because `_CachingBody` exists for exactly this. The worker now reads the
tick's own `_last_observation` (pinned: 5 ticks = exactly 5 observes). forge11
live-proof: `win=7/8 -> 13/16 -> 18/21` filling in the runner for the first time,
then a relocation CHAIN (2611,474 -> 2610,484 -> 2606,499) walking off the drained
mountain autonomously. The lesson joins the mis-indented monitor and the `_why`
wipe: telemetry that touches shared state is not free — and the bug was never in
the skill, but in the harness watching the skill.

**Terrain + lifecycle addendum (commits `d09a636`, `dd5a0e5`)**: two more root causes
behind the same "the economy stops and nothing says why" surface.

1. **Relocation was terrain-blind.** The machinery worked (forge11: window fills,
   fires, chains) but walked a blind compass — forge12 burned a six-spot pool in
   minutes, half of it on the mountain's WRONG FLANK. `uomap.py` now reads the UOP
   land map (ported from v1) and `find_mine_spots()` surveys stand tiles beside real
   rock, one per 8x8 HarvestBank cell, path-filtered by BFS over walkable land. It
   rediscovered the hand-calibrated Phase-3 trade stand from map data alone.
   Relocation consumes the survey as an ITINERARY (nodes installed on arrival,
   within one tile — the last step beside a wall is where greedy walking deflects).
   forge13 live: home vein died -> pool #1 -> fresh bank -> died -> pool #2, each
   hop landing exactly on the surveyed stand.
2. **`bank_gold`'s forge1 anomaly, finally root-caused** — and it was never the FSM
   (the forced-state gate that exonerated it was right). A capability run that ENDS
   without achieving (the FSM gives up and walks home) marked its goal id finished,
   after which every step no-opped while `CapabilityGoalComplete` refused to close an
   unachieved frame: the goal sat admitted until its deadline. An offline
   Life-over-MockBody repro made it deterministic in one afternoon — the complement
   to "force the state under test": **forced-state gates find FSM bugs, offline
   repros find LIFECYCLE bugs**. Wrappers now leave a neutral run-finished marker and
   the frame closes as FAILURE; a transient giveup costs one trip, not the day.

**The forced-state gates, as a roster** — these are the `live_*_gate.py` scripts that exist
to make one otherwise-unreachable state happen on a shard, as opposed to the `live_*.py`
one-shot proofs of a working skill. Kept here because this is where the method is stated:

| gate | the state it forces | verdict |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `live_urgent_bank_gate.py` | the urgent-bank BAND with craft genuinely also ready, plus a counterfactual through the real rule on the SAME observation | passed (commit `a2819c9`) — see the paragraph below |
| `live_steering_gate.py` | a two-candidate overlap the natural trajectory does not produce (the natural-overlap run logged zero consults) | passed — implementation record item 8: `['buy_reagent','fetch_gold'] -> fetch_gold (LLM)` and the agent admitted exactly that |
| `live_frame_overdue_gate.py` | **an economy frame past its deadline that CANNOT yield** — the exit-edge hold's third bound, unreachable by an ordinary run because `expire_due` wins the comparator race by one tick whenever the frame can yield | passed first attempt, 2026-08-03 — **§7** |

`live_tinker_bank_gate.py` is the structural template all three follow: spawn body →
`GmControl.stage` → build the Life → drive `life.tick()` in-process → `print_gate_verdict`.

forge14 (everything landed): survey -> two pool relocations -> four sales -> **93g
deposited at 175g, pack settling at exactly the 82g reserve** -> 75g iron restock ->
next batch crafted and sold. Net +135g. Scope note, now closed: that deposit had iron=3
(below a craft batch), so both branches would have fired. The band's EXCLUSIVE proof
came from a dedicated forced-state gate (`live_urgent_bank_gate.py`, commit a2819c9):
a full batch of iron in the pack (craft genuinely ready — `ready_capability_ids`
returns BOTH), gold over the band, and a counterfactual run through the REAL rule on
the SAME observation with `bank_reserve` raised so the patient branch stays eligible
while the band goes out of reach. Result: wired rule -> `bank_gold`, counterfactual ->
`craft_tongs`, agent admitted the bank, deposited 93g, pack settled at exactly 82.
One knob apart, one observation, opposite decisions — that is the band and nothing
else. Its first attempts also exposed a REAL defect the gate was not
looking for: `_find_market_mobile` resolves shops by nearest-to-requested-spot, and a
vendor and a banker staged one tile either side of that spot are equidistant — the tie
broke toward whichever the observation listed first, so the tinker asked the *Tinker*
NPC to open a bank box and threw the trip away one tick later (`_NO_ENTRY`, silent).
`stage_shops` now pins `{(readback x,y): serial}` and the resolver prefers the pin
(commit dcc9552); the gate went 1/3 to 3/3. Identity-not-distance, applied one layer
below where staging had already applied it.

## Stale-UI wedge (2026-07-31, forge15-18)

A full-stack forge-pair verification found the ONE defect the whole autonomy stack was
blind to, and the diagnosis chain is worth keeping as a template:

- **forge15** (symptom): Pim wanted `fetch_iron` for 671 straight ticks with 38 delivered
  ingots at his feet and `ready=[]`. Sixteen readiness gates share one "idle UI" clause,
  so a SINGLE open surface refuses every capability at once. The disagreement detector
  (audit proposal 2) was the only thing that named it — its whole reason for existing.
- **forge16** (identity): the pair status line gained a `ui=` field, and the next wedge
  said `ui=shopbuy` — a vendor BUY window left behind by a finished `buy_iron` trip, 357
  disagreement ticks. Not a gump: the first repair (gump-only) could not have helped.
- **forge18** (repair, live): the detector now REPAIRS on firing — closing a gump
  (`GumpResponse` button 0) or cancelling a vendor window (an EMPTY `BuyItems`/`SellItems`
  list, which ServUO answers with `EndVendorBuy`). Live: `** Pim: closing an unowned
  vendor BUY window **`, after which `buy_iron` was ADMITTED again — the wedge cleared and
  the economy resumed (+165g at that point, 88g banked over two deposits).

The wedge RECURRED later in the same run — `buy_iron` admitted, `ui=shopbuy`, the agent
motionless — and I called it a second, still-open defect while watching it. That was
premature: the repair deliberately waits while a goal owns the surface, and once that
goal closed the detector fired again, cancelled the window again, and the economy resumed
(gold 77 -> 112 -> 147; two repairs, two recoveries, final net **+235g at ~1900g/h** with
88g banked). So the shape is SELF-HEALING but slow: each recurrence costs one goal
lifetime before the repair may act.

**That gap is now CLOSED (commit `8cdd2f0`, current HEAD)**, and it was real: the buy FSM had
a timeout for a window that never ARRIVES but none for a window already open when it did not
expect one, so each recurrence spent requests the server ignores while a window is up, ran its
counter to `POPUP_TIMEOUT`, threw the trip away, and only THEN let the Life's repair act. Both
popup stages (`_buy_step`, `_sell_step` in `anima2/skills/market.py`) now inspect an
already-open window FIRST: if it belongs to the vendor we came for, USE it — skip straight to
the order, since it is the very window the popup was going to open; if it belongs to anyone
else, cancel it with an EMPTY item list, which is ServUO's `EndVendorBuy` (the same close the
Life-layer repair uses). The sell stage also cancels a stale BUY window, since either surface
blocks it. Belt and braces on purpose: the trip handles the case it can see, the Life handles
the case where no trip exists to see it.

This doc's own prescribed method was followed — reproduced offline against MockBody (which
models `shop_buy` and the empty-list cancel) rather than hunted live: three FSM-layer tests
(ours-is-used, stranger's-is-cancelled, and the no-window path unchanged), suite at 1404.

**The honest half**: the FSM-layer fix has OFFLINE TESTS ONLY. No live run has exercised it
yet, so live re-verification is still outstanding — only the LIFE-layer repair is live-proven
(forge18, above). *(A live run on 2026-08-03 did not exercise the FSM-layer fix's case at
all — no trip started against a window someone else had left — but it did show the COST
this section says was closed, back in full: the wedge recurred three times in one run, each
recurrence burning a whole `buy_iron` budget, and the last never cleared — 556 dead ticks.
The paragraph is left standing as written; evidence and two falsifiable candidate mechanisms
are in the 2026-08-03 entry, §6.7, now follow-up 16.)* And a stale target CURSOR remains
deliberately unrepaired at both layers: the
body's own target state is not the Life's to clear, and cancelling one mid-flight would race
the skill that opened it.


## Proposal 5 was WIRELESS — and proposal 1's defect class was still live (2026-08-02)

Two re-examinations of already-"done" work, both of which found the mechanism present and
the wiring absent. The pattern is worth naming on its own: **a proposal implemented at the
callee and never at the caller reads as landed from every angle except the one that
matters.** The implementation record above marks both #1 and #5 complete, honestly — the
code each describes does exist. **The gap is a different one for each, and an earlier
draft of this paragraph flattened them into "neither could be USED", which is false of #1
and contradicted by this document's own record of it:**

- **#5's channel could not be used at all.** No production site could pass a threshold, so
  nothing outside the tests ever did (details below).
- **#1's suite could be used, was used, and earned its keep** — implementation record #1
  above: it failed on the latent mage drift on its first run and later caught a genuinely
  dead `bank_gold` branch in a brand-new `TinkerLife` before that Life's first live run.
  Its defect is narrower and it is coverage: the suite was BLIND along three whole axes at
  once — every fixture injected a backpack, every knob was pinned at its module default
  (where rule and gate agree by construction), and both craft lattices pinned `craft_spot`
  to the player's own tile. Four distinct rule-vs-gate disagreement classes were sitting
  inside those blind spots — the three pack-absent fetches recorded below and the
  carpenter's craft, whose batch clause is reachable on a shipped runner today. A suite
  that runs and passes is not the same as a suite that looks.

**Proposal 5 ("Life thresholds → constructor parameters").** The record says
"`bank_reserve`/`econ_grace` params". True — and `LifeSpec.life_factory` was typed
`(body, persona, routes) -> Life`, `LifeRunner.run` called it with exactly those three
positional arguments, and both shipped factories in `village.py` were
`lambda body, persona, routes: ...`, which would have raised `TypeError` on any keyword. So
no production site could pass a threshold and none ever did — only tests, by constructing a
Life directly. The prerequisite the proposal existed to create ("for genome axes, bandit
tuning, and slow-loop steering to touch them") was therefore never created, and CLAUDE.md's
deferral precondition (a) was nominally satisfied by it. What closed the gap:

- `LifeSpec.knobs` (a dict forwarded as keyword arguments) and `LifeRunner.build_life()` —
  a NAMED seam, because `run()` needs a live shard, so a knob channel proved only by
  re-implementing that one line in a test is a channel asserted but not wired. Offline
  tests and the live path now call the same method.
- Both `village.py` `LifeSpec` factories take and forward `**knobs` — and, as of the same
  day, both `LifeSpec` RUNNERS grew the caller-side end of it:
  `run_carpenter_life(knobs=...)` / `run_woodsman_life(knobs=...)` pass
  `LifeSpec(knobs=dict(knobs or {}))`. Copied, not aliased: a sweep script that reuses one
  dict must not be able to retune a Life that is already built. Default `None` keeps every
  existing caller byte-identical. Five of the seven construction sites still have no entry
  point — follow-up 2.
- `anima2/knobs.py` — the ONE clamped read: `knob_int(memory, key, default)` for a knob a
  `decide` staticmethod must see (it can only see memory), `knob_param(value, default)` for
  one only `tick()` reads, `type(value) is int` so a `True` clamps instead of passing as 1.
  `skills/market.py::_bank_reserve` (name, signature, docstring intact) is now that read
  bound to its key. This generalizes the lesson the natural-deposit campaign paid for above
  — "the bank_reserve raw-vs-clamped read that recreated the rule-vs-gate drift class
  through the tuning knob itself" — so knob N+1 inherits it without anybody remembering.
- Two new knobs (`WarriorLife(disagreement_ticks=...)`, floor 1 because floor 0 makes
  `streak >= window` true every tick and a HEALTHY Life would then self-report and fire
  `_clear_stale_ui` on every tick; `TinkerLife(bank_trip_surplus=...)`), and `econ_grace`
  hardened through the same clamp — at **floor 2**, which is a correction: it shipped at
  `knob_param`'s default floor of 0, where every malformed write (`-1`, `2.5`, `True`,
  `"6"`) landed on a Life with NO hysteresis, the exact mid-equip failure `ECON_GRACE`
  exists to prevent, while the adjacent comment claimed the clamp ruled that out. Floor 1
  would not have fixed it either — `_econ_streak` is incremented before
  `streak < grace` is tested, so 0 and 1 both switch on the first tick. Each floor is now
  the smallest value that still does the job its knob exists for, and each is pinned by a
  test. Review-caught, offline.
- **The concordance suite was blind to knobs.** It pinned each knob at its module default,
  where rule and gate agree BY CONSTRUCTION — a rule-side-only conversion passed untouched.
  It now sweeps every lattice point at eight settings per knob (wired / tuned down / tuned
  up / absent / negative / float / bool / string), in BOTH directions: the malformed rows
  catch a raw rule-side read, and `tuned_up` catches the opposite shape, a rule that cannot
  see its own knob. 1404 → 1419 tests, ~0.08s → 0.49s — both figures are this bullet's
  state when it was written, not the shipped tree's; the `craft_spot` (×5) and vendor (×2)
  axes landed after this snapshot and the file finishes the round at **1.87s** (7 tests,
  the tinker alone 1.47s). Stated because the "Keep doing" list names the <4s offline
  suite as a practice to protect, so the per-file cost is the number that matters.

**Proposal 1's defect class, found still live in the readbacks.** Twenty hand-copied
observation readbacks across the five Life modules (plus four in `life_runner.py`) are now
one definition each in `anima2/obsview.py`. Merging them exposed a real readback defect,
not just duplication: `carpenter._owns`, `tinker._owns_tool` and `woodsman._has_axe` wrote
`i.container in (bp, player)` WITHOUT the `bp is not None` guard `warrior._has_weapon` had
from the start. A ground item's container is `None`, so with our own pack out of the
observation a saw/axe/tongs lying on the ground read as OWNED while
`capabilities._owned_tool` (guarded since it was written) correctly refused.

*Two corrections to the first draft of this entry, both measured rather than argued.*
**(i)** It said the rule then "walks on to craft toolless". That is unreachable: with no
pack in the observation `pack_amount` answers 0 for the boards/iron every craft branch
requires, so no craft branch can fire. What actually happens is that the rule SKIPS
`fetch_saw`/`buy_saw` and falls through to a LOWER branch — carpenter with boards on the
ground → `fetch_boards`, carpenter without → `hunt`, woodsman → `hunt`. **(ii)** The
`owns` guard alone does not close the disagreement in that state, it renames it. All five
fetch gates open with `_backpack_serial(ctx) is not None` (`_make_tool_fetch_ready`,
`_make_fetch_ready`, `_make_fetch_ready_set`, `_fetch_ready`), and the merged
`on_ground`/`ground_amount` did not carry that clause — so the guarded carpenter went from
wanting `fetch_boards` against `ready=[]` to wanting `fetch_saw` against `ready=[]`, and
the woodsman, which wanted NOTHING here at HEAD, newly wanted `fetch_hatchet`. Measured on
a 30,000-state random sweep of `(observation, memory)`, HEAD and the working tree in one
process: 2,009 newly-disagreeing woodsman states and 611 newly-disagreeing carpenter ones
(14 further carpenter states merely renamed a refusal that was already there). On a healthy
agent each of those prints a disagreement and closes a UI surface every tick. Review-caught
before any live run and fixed once, in the same place: `on_ground` and `ground_amount` now
require our pack too, exactly as `owns` does. A HEAD-vs-now differential over the same
30,000 states then shows the only behavior changes the READBACK MERGE leaves are
pack-absent ones, every one of them a fetch the gate refused becoming `hunt`: mage
`fetch_gold`, tinker `fetch_iron` and carpenter `fetch_boards` — **three** halves, each
latent since the day it was written (`tinker_life.py`'s own banner records that that Life
was written AFTER this audit and still copy-pasted `_owns_tool` and `_iron_on_ground` from
the carpenter, which is the clearest evidence there is that the copies were the defect).
With a backpack present the readback merge alone is behavior-identical to HEAD across all
five Lives.

*Corpus note, because the counts in this document do not all come from one, and one of
them cannot be reproduced from this document at all.* The first draft of the sentence above
named only two of the three fetches — it omitted the tinker — and cited "mage `fetch_gold`
×291, carpenter `fetch_boards` ×41" from that session's own seeded harness, whose seed was
never written down. Re-measured 2026-08-02 with a seeded harness — described here in full
but **NOT committed**, so "re-runnable" means re-buildable from this description, not
checked out and executed; that is a weaker claim than the first draft of this sentence
made, and the missing fifth row below is what the difference cost. `random.Random(20260802)`,
**30,000 randomized `(observation, memory)` states per Life** (150,000 total — backpack
present in half, each item placed pack / worn / ground-in-reach / ground-out-of-reach / a
neighbour's pack / absent, every route key present / absent / malformed, and every knob
key **that exists at HEAD** — i.e. `bank_reserve` — the same way). That last scoping is
the correction, not a clarification: `bank_trip_surplus` becomes a memory knob only in the
working tree, the sweep did not vary it, and the class it moves is the row this table was
missing. HEAD `8cdd2f0` and the working tree ran as two processes off the same seed and
were diffed row for row:

| behaviour change, HEAD -> now | states | class |
|---|---|---|
| mage `fetch_gold` -> `hunt` (pack ABSENT) | 2255 | readback merge |
| tinker `fetch_iron` -> `hunt` (pack ABSENT) | 995 | readback merge |
| carpenter `fetch_boards` -> `hunt` (pack ABSENT) | 991 | readback merge |
| carpenter `craft_carpentry` -> `hunt` (pack PRESENT) | 200 | craft-concordance fix |
| tinker `craft_tongs` <-> `bank_gold` (pack PRESENT) | see below | knob conversion |

**The fifth row was missing until a review re-ran the differential (2026-08-02), and the
sentence it displaced said "...and nothing else in 150,000 states."** Two independent
reviewers, each with their own generator and their own seed, found a class this table did
not have; that is the second time in this one entry that a measured behaviour change went
unrecorded, and the first time is the subject of the section immediately below. It is
recorded here in full.

It is neither a readback nor a gate change: it is the `BANK_TRIP_SURPLUS` **knob
conversion**. HEAD read the module constant (`gold > reserve + BANK_TRIP_SURPLUS`, 75);
the working tree reads `surplus = knob_int(memory, "bank_trip_surplus", BANK_TRIP_SURPLUS)`
(`anima2/tinker_life.py`), so a memory carrying that key at anything other than 75 moves
the urgent-bank band, in BOTH directions — tuned down or malformed (`0`, `-100`, `True`,
`"80"`, `12.5`, all of which clamp to 0) turns `craft_tongs` into `bank_gold`; tuned up
(`300`) turns `bank_gold` into `craft_tongs`. Minimal repro of the first direction: pack
present, tinker tool + 5 iron + 83 gold at (5,5), memory `{'bank_reserve': 82,
'bank_trip_surplus': 0, 'banker_spot': ((10,10),), 'craft_spot': (5,5)}` -> HEAD
`('economy', 'craft_tongs')`, now `('economy', 'bank_gold')`.

Its verdict, which is why it belongs in a different column from row four: it is a
PRIORITY-BAND change, **not** a rule-vs-gate violation. `ready_capability_ids('tinker',
ctx)` is `['bank_gold', 'craft_tongs']` on both sides of every flip — the rule reorders
two capabilities the gate admits either way, so nothing wants what the gate refuses.
Nor does it reach a shipped run untuned: at HEAD nothing wrote `bank_trip_surplus` into
any memory at all, and in the working tree `TinkerLife.__init__` always writes it, at
`DEFAULT_BANK_TRIP_SURPLUS` (75, the same constant) unless a caller tunes it. So the row
is reachable only through the tuning channel, which is exactly the surface it was added
for and exactly why it must be in the table rather than argued away.

The first three rows are the readback merge; the fourth is the craft-concordance fix
recorded in the next section, which is why "identical with a backpack present" is scoped
to the merge and is no longer true of the tree as a whole — and the fifth row is why that
scoping needs the further qualifier "the READBACK MERGE alone", which the sentence above
the table already carries: reverting only the `bank_trip_surplus` line leaves exactly the
three pack-absent classes, so the merge's own behaviour-identity claim survives this
correction intact. Absolute counts are corpus-dependent and are NOT comparable to the
×291/×41 above, nor between the runs below; what reproduces is WHICH classes change.
Two internal
consistency checks worth keeping: the woodsman shows **zero** changed states here, which
is the 2,009 newly-disagreeing woodsman states above being closed rather than forgotten
(they existed only in the intermediate tree, where `owns` was guarded and `on_ground` was
not); and every capability wanted at HEAD is still wanted somewhere now, so no fix in this
round created a dead branch.

*Third measurement, closing the correction (2026-08-02).* The fifth row was confirmed
with a THIRD harness — generator written from scratch while fixing this entry, sharing no
code with either reviewer's or with the run that produced the counts above, `git archive
8cdd2f0` unpacked as an `animahead` package and both provenances asserted on
`module.__file__` before the first diff. 200,000 states (40,000 per Life, seed 20260802),
placements and route/knob values as described in the corpus note plus `bank_trip_surplus`
swept: **exactly five changed classes, no sixth** — mage `fetch_gold`->`hunt` ×4944,
carpenter `fetch_boards`->`hunt` ×2770, tinker `fetch_iron`->`hunt` ×2636 (all pack
ABSENT), carpenter `craft_carpentry`->`hunt` ×389 and tinker `bank_gold`->`craft_tongs`
×1 (both pack PRESENT). The woodsman's zero holds. Three generators agreeing on the
membership of the set and disagreeing wildly on the counts is the intended reading of
"absolute counts are corpus-dependent": run four should expect the five classes and
should NOT expect these numbers.

That is precisely the rule-vs-gate disagreement class proposal 1 exists to kill, and the
suite could not see it because every fixture in it injects a backpack. Carried into the
merged definitions with their lessons
intact: the mage's `PICKUP_RADIUS` clause — already fixed by hand in implementation record
#1, but whose docstring still recorded that "the carpenter's identical fix was never
back-ported", the seventh drift this audit found latent — now exists once, so there is
nothing left to back-port; the worn-tool widening; and the `Graphics = int | Iterable[int]`
signature that ends the silent-`0` trap of handing a frozenset to a `==` comparison.
`pack_has`/`on_ground` stay separate from `pack_amount`/`ground_amount` because
`ItemView.amount` defaults to `0`, so a saw in the pack has amount 0 and `> 0` would be a
regression. Every removed docstring was merged into the function that
replaced it. `capabilities.WORKING_CAPITAL_RESERVE` was deleted in the same pass — zero
readers anywhere; its sizing lesson (`15*5 + 1*13 == 88`, and why registry order never
needed reordering) moved into `_bank_ready`, where `bank_reserve` is actually consumed.

**The honest half**: all of it is OFFLINE. 1422 tests green (1419 after the knob sweep,
plus the three this review round added: the two missing-backpack fixtures above and the
`econ_grace` floor), ruff clean, no live run has
exercised either change. And precondition (a) is only PARTIALLY satisfied — the channel is
real, the coverage is four thresholds. See the follow-ups below before treating it as done.

### The fourth violation, recorded late: carpenter `craft_carpentry` (2026-08-02)

The differential probe that produced the table above reported a FOURTH violating class and
the first draft of this entry wrote down only the three pack-absent ones. Leaving a
measured violation out of the record is exactly the failure this document exists to
prevent, so it is written here in full, after the fact, with the verdict that followed it.

**What was measured.** `('carpenter', 'craft_carpentry', has_backpack=True)`: the rule
wants `craft_carpentry` while `ready_capability_ids("carpenter", ctx)` refuses it, with our
own pack IN the observation — so it is not the missing-backpack class at all, and none of
the readback work touches it. 200 states in the reproducible corpus above; 308 in the
verifying session's own 30,000-state carpenter sweep (a different corpus — see the corpus
note; the class is what reproduces, not the count). It was the ONLY forward-concordance
violation left anywhere once the readback merge landed, and that is measured rather than
inferred: running the same corpus against a tree with the merge IN and this one guard
reverted gives **200 violations, all of them this row, and zero of anything else** across
five Lives and 150,000 states, with every Life's every branch wanted at least once.

**Cause.** `carpenter_life.decide_mode`'s terminal `return "economy", "craft_carpentry"`
was an unguarded fallthrough. The gate (`capabilities._make_craft_ready`, reached through
`_craft_ready_for(CarpenterCraft)`) applies three world-fact clauses and the rule mirrored
one. Unmirrored: **`_craft_at_spot`** — within `CarpenterCraft.craft_spot_radius` (3) of
`craft_spot` — and **`0 <= made < batch`**, where `CarpenterCraft.craft_batch` is 1, so a
single finished throne in the pack closes the gate. `tinker_life` has mirrored both since
it was written, and this module's own two sibling branches ("no tool and no way to get
one", "no material and no means") keep the invariant; only the terminal one broke it.
`craft_spot_within` was made public for precisely this reason — its docstring says so — and
the carpenter was the one Life that never called it.

**Reachability, stated as the two unequal halves it actually has.** This matters because
"the orchestrator always stages it" is the kind of dismissal that has to be re-checkable:

- The `craft_spot`-ABSENT form is a probe artifact, and specifically so. Both production
  sites that construct a `CarpenterLife` write `craft_spot` into the ECONOMY agent's memory
  before any thread starts — `village.py::run_supply_pair` writes it three lines after
  construction and ~20 lines before `t.start()`; `run_carpenter_life` ships it in
  `Staged.econ_memory` and `LifeRunner.run` applies it at `life.econ_agent.memory.update(
  staged.econ_memory)` before starting the worker — and the value is always `gm.stage`'s
  settled `(pos.x, pos.y)` ints, never a float and never `None`. A staging failure raises
  `StagingError` out of `run()` before `build_life` is called at all. Re-check that by
  re-reading those two sites; do not take this sentence for it.
- The BATCH clause is live-reachable on a shipped runner **today**. `run_supply_pair`
  stages with `strict=False`, and `stage_shops` on a failure prints and continues with the
  route key unset — a documented, live-observed outcome (a Banker resolving to the
  Weaponsmith's serial). An unrouted Sten still has his staged saw and still gets boards
  from Bjorn, so he crafts exactly one throne; then `sell_furniture` is skipped for want of
  a vendor, fetch/buy are skipped with boards already in the pack, banking is unreachable
  with no income, and `craft_carpentry` is wanted against `ready=[]` forever.
- The RADIUS clause is one loose leash away from live. `FetchBoards` walks up to
  `PICKUP_RADIUS` (6) — twice this craft radius — to a delivered pile and has no walk-home
  leg, and with no goal admitted `CapabilityWait.can_run` is True and sits ABOVE `Wander`
  in the capability planner, so the agent HOLDS position rather than drifting home. No
  shipped runner currently produces both ground boards and a loose leash
  (`run_carpenter_life` has the loose leash but buys its boards; `run_supply_pair` has the
  pile but leashes Sten to `min(max(1, shop_reach), PICKUP_RADIUS - 1)`), so today this
  half survives on three accidents rather than on the rule being right.

**What it costs when it fires** is not a missed craft, it is a permanent one plus a repair
loop. Nothing else in a craft chain fires with material already in the pack, so the want
never changes; and `_detect_disagreement` re-fires on EVERY tick the streak is at or above
`disagreement_ticks` (default 10) — there is no rate limit — calling `_clear_stale_ui`
each time. That repair is a harmless no-op only while no UI surface is open; with a vendor
BUY window present it emits one `BuyItems(vendor, items=[])` cancel per tick, and the
verifying session's production-shaped probe counted 16 of them in a 30-tick run on an
otherwise healthy agent (first flagged at tick 15, which is what a default window of 10
gives). The mechanism is readable in `warrior_life.py:443-487`; the count is that probe's.

**Fixed**, one branch in one file: `carpenter_life.decide_mode`'s terminal branch now
guards on `pack_amount(obs, _FURNITURE_GRAPHIC) < CRAFT_BATCH and craft_spot_within(obs,
memory, _CRAFT_RADIUS)` and falls through to `("hunt", None)`.
`tests/test_life_gate_concordance.py` gained the axis that would have caught it — a
`craft_spot` axis (at / radius edge / one past / absent / malformed) on BOTH craft
lattices, plus a routed/unrouted `vendor_spot` axis for the carpenter — because the blind
spot was that both craft lattices PINNED `craft_spot` to the player's own tile, so neither
craft Life was ever walked off its stand. `tests/test_carpenter_life.py`'s `ROUTES` fixture
gained `"craft_spot": (5, 5)`: that is adding the wiring both production paths actually
write to a fixture that was testing a configuration which never runs, and no assertion
changed. Post-fix, the same 150,000-state sweep reports **zero** forward-concordance
violations across all five Lives, and `craft_carpentry` is still wanted (72 states in that
corpus) — the guard did not create a dead branch, which is the other half of the check.

**Offline only when written.** The 2026-08-03 post-fix carpenter (§6.1) then ran
`craft_carpentry` live on a brain containing this guard, with no rule-vs-gate
disagreement — so the guard has live exposure, though no run has reached the
unrouted state it exists for. Gates after everything in
this entry (the readback merge, the knob channel, its two runner entry points, the craft
guard and its new axes, and the review round below): **1436 tests green** in ~6s, `uvx
ruff@0.8.0 check .` and `uvx ruff check .` both clean — up from the 1422 quoted in "the
honest half" above and the 1431 quoted here before the review round, both of which are
their paragraph's state at the time it was written, not the tree's state now. Every
count in this document is an as-of, and this is the sentence that says so once.

**Review round (2026-08-02), what it changed.** Three blocking findings, all three
reproduced before being fixed rather than taken on trust: the knob channel accepted
`profession`/`steering` and re-professioned a Life (fixed with a `KNOBS` allowlist
checked in `LifeSpec.__post_init__` — before the login, not after the staging); this
entry's HEAD→now table was missing a fifth measured class (added above, with a third
independent differential); and `docs/HISTORY.md`'s new preamble claimed the phase
narrative was untouched while the same change set had deleted the Phase 7 forward
pointer (the clause is restored, struck through and annotated — it is the exhibit for
this document's own top risk 4). Five minor findings fixed alongside them, each recorded
where it belongs: follow-ups 1, 3b, 6 and 9 above, the timing figure in the knob-blindness
bullet, and the `wander_leash` qualification now carried in CLAUDE.md and DESIGN.md.
One suite-hygiene fix outside this change set: `tests/test_ipc_body.py`'s two
hanging-bridge fixtures orphaned a `sleep 60` grandchild and added an intermittent
60-second stall to roughly every other full-suite run (measured, `--durations`-attributed,
orphan confirmed at PPID 1); `exec sleep 60` removes it, and the reason it is a fixture
fix rather than a change to `IpcBody.spawn`'s process handling is written above the test.

### Follow-ups, scoped and deliberately not done

Deferrals belong in writing, not in a chat report. Ranked by how much a live run would care:

1. **The missing-backpack state is covered by two fixtures, not by the lattices.** Both
   halves are now asserted in `tests/test_life_gate_concordance.py` — `owns()` is `False`
   for a ground tool with no backpack, and no Life's rule wants anything the gate refuses
   in that state. *Mutation-tested, and the first version of this sentence ("each fails if
   either clause is removed") was too strong:* deleting the `bp is not None` clause from
   `obsview.owns` fails the OWNED test only (1 failed, 6 passed) — with the ground guard
   still in place `owns()==True` short-circuits the tool branches, so no rule reaches a
   refused fetch. Deleting the pack guard from `on_ground`/`ground_amount` fails BOTH
   (2 failed, 5 passed). So: the owned half is guarded by one clause, the ground half by
   either. That asymmetry is the point of implementation record (ii) — fixing `owns`
   alone renamed the disagreement rather than closing it — restated as a test property.
   Also corrected in the same pass: that second fixture's memory named eight routes that
   do not exist (`reagent_vendor_spot`, `saw_vendor_spot`, ... against the real
   `mage_vendor_spot` / `tool_vendor_spot` / shared `vendor_spot`), so its "every route
   wired, nothing declined for the WRONG reason" comment was false for every buy/sell
   branch but the warrior's; the keys are read off the skill classes now. What remains:
   the five per-profession LATTICES still inject a backpack at every point, so this is two
   hand-built observations rather than an axis, and no live run has been in the state
   since the fix.
2. **CLOSED 2026-08-05 — all seven construction sites have an entry point. See §10.**
   *(History kept, because the item was rewritten twice as it shrank rather than deleted:
   it began as "No village runner exposes a knob"; the two `LifeSpec` runners were wired
   on 2026-08-02; the remaining five — including the flagship `run_forge_pair` this entry
   singles out below — on 2026-08-05, through `life_runner.build_tuned_life` and a shared
   `validate_knobs`. What the entry says about the HAZARD of threading a dict per runner
   is why that shared seam exists rather than five copies. The text below is the
   2026-08-02 state.)*
   `village.run_carpenter_life(knobs={...})` and `run_woodsman_life(knobs={...})` now take
   a caller-side dict and pass it as `LifeSpec(knobs=dict(knobs or {}))` — copied, not
   aliased, so a sweep script reusing one dict cannot retune a Life that is already
   running. The path a test walks is the path the runner walks:
   `run_X_life(knobs=)` → `LifeSpec.knobs` → `LifeRunner.build_life` → the Life's own
   memory and attributes, each clamped through `anima2/knobs.py`.
   What is still wireless: the other four runners (`run_supply_pair`, two Lives;
   `run_forge_pair`; `run_warrior_village`; `run_artisan_mage_village`) build their Lives
   INLINE with no factory and no `LifeSpec`, so there is nothing to forward through —
   five of the seven construction sites, and giving them the seam means either threading a
   dict through hand-written construction per runner (no single point that stays true) or
   migrating them onto `LifeSpec`. `run_forge_pair` is the closest candidate — one
   `TinkerLife(...)` call — and it is also the one that matters most: the flagship
   miner→tinker chain, the positive-margin loop a fitness run would actually measure.
   Note also that `LifeRunner.run()` itself is inspection-covered, not test-covered (it
   needs a live shard); it reaches the Life through the same `build_life` call the tests
   call, which is why that named seam exists rather than `run()` inlining the factory.
3. **No genome touches a Life.** `foundry/archive.py::Genome`'s four axes are
   `profession`, `sociability`, `deliver_threshold`, `cognition_tier`; nothing maps any of
   them onto a `LifeSpec.knobs` entry. Until something does, the channel is unused in the
   direction §E's criterion is about. *(One of those four names is now REFUSED by the
   channel rather than merely unmapped: `knobs={"profession": ...}` splatted straight
   into `WarriorLife.__init__` and re-professioned the Life while the spec, the staging,
   the worker label and `telemetry_line`'s `ready=` list stayed on the old profession —
   a permanent want-vs-refuse standoff the operator's own status line contradicted.
   `LifeSpec.__post_init__` now checks every key against the Life's `KNOBS` allowlist,
   so whoever wires the genome up has to map that axis to something real instead of
   getting a silent split. Review-caught, offline.)*
3b. **Dead capacity: 5 of the tinker's 11 registry capabilities are unreachable through
   its Life.** `TinkerLife.decide_mode` can return 6 ids; the registry binds 11, so
   `craft_saw`, `craft_hatchet`, `deliver_saw`, `deliver_hatchet` and `deliver_gold` go
   READY in randomized states and can never be executed — `WarriorLife.tick` fills
   `self.candidates` from `decide`/`decide_all` and `_LifeClient._pick` may only return a
   member of that list, so the CapabilityCognition on a TinkerLife cannot propose them.
   That is the Brick-10 tinker→carpenter/lumberjack tool link and the tinker→mage gold
   hand-off, unreachable THROUGH A LIFE. Inert today, not harmful: no shipped runner
   wires a TinkerLife's drop keys (`run_forge_pair`, the only TinkerLife runner, stages
   `vendor_spot` and `banker_spot`; the one production `mage_drop` belongs to
   `run_artisan_mage_village`'s artisan, a plain Agent under `CapabilityCognition(None)`).
   NOT closed by adding branches: that is a live-behaviour change to the flagship
   positive-margin Life and nothing offline can predict a real forge day. What was done
   instead is make it impossible to lose again — the concordance suite's coverage check
   is REGISTRY-DERIVED now and asserts EQUALITY against a named
   `_NOT_DRIVEN_BY_THE_LIFE` allow-list, where the old hand-listed 6-id `expected_caps`
   could not see the gap at all. Review-caught; it had been latent since the tinker
   shipped.
4. **CLOSED 2026-08-05 — see §11.** *(Text kept: "`wander_leash` is the cheapest remaining
   axis (§E's 'exploration radius') and needs no Life work — but it arrives through
   `Staged.leash`, a DIFFERENT channel, and `skills/movement.py::Wander._homeward` clamps
   it its own way (a malformed value falls back to the class default rather than to a
   floor). Unifying the two is small; noting the divergence matters more.")* Two things it
   did not anticipate: the unification had to go toward the FLOOR rather than the fallback
   (the fallback is discontinuous, which disqualifies it for a searched axis), and "needs
   no Life work" was wrong — it is the only knob something else already writes, so making
   it an axis meant deciding whether a tuned value or a runner's derived `set_leash` wins.
5. **~20 SHARED cost/threshold constants cannot become knobs mechanically.** Each gate's
   threshold is captured into a closure at import time (`_make_sell_ready(...)`) inside a
   `frozen=True` `CapabilityBinding` in a `MappingProxyType` registry that declares itself
   an authority boundary. There is no per-instance path; creating one means a shipped gate
   reads agent memory — a registry-contract decision, not a conversion.
6. **CLOSED 2026-08-07 — all three merged into the skills layer. See §12.** *(Text kept.
   One thing it got wrong, and in the reassuring direction: it ranked the bandage pair last
   and described it only as "`0x0E21` restated as a literal". That pair was read by OPPOSITE
   SIDES of one decision — the rule counted the single art, the gate counts the family — so
   it was a want-vs-refuse standoff one added graphic away, and the concordance suite could
   not see it because a singleton family agrees at every lattice point.)*
   **Three constants are still defined twice** and must be merged before either side can be
   tuned: `warrior_life.UPGRADE_RESERVE` ↔ `capabilities._UPGRADE_RESERVE` (both 243 today,
   and — review-corrected — the **same source, two names**, one of them via
   `warrior_life.ARMOR_PRICE`: all three are `BuyArmor.tool_price_estimate` off the one
   class object, verified identical at runtime, so unlike the third pair below these two
   cannot diverge numerically; the merge is still owed, but the risk is a tuning write

   `capabilities._FETCH_BOARDS_THRESHOLD` (both 19, same source, two names),
   `skills/warrior.BANDAGE_GRAPHIC` ↔ `skills/survival.BANDAGE_GRAPHICS` (`0x0E21` restated

7. **Retreat thresholds and rest timing (§E's own examples) cannot be knobs today.** The
   capability manifest validator requires `vars(prefix[0]) == {}` for the `Survive` slot
   (and `vars(wander) == {}` for `Wander`) — any per-instance survival tuning fails
   verification. Changing that is an authority-boundary decision, and the boundary is there
   on purpose.
8. **Gate-side readbacks still duplicate `obsview`.** `capabilities.py` and `skills/*.py`
   take a `SkillContext`, not an `Observation`, and sit BELOW `obsview` (which imports
   `BACKPACK_LAYER`/`GOLD_GRAPHIC`/`BANKBOX_LAYER`/`PICKUP_RADIUS` *from* `skills`), so
   deduping them first requires moving those four UO constants below `skills`. `obsview.owns`
   mirrors `capabilities._owned_tool` clause-for-clause to make that day cheap.
9. **Readback copies that remain — the counted inventory.** *(Was: "11 `live_*.py` gate
   scripts and three `village.py` closures", which undercounted `village.py` and did not
   mention `curriculum.py` at all. Recounted 2026-08-02 after this round's dedup;
   the criterion is mechanical and re-runnable — a file that reads `Observation.items`
   directly, i.e. `grep -E '\.items($|[^(])'` minus `dict.items()`,
   `live_fitness_gate.py`'s `summary.items_into_pack`, and — review-added, because
   re-running the grep on `village.py` did not reproduce its sub-count without it — the
   `prof.items` / `PROFESSIONS[...].items` PROFESSION-SPEC lists, which are staging
   equipment manifests and not observations at all. The `live_*.py` numbers reproduce
   exactly (17 files match, minus `live_fitness_gate.py` = the 16 named, out of 32), and
   so does `curriculum.py`'s "DONE" (zero matches now).)*

   - **`village.py`: SIX Observation readbacks, not three** — five by the grep above
     plus one that the grep cannot see, the `near` chebyshev (~v:2451), which reads

     excluded ON PURPOSE rather than pending. Three are UNBOUNDED ground scans that
     `obsview.ground_amount` cannot serve, because `ground_amount` carries the fetch

     `_pipeline_progress._ground_gold` (~v:2424). Two more are POSITIONAL, and `obsview`

     replacement. (`run_warrior_village`'s monitor also counts nearby hostiles off

     Its four readbacks (`_backpack`/`_bankbox`/`_pack_amount`/`_bankbox_amount`, feeding
     the pack-threshold and bank-threshold milestone builders) fell through every category

     `live_craft_goal`, `live_curriculum_goal`, `live_hunt`, `live_market`, `live_mine`,
