10. **FIXED OFFLINE the same day (2026-08-03) — see §5 of the section below; the text
    that follows is the deferral as it stood, kept because the reasoning is what picked
    the repair.** What shipped is a FOURTH option none of the three below: an EXIT-EDGE
    HOLD in `WarriorLife.tick` — the mode is held at `economy` while a frame is live, so
    the frame's own agent keeps being ticked and reaches its own retirement paths, bounded
    by a third rule (an overdue frame RELEASES the hold) because the first two bounds turn
    out not to be general. It is (i)'s intent without (i)'s cost: exactly one inner agent
    is still ticked per orchestrator tick, so no route or reflex cadence changes; it needs
    no new `expire_due` caller, so (ii)'s "expire frames the agent never got to advance"
    does not arise; and it removes the lying report (iii) would have papered over rather
    than removing the symptom that makes the mechanism findable. **No live run has
    exercised it.**
    *The original deferral, verbatim:* deferred rather than fixed because
    every candidate repair is a change to the orchestrator's tick shape or to the goal
    lifecycle, both load-bearing, and one live sighting is not enough to choose between
    them. The three that were considered, with what each costs: (i) tick the econ agent
    once more on the tick the mode leaves `economy` while a frame is live — smallest, but
    it makes "which agent ticks" conditional on goal state, and the one-agent-per-tick
    rule is what keeps the two inner agents' route/reflex cadences honest (the comment at
    `warrior_life.tick` records that an extra pump already broke that once); (ii) count
    `GoalFrame.deadline_tick` in ORCHESTRATOR ticks rather than the owning agent's, so an
    unticked frame still expires — but `expire_due` is only ever called from inside
    `Agent.tick`, so this needs a caller that does not exist and would expire frames the
    agent never got a chance to advance; (iii) leave the frame and fix only the REPORT,
    e.g. `telemetry_line` marking an `admitted` goal whose owner has not ticked since the
    last sample. (iii) is the cheapest and addresses the cost that was actually paid here
    (a status line that lied for 272 ticks) rather than the mechanism, which is the reason
    it is written down rather than done: it would also remove the symptom that makes the
    mechanism findable. Nothing here is urgent — the same section shows the frame retires
    on its own the moment the mode returns to `economy` — but it is not free either.
11. **`terrain` is offered by the body and read by nobody.** Bridge schema v17
    (anima-client `867556f`) added `Observation.terrain`: a square window of walkability,
    standing Z, and the serial of a closed door that would have to be opened first,
    surveyed through the same `path::Terrain` the driver's own auto-walk route uses.
    `anima2/contract.py` has no `terrain` field (`grep -n terrain anima2/contract.py` →
    nothing), so the brain still cannot tell a wall from open ground from an observation
    alone. This project has already paid for that blindness live — forge12 burned a
    six-spot relocation pool, half of it on the mountain's WRONG FLANK (the "Terrain +
    lifecycle addendum" above) — and answered it by teaching the BRAIN to read the UOP
    land map itself (`anima2/uomap.py`). Both answers can coexist and they answer
    different questions: `uomap.py` surveys the whole map offline to pick where to GO,
    `terrain` says what is walkable HERE, right now, including doors and dynamic blockers
    a map file cannot know about. The reason this is a follow-up and not a task is cost:
    the commit message calls `terrain` "the largest field in an observation and it is
    re-sent every poll", and it is `None` unless the bridge is started with a
    `terrain_radius`, so wiring it means deciding a radius and paying for it on every tick
    of every agent — a live-budget question, not a parsing one.
12. **PARTLY DISCHARGED 2026-08-03 — see §6 below; the deferral text is kept because it is
    what the runs were aimed at.** Three live runs happened the same day. What is now
    live-proven: the defect itself is gone (30 of 32 lying status lines → 0 of 33, same
    command), the hold was directly observed 31 times with its frame's clock advancing and
    the frame retiring inside budget, bound 2 (the deadline) closed two unachieved frames,
    and the disagreement detector and its repair still fire under the hold. What is NOT:
    **no frame ever went overdue**, so the third bound — the overdue release plus
    `_repair_overdue_frame`, the bound added because the FIRST version of this fix livelocked
    the wedged world (§5's first refutation: "four of the five Lives were pinned in economy
    mode with `hunt_after = 0` for the whole 3000-tick window"; the "24 of 24 worlds" that
    stood in this sentence, and in `57422e4`'s pushed commit message, is unsourced — see the
    CORRECTION note in §5) — has zero live ticks, and no death occurred mid-transaction.
    `!frozen` never appeared on 306 samples, but §6.3 records that this is ENTAILED by those
    two absences rather than independent of them. Bound 1 is unexercised as far as the logs
    can tell. §6.3 says what a run must do to reach the rest. *The original deferral,
    verbatim:*
    **The exit-edge hold (§5 below) has NO live exposure, and it changes the hot path.**
    Named as a follow-up rather than buried in the fix's own record, because it is the one
    thing that outranks everything else on this list the moment a shard exists again: which
    inner agent gets the tick is the innermost decision all five Lives make, and the only
    evidence for the change is `MockBody`. §5's "what the next live run should look for"
    is the checklist. Until then, treat every claim in §5 as offline.
13. **Death evidence is recorded per-agent, and the hold makes economy stretches longer.**
    `Agent.tick` freezes the alive→dead attribution evidence on ITS OWN memory
    (`agent.py:594-615`: `death_rolling_alive_pos` → `death_last_alive_pos`), and
    `RecoverDeath` reads it back from whichever agent is ticking. The two Lives' agents
    keep separate memories BY DESIGN, so a death that lands during an economy stretch
    leaves the hunt agent's rolling position stale — it is the last place the HUNT agent
    saw, not where the character died. This is pre-existing and was already reachable (the
    carpenter has no work skill and runs its economy agent nearly every tick), but the hold
    lengthens the window, so it is written down now rather than after a corpse is walked to
    the wrong tile. Not fixed here: sharing the evidence across the two memories is the
    shared-dict mistake `warrior_life`'s own docstring records being live-caught, so the
    repair is a deliberate hand-off, not a merge.
14. **`_run_worker`'s no-progress pulse reads the HUNT agent's reward** (`_pulse` calls
    `agent.episodes.total_reward()`, and `WarriorLife.episodes` is `hunt_agent.episodes`),
    which is frozen for the whole of any economy stretch — held or not. During a hold the
    pulse therefore leans entirely on `steps`/`says`/position. Arguably an improvement
    (the pre-fix freeze was masked by hunt-mode wandering keeping `steps` moving), but a
    long legitimate held transaction that also stops walking will now trip `NO PROGRESS` —
    correctly, since it IS stuck, which is why this is a note and not a defect.
    *CONFIRMED LIVE 2026-08-03 (§6.2): the 275-tick hold printed `** Pim: NO PROGRESS for
    120 … 360 ticks **` while `+hold` and a moving `@age` sat on the same screen saying why.
    (An earlier draft of this note said `40 … 360`; the 40- and 80-tick pulses in that log
    PRECEDE the hold window — the tinker had already been standing still for two pulses when
    the first `+hold` sample printed. Inside the window the pulses are 120, 160, 200, 240,
    280, 320, 360.) The prediction held and so did the verdict — a note, not a defect.*
15. **The ladderless capability families ride their whole budget to close.** Only
    `SellItemCapability` and `BankGoldCapability` write `cap_run_finished_goal_id`, so
    `CapabilityGoalComplete` can close an unachieved craft / buy / tool-buy / fetch /
    process / deliver frame by neither of its two branches. Live on 2026-08-03 (§6.4) a
    `craft_tongs` frame that reached `cap_craft_stage=finished` with 4 tongs of a batch of 5
    — terminal, unachieved, iron exhausted — sat admitted for 266 more econ ticks and left
    only on its 300-tick deadline; three `buy_iron` frames did the same against 180. Under
    the hold that is bounded and self-clearing, so it is not a defect; it is a latency,
    and it is the difference between a Life retrying in 3 ticks and retrying in 300. The
    repair is the one the forge1/forge13 lifecycle fix already established — a neutral
    run-finished marker on the give-up path of the other families — and it is NOT free:
    each family's "the trip is over" condition has to be defined as carefully as the sell
    wrapper's was, and defining it wrong closes frames that are still in flight. Deferred,
    not dismissed.
    ***CRAFT CLOSED 2026-08-18 — §47.** The same shape recurred on
    `forge-20260818-0003` worse than §6.4: iron already on the ground
    (`pim_sees=12` then 22), `want=fetch_iron`, and two `craft_tongs` frames
    still rode 300/300. Craft now writes the marker at `finished`. Buy was
    follow-up 19. Fetch / process / deliver still have no marker — bound 2's
    remaining production path, pinned by
    `test_a_deadline_retirement_is_reported_as_bound_2_not_bound_1`.
16. **A vendor BUY window left behind by a finished `buy_iron` trip did not clear, live —
    forge16's shape, recurring, at one full goal lifetime per recurrence.** §6.7 has the
    evidence: 75 consecutive samples with `ui=shopbuy`, three full `buy_iron` budgets
    burned, the Life-layer repair firing between them and the surface back on the next
    sample. It is NOT a refutation of `8cdd2f0`, but the reason is narrower than an earlier
    draft of this entry claimed. That draft said `8cdd2f0`'s case — a window already open
    when a trip STARTS — "never arose"; it did arise. The FIRST wedged trip opened the
    window itself, but wedged trips 2 and 3 both began with `ui=shopbuy` already present on
    the preceding sample. What they never did is **reach the popup stage where `8cdd2f0`'s
    empty-list cancel lives**: every one of their samples reads `mkt_phase=craft` with
    `buy_stage` absent. So the correct statement is that the run gave the FSM-layer fix **no
    OBSERVED OPPORTUNITY to fire**, not that its case did not occur — and "why a re-entering
    buy trip never reaches its own popup stage" is now the sharper question. Two
    falsifiable candidates are listed in §6.7; both are offline-reproducible against
    `MockBody`, which already models `shop_buy` and the empty-list cancel. This outranks
    follow-up 15: it cost the 1800-tick run its last 556 ticks, and it is the same wedge
    family (forge15/16/18) this project has now paid for four times.
    ***FIXED OFFLINE the same day (2026-08-03) — §8.2, and the sharper question above is
    answered.*** Offline reproduction found THREE defects composing into the observed shape,
    not one: `buy_offer_reopens` outlived its trip (so one unlucky trip made every later trip
    give up on its FIRST `window` tick — that is candidate (b), sharpened from a guess into a
    measured mechanism); the "re-roll" emitted no action, and a vendor window is a snapshot,
    so it re-read the identical entry list; and no give-up path closed the window the trip had
    opened. All three are fixed, mirrored to the tool-buy FSM, with the exit-edge close's
    safety argument in its own docstring. **Candidate (a) — the cancel is emitted but the
    surface does not close, server-side or through a stale `shop_buy` — is untouched and
    remains open.** LIVE-UNVERIFIED: no shard was available (§8.5), so the composition into
    the observed log is CONSISTENCY, not proof. The follow-up stays open until a forge pair
    runs.
17. **The forge miner stopped producing at t=765 of an 1800-tick run and NOTHING flagged
    it** — the liveness gap this document already named on 2026-07-30 ("a liveness line for
    NON-Life agents (the forge miner died silently — `out+0.0 steps=0` for 72 samples with
    nothing flagging it)"), recurring with the same signature and never fixed. §6.5 has the
    evidence: Grimm's cumulative reward froze at `out+176.9` and his `steps` at 139 on the
    t=765 sample and neither moved on the 126 samples that followed; `ph=mine` and `iron=0`
    on every one of them, with no `smelt` and no `deliver` phase ever again; and his
    relocation window turned all-stuck (`win=n/n`) from that point on, where before it had
    read `win=0/24`. He kept relocating — position moves, and `steps` counts only `Walk`, not
    the `WalkTo` a relocation uses — so from outside he looks like a working miner. **What
    the log cannot say is WHETHER HE DIED**, and that is the finding: `life_runner.hp_readout`
    exists and prints `DEAD`, but `run_forge_pair`'s status line calls it for neither agent,
    so a NON-Life agent's death, a lost pickaxe and a dead vein are indistinguishable from
    the tape. The cheap fix is the one already proposed in 2026-07-30 and still not done: put
    `hp_readout` (or any liveness field) on the non-Life half of every pair status line.
    Ranked here rather than in §6 because it silently halved the flagship chain's supply side
    and nobody watching the run could have known.
    ***PARTLY DISCHARGED 2026-08-03 — §8.1.*** A work-liveness alarm now fires on any agent
    whose ledgers stop moving while it is running a non-idle skill: `NO OUTPUT for 240 ticks`,
    escalating, with `eps=` on every status line, `!stalled` while it holds, and
    `· STALLED n` in the terminal suffix. The 240 is measured off these two logs (worst
    HEALTHY reward-silence 159 ticks; zero false positives on both healthy windows) and it is
    armed by the skill the agent is actually running, because without that gate it is 100%
    wrong on a healthy `townsfolk`. **The other half — WHETHER HE DIED — is NOT done.** The
    pair line still prints no hp for either agent, so a death, a lost pickaxe and a dead vein
    are still indistinguishable. That half is follow-up 18. LIVE-UNVERIFIED either way: the
    alarm has never run on a shard (§8.5).

## The knob channel's first live run — and the frame nobody was ticking (2026-08-03)

**ONE run of 300 ticks**, not an endurance test and not a comparison:
`python -m anima2.village --carpenter --knob bank_reserve=400 --ticks 300 --monitor`
against a local ServUO shard at `127.0.0.1:2594`. It is the first live exposure of
anything in the 2026-08-02 entry above, all of which closed with "ALL of this is offline".
Thirty-two telemetry samples came back. Three things are worth the record: one success,
one standing hazard, and one finding this document had already named as a blind spot and
had never seen.

### 1. The tuning channel carried a value on a shard

The staging banner, log line 18:

```
staged: Sten@(2609, 474) and 129g seed  (reserve 400)
```

`400` is the value passed on the command line. It is not a copy of the argument being
echoed back: `life_runner.py::staged_line` reads it with
`reserve = _bank_reserve(life.econ_agent.memory)` — the SAME clamped read
(`skills/market.py::_bank_reserve`, bound to `knobs.knob_int`) that the rule and the
`bank_gold` gate use — off the economy agent's own memory dict, after the Life was built.
The module default it displaces is `carpenter_life.BANK_RESERVE` = `BOARD_BATCH_COST +
SAW_COST` = 129, which is also the seed printed one clause earlier in the same line, so a
banner reading `129g seed (reserve 129)` would have been indistinguishable from a channel
that silently dropped the knob. It read 400.

So the whole path is live: command line → `village._parse_knobs` → `run_carpenter_life(
knobs=)` → `LifeSpec.knobs` → `LifeRunner.build_life` → `CarpenterLife.__init__` → the
economy agent's memory → every reader through `knobs.py`'s clamp. That is exactly the
thing CLAUDE.md's precondition (a) was missing, and it retires "offline-proven only" for
the knob channel.

**It retires nothing else, and the same run says why.** The tuned value was behaviourally
INERT: the carpenter finished on 93 gold, and the branch `bank_reserve` gates
(`pack_amount(obs, GOLD_GRAPHIC) > _bank_reserve(...)`) is false at 93 against 129 and
false at 93 against 400, so no decision in the run could have differed. A channel that
carries a value is proven; a value that STEERS is not. Follow-ups 2 and 3 above are
untouched by this run — `Genome`'s four axes still map onto no `LifeSpec.knobs` entry, and
the four inline runners (five of the seven construction sites, including the flagship
positive-margin forge pair) still have no seam to forward a dict through. Do not read this
section as clearing the Phase 7 item 2 rerun.

### 2. The bridge was a version behind — a standing hazard, now named

The first attempt never reached the shard. It died at the handshake:

```
unsupported bridge schema 17; expected 16
```

`ipc_body.py` declared `SUPPORTED_SCHEMA_VERSION = 16` while the sibling repo had moved to
17 in `anima-client` commit `867556f` ("feat: close five Tier 1 gaps, and give brains
terrain perception"). CLAUDE.md's own Dev section says to keep the two in lockstep; the
lockstep is one integer, and nothing outside a live attempt checks it. That is the hazard
worth naming: **body work lands in this repo as a version bump plus a serializer diff, and
the only thing that reports the omission is a run that has already paid for a login.**

**How the bump was verified — by diffing the serializer, not by trusting the changelog.**
`git show 867556f -- crates/anima-contract-json/src/lib.rs`, deleted lines only, is seven
lines: a doc-comment continuation, `pub const SCHEMA_VERSION: u32 = 16;`, a
`WaypointView,` import, three test lines (`fn schema_v16_retains_waypoint_exact_shape`,
`assert_eq!(SCHEMA_VERSION, 16)`, and a `Action::Say { text: "hi".into() }` fixture) — and
one line that touches something this brain emits: the `"Say"` parse arm, which gained an
OPTIONAL `mode` whose absence still means plain speech. **No JSON key was removed or
renamed**, so every existing emission and every existing readback stays valid, which is
why the bump is a number and a comment rather than a parser change. The reasoning is
recorded above the constant in `ipc_body.py` so the next bump is diffed the same way.

**What v17 offers that anima2 does not read: `terrain`.** An optional square window of
walkability, standing Z, and the serial of a closed door — surveyed by the driver through
the same `path::Terrain` its own auto-walk route uses, so brain and driver cannot disagree
about what is walkable. `anima2/contract.py` has no such field. "The brain cannot tell a
wall from open ground" is not a hypothetical cost here: it is a root cause this project
already paid for live, in forge12's six-spot relocation pool burned half on the mountain's
wrong flank (the "Terrain + lifecycle addendum" above). That is now follow-up 11, with a
name and a reason it is not free.

### 3. The finding: a `sell_furniture` frame nobody was ticking

**The observable.** Thirty consecutive telemetry samples, from `t=28` to the
`[BUDGET SPENT]` line at `t=300` — 272 of the run's 300 ticks — read:

```
— carpenter [hunt] want=None admitted=sell_furniture ready=[] saw=yes boards=1 furniture=0
  hp=80/80 gold=93 banked=0 net=-36g (-10792g/h) —
```

`want=None` with an admitted goal lingering, `ready=[]`, and `grep -c DISAGREEMENT` over
the whole log = **0**. The furniture count is 0 in every one of those samples: the throne
that goal was selling had been sold at `t=28` (gold 69 → 93).

**CONFIRMED: it is a live frame, not a stale string.** `life_runner.telemetry_line` reads
`admitted` off `life.econ_agent.goal_stack.current` and falls back to `"?"` on any
exception, so a `GoalFrame` whose `goal.params["capability"] == "sell_furniture"` was on
the economy agent's stack for all 272 ticks.

**Cause — and it is NOT "a finished goal that never came off".** Exactly two things retire
a frame, and both live INSIDE `Agent.tick()`: `CapabilityGoalComplete`
(`profession.py:283-328`, `consumes_goal = True`), which the planner must select on a
LATER tick before `agent.py:902-912` calls `goal_stack.finish(...)`; and
`goal_stack.expire_due(...)`, called only at `agent.py:545` and `agent.py:648`.
`SellItemCapability.step` never returns a terminal status — every exit is
`Status.RUNNING` — so retirement always needs at least one more econ-agent tick after the
FSM's markers land. Meanwhile `WarriorLife.tick` ticks exactly ONE inner agent per
orchestrator tick (`warrior_life.py:406`), and `carpenter_life.decide_mode`'s
`sell_furniture` branch is keyed on `pack_amount(obs, _FURNITURE_GRAPHIC) >=
SELL_FURNITURE_AT`. **The instant the vendor took the throne that clause went false**, and
with boards=1 (< 19), none on the ground and 93 gold < `BOARD_BATCH_COST` 114 the rule fell
through to `return "hunt", None` — "no material and no means — wait rather than stall",
which is correct. Mode left `economy` on the very tick the sale landed, the econ agent was
never ticked again, and its stack, its planner and its deadline check all froze with it.
The frame is stranded **mid-transaction**, not finished: offline the frozen memory is
`mkt_phase='sell'`, `mkt_tick=5`, `sell_paid=24.0`, with **neither**
`cap_sell_finished_goal_id` nor `cap_run_finished_goal_id` ever set. The economic goal was
achieved; the frame's own terminal markers were not. The deadline is irrelevant for the
same reason — `deadline_tick=180` counts ECON-AGENT ticks, and that counter stopped at 5.

**Reproduced offline, no shard needed.** A real `CarpenterLife` over the real
`anima2.mock_body.MockBody`, production `tick`/`decide_mode`/`Agent`/`CapabilityPolicy`/
`SellItemCapability`/`telemetry_line` throughout:

```
t=10   [economy] want=sell_furniture admitted=sell_furniture ready=[] boards=1 furniture=0
       gold=93 | frame=id=1 sell_furniture state=active deadline=180 | econ_ticks=4
t=400  [hunt   ] want=None admitted=sell_furniture ready=[] boards=1 furniture=0
       gold=93 | frame=id=1 sell_furniture state=active deadline=180 | econ_ticks=5
  frame still on the stack : True      econ agent ticks : 5      orchestrator ticks : 400
  goal history (retired)   : []        rule_gate_disagreement : None
```

Line for line the live shape, with the live numbers. What the shard supplies that MockBody
does not is the vendor's side: MockBody models `gumps` and `shop_buy` but has no
`shop_sell` and no vendor NPC, so the sale was applied by editing the mock world at the
moment the FSM engaged (remove the throne, gold 69 → 93). Nothing else is stubbed, and the
stranding does not depend on the FSM's internal phase — only on `decide_mode` flipping out
of economy on the tick the throne leaves the pack, which those two edits reproduce
faithfully. This is the method this document already prescribes: **forced-state gates find
FSM bugs, offline repros find LIFECYCLE bugs.**

**REFUTED, as stated: "the frame masks the disagreement detector for the rest of the
run".** The code fact behind that claim is real — `_detect_disagreement`
(`warrior_life.py:453`) requires `self.econ_agent.goal_stack.current is None` — but the
consequence does not follow, on two independent grounds:

- **It is not what silenced the alarm in this run.** In the logged state `mode` is `hunt`
  and `target_cap` is `None`, so the first two conjuncts of that guard already fail
  without the goal stack being consulted at all. The zero DISAGREEMENT count is fully
  explained by the rule correctly answering "wait rather than stall": there was no want,
  so there was nothing to disagree about. Nothing was masked here.
- **The masking is a bounded delay, not a disable.** The guard can only matter when
  `mode == "economy"` — and the moment the mode is economy the econ agent is ticked again,
  which is precisely what lets the stale frame retire. Measured A/B offline, both arms
  ending in an identical world (an unowned gump, so `ready=[]`, plus enough gold to want
  `buy_boards`): with the stale frame the alarm first fires at **+30** and with an empty
  stack at **+15**; `_clear_stale_ui` closed the unowned gump in BOTH arms, and the stale
  frame retired at +21 with `outcome=failure` via the neutral `cap_run_finished_goal_id`
  marker at `skills/market.py:1529` — the forge1/forge13 lifecycle fix doing exactly its
  job. *(That same A/B, re-run after §5's fix: **15 in BOTH arms**, the repair still
  firing exactly once. The bounded delay is now zero, and the assertion is a test —
  `test_the_hold_does_not_delay_the_disagreement_detector`.)*

**So the cost is three things, and "the alarm is off" is not one of them:** a ~15-tick
delay on any disagreement arising after the strand (`_apply_cognition`, `agent.py:436-442`,
refuses every new capability proposal while a frame is live); ~20 econ ticks then spent
walking the return leg of a sale that finished 280 ticks earlier; and the unconditional
one — **the telemetry lies**. For 272 ticks the operator's status line said
`admitted=sell_furniture` while nothing was executing it. `telemetry_line`'s own docstring
says the want-vs-admitted ambiguity "cost three runs and one wrong root cause"; this
reintroduces it on the `admitted` side.

**The blind spot, now live-reachable.** This document already said, verbatim at line 155:
"stale finished-goals (want=None with an admitted goal lingering) are a distinct shape the
disagreement detector deliberately does not cover." The log is that shape exactly, and
before this entry was written `grep -rn "want=None" docs/` returned that one line and
nothing else — the shape had been named and never observed. This is the first time it has
been seen with live numbers. **The mechanism is a different one than the phrase
implies**, and that difference is the useful part: the audit's referent was a goal whose
FSM finished and whose frame was never popped (forge1's `bank_gold`), which the
run-finished marker fixed. Here the frame's OWNER AGENT stops being ticked, so the FSM
never reaches the marker at all — upstream of that fix. Same signature, new cause. And the
cause is structural to `WarriorLife.tick`'s one-agent-per-tick shape rather than to the
carpenter: any Life whose mode rule can leave `economy` while an economy goal is live
reaches it. The carpenter reaches it EASILY because it has no work skill and its sell
branch is keyed on the very item the sale removes. Deferred as follow-up 10, with the three
candidate repairs and what each costs. **FIXED the same day — §5 below (offline when written;
§6 records the three live runs and which parts of it they reached).**

*And the line-155 sentence needs a correction, not just a citation.* "The disagreement
detector deliberately does not cover it" is still TRUE of that detector —
`_detect_disagreement` still requires an empty goal stack, on purpose, and §5 changed
nothing there. What is no longer true is the implication that the shape therefore goes
unreported: the orchestrator now covers it with a SECOND self-report of its own,
`frame_overdue` → the runner's throttled `FRAME OVERDUE` line, which fires exactly where
the detector cannot (a frame IS on the stack). `want=None admitted=X` also stops being a
symptom by itself — under the hold it is the LEGITIMATE shape of an owed transaction, and
the telemetry says which it is with `+hold` versus `!frozen`/`!overdue`. Line 155 carries
that update inline.

### 4. The economics, live for the second time

`docs/CARPENTER.md` has recorded the solo carpenter's numbers since the day that Life
shipped; this run reproduces them from a different runner invocation, and the doc now
carries both. Every figure lines up:

| quantity | CARPENTER.md | this run | source |
|---|---|---|---|
| seed | 129 | 129 | `BOARD_BATCH_COST + SAW_COST` = 114 + 15 |
| boards bought | 20 for 60g | gold 129 → 69 | Board buy 3g (`SBCarpenter.cs`) |
| boards per throne | 19 | 20 bought − 19 = boards=1 left | `CarpenterCraft.craft_material_per_item` |
| throne sale | 24g | gold 69 → 93 | Throne sell 24g |
| restock line | 114g | 93 < 114 → stops | `BuyBoards.buy_amount` 38 × 3g |
| per-throne margin | −33g on 19 boards | `net=-36g` | the doc's own price table |

The run's `net=-36g` IS the doc's −33g plus the one board it could not use. The doc's
verdict applies unchanged: *"At vendor prices the craft destroys value, so a carpenter
buying its own material cannot profit, however well the code works."* This run is not a
new defect — it is the stated warning, measured a second time, and the agent ending
**below the price of its own next batch** (93 against 114) is the warning's whole content.
Detail and the one number the two runs do not share is in `docs/CARPENTER.md`.

*Unremarked in the log and noted here because nothing else will: hp held at 80/80 through
`t=216` and then fell to 47/80 by `t=291`, recovering to 48 at the final sample — 33 hp
over the last ~85 ticks of wandering. Nothing in the run reports being hit and no reflex
fires that the telemetry shows. Not investigated, and a single run is not evidence of a
defect; it is written down so a second sighting is a second sighting.*

### 5. The fix — an exit-edge hold, and the third bound the review had to put on it

**Follow-up 10 is closed. OFFLINE ONLY WHEN THIS WAS WRITTEN — read "The honest half"
below, then §6, which records the three live runs and names the bound they never
reached.** Three production files: `anima2/warrior_life.py` (the fix),
`anima2/life_runner.py` (the telemetry that lied), `anima2/village.py` (the report
channel — and one pre-existing hole it closed, below), plus a new
`tests/test_life_frame_hold.py`. 1440 → **1462 tests**, **no existing assertion modified,
weakened or deleted**, both ruff gates clean. `docs/WOODSMAN.md` carries the operator
legend for the new telemetry.

*The pre-existing hole, worth its own sentence because the new report would have shipped
dead through it:* `_ThrottledAgent` (the proxy `run_supply_pair` and
`run_artisan_mage_village` drive their Lives through) has no `__getattr__`, so
`_run_worker`'s `getattr(agent, "rule_gate_disagreement", None)` was unconditionally
`None` for the throttled carpenter and the throttled mage — **the two Lives that run their
economy agent nearly every tick were the two that could never print the disagreement
alarm.** Both self-reports now pass through explicitly.

**The mechanism, restated in one paragraph.** `decide_mode` is a pure function of
`(obs, memory)` — that purity is the property the ~150k-point concordance lattice rests
on — so it structurally cannot see the goal stack, and it answers `"hunt"` the instant the
world-fact its branch was keyed on flips. For every economy branch of all five Lives that
fact is something the transaction ITSELF changes: the goods leave the pack, the coin
moves, the pile is lifted. For a sale that instant is the moment the vendor TAKES the item
— mid-transaction, with the walk home still owed. `WarriorLife.tick` then ticks the OTHER
agent, and three things freeze together because all three only advance inside
`Agent.tick`: the capability FSM, the `cap_*_finished` markers it would have to write
(every capability `step` returns `RUNNING`, and `CapabilityGoalComplete` is a planner
skill selected on a LATER tick), and `GoalStack.expire_due`, whose deadline is counted in
that same agent's ticks. §3 above has the live numbers.

**What the fix does.** `WarriorLife.tick` gains an EXIT-edge rule, after the `econ_grace`
entry hysteresis and before `self.mode` is assigned: if `decide` answered `"hunt"` while a
frame is live on the economy stack, the mode is HELD at `"economy"` so the frame's owner
agent keeps being ticked and can finish what it started. Three deliberate non-changes ride
with it: **`decide_mode` is not touched** (all five stay pure, so the concordance lattice
and its `_NOT_DRIVEN_BY_THE_LIFE` equality are untouched); **`want=` is not rewritten** to
the held frame's capability, because fixing the `admitted=` lie by rewriting `want=` would
re-create the identical ambiguity on the other side; and **one agent per orchestrator tick
stays the rule** — follow-up 10's candidate (i), a second economy tick on the exit edge, is
exactly what was NOT done, since that is what would make "which agent ticks" conditional on
goal state and break the route/reflex cadence the extra-pump comment records. The one
addition is a pin: `_econ_streak` is held at `econ_grace` for the duration, so the tick a
transaction retires commits straight back to the economy instead of re-earning a
hysteresis that exists to filter a mid-equip transient, not a completed trade.

**The bound, and why it needed a third one.** The first implementation claimed the hold
was self-limiting on two bounds. It is not, and the review measured why:

1. **The FSM's give-up ladder** returns `mkt_phase` to `"craft"` and writes
   `cap_run_finished_goal_id`, which `CapabilityGoalComplete` closes as a FAILURE. This is
   the usual exit and it is the forge1/forge13 lifecycle fix doing its job — but
   `grep -rn cap_run_finished_goal_id anima2/` has exactly **two writers**,
   `skills/market.py:1529` (`SellItemCapability`) and `:1920` (`BankGoldCapability`).
   Every buy / tool-buy / craft / fetch / process / deliver frame has no ladder at all.
2. **The frame's own deadline**, via `GoalStack.expire_due` — reachable only because the
   hold keeps the economy agent ticking. But `CapabilityPolicy.deadline_can_expire` defers
   at an unsafe yield point, and every `*_can_yield` in `capabilities.py` carries the same
   unconditional "idle UI" clause — **all EIGHT of them, measured**: hand each one a
   `SkillContext` whose observation carries a single `GumpView` and `_bank_`, `_buy_`,
   `_craft_`, `_deliver_`, `_fetch_`, `_process_`, `_sell_` and `_toolbuy_can_yield` all go
   `True → False` (the bank's arrives indirectly, `_bank_idle` → `_bank_ui_clear`).
   `deadline_can_expire` is `not achieved and can_yield`, so **one unowned gump — the
   forge15 wedge, already in this document — holds bound 2 open forever, and holds bound 1
   open too**, since the readiness gates share that clause. A safety interrupt does the
   same with no surface to blame at all: `Survive`/`WarriorSurvive` sits above the
   capability skills in the CAPABILITY planner too, so a wounded character's economy agent
   is TICKED every tick while its FSM is never STEPPED.
3. **So overdue releases the hold.** `frame_overdue` — the frame is past its own
   `deadline_tick` on the economy agent's clock — drops the hold, and the Life becomes
   exactly the pre-fix Life: a stale frame, but alive and hunting. The release depends only
   on the frame's deadline and the economy counter, both of which the hold itself keeps
   advancing, and never on the FSM being stepped — which is precisely what those two states
   withhold. Worst case is now the frame's own budget in orchestrator ticks, and the
   registry's whole spread is small: `bank_gold` **120**, every buy / sell / fetch /
   deliver / process / upgrade **180**, the five `craft_*` **300**. So no hold can outlive
   300 orchestrator ticks plus the repair's slack — measured worst case over 1000
   randomized worlds, 270.

One extension, capped: an overdue frame first gets `_clear_stale_ui` POINTED AT IT
(`_repair_overdue_frame`). That repair's no-goal precondition exists because "a
mid-transaction gump belongs to a live goal" — a frame that has burned its ENTIRE budget
without once reaching a safe yield point has forfeited that premise, and the open surface
is the documented reason it can neither give up nor expire. When a close lands the hold is
extended one tick so the economy agent can USE it; capped at `OVERDUE_REPAIRS = 3` per
frame (one per closable surface class), so repair-and-extend cannot itself run away.
`frame_overdue` is computed from the frame and the clock ALONE, never gated on the hold —
gating it was review-caught blinding the report in exactly the half of the wedge where the
rule still happened to want the capability, and what the rule wants has nothing to do with
why the frame cannot finish.

**Death is the other override, and it covers the whole EPISODE.** `RecoverDeath` is a
WORK-planner reflex whose `can_run` is `dead OR death_waiting_resurrection OR
death_corpse_pending`, and the corpse-reclaim leg runs entirely with `obs.player.dead`
already False. The first implementation keyed the override on `obs.player.dead` alone;
measured, that took the body back from `RecoverDeath` the tick after resurrection and
deferred gear recovery by up to the frame's whole budget (177 ticks for a warrior) — the
naked death-loop `WarriorLife` exists to end. `_death_episode_open` reads all three, off
the HUNT agent's memory, because `decide` sends every dead tick to the hunt agent and so
that is the only agent that ever owns a death episode. The frame simply WAITS while the
episode runs (telemetry says `!frozen`), and the hold resumes when it closes.

**Two claims the review REFUTED, recorded as refutations rather than as the claims.**
Both were the implementation's own, and both were load-bearing:

- *"The hold is SELF-LIMITING, and it is being ticked that limits it."* False in two
  measured states, because being TICKED is not being STEPPED. Re-measured for this record
  against a two-bound build of the working tree (the release and the repair removed,
  nothing else): with an unowned GUMP plus any non-sell/non-bank frame, **four of the five
  Lives were pinned in economy mode with `hunt_after = 0` for the whole 3000-tick window**,
  frame still live, `frame_overdue` True the entire time — **a total livelock, strictly
  worse than the zombie frame the hold was written to fix**, which at least kept hunting.
  A wounded warrior with a starved FSM does the same with no surface to blame at all,
  because `Survive` owns the economy agent's hands. The three-bound version above is what
  shipped, and it takes the same rows to `liveFrame=None`, back in hunt.
  *One surface does NOT reach it, and the reason is a fix already in this document*: a
  stale vendor BUY window is cancelled by the buy FSM's own popup stage (commit `8cdd2f0`,
  the stale-UI addendum above), so `shopbuy` rows release under all three builds. The
  belt-and-braces from that day is load-bearing here; only the gump has no FSM-level owner.

  **CORRECTION, 2026-08-03 — an unsourced number this refutation was made to carry.**
  Four places in the tree (CLAUDE.md, this file twice, `docs/HISTORY.md`) came to say the
  two-bound build "livelocked **24 of 24 worlds**", each citing this bullet. It does not say
  that, and no committed artifact does: `git show HEAD:docs/AUDIT-2026-07-29.md` has no
  "24 of 24" anywhere, and neither does the rest of the tree apart from those four
  sentences. The figure came from a scratch script that was never committed, so it is **not
  reproducible from this repository** — and the same figure rides the pushed commit message
  of `57422e4` ("measured at 24 of 24 worlds permanently held, work agent ticking 6 times in
  3000"), which cannot be amended. **This note is the correction the history cannot carry.**
  What this record CAN stand behind is the two measurements that are actually written down:
  this bullet's own "four of the five Lives were pinned in economy mode with `hunt_after =
  0` for the whole 3000-tick window", and the table below's wedged-gump row
  (`hunt_after=0`, mode pinned to `economy`, frame live, overdue) across 5 professions. A
  "24 of 24" probe may well have been run — it has the shape of the randomized-corpus rows
  in that table — but it left no artifact, so cite the two that did.
- *"No offline world reaches `frame_overdue`: measured worst-case overdue across four
  hostile worlds is 0 ticks."* False. The state is reached by plain worlds built from
  fixtures already in the new test file, and by 128 of 600 randomized worlds. The test that
  simulated it by shortening a live frame's deadline was deleted and replaced with four
  tests over untampered worlds. A measurement that says a state is unreachable is exactly
  the kind of claim this document exists to stop from shipping unchecked.

**What the tests prove** (`tests/test_life_frame_hold.py`, 22 tests). The whole file was
run against a clean `git archive` of HEAD `cc2463c` — **21 of the 22 FAIL there**, and the
one that passes is the one that must (`admitted=None` for an empty stack is asserted
byte-identical). So this is a regression suite for the defect, not a description of the
new code:

- **Structural, not the carpenter's** — parametrized over all five Lives with one uniform
  self-falsifying transaction (`bank_gold`, keyed on `gold > reserve` in every rule): the
  coin moves, and the mode must stay `economy` until the frame RETIRES with a terminal
  outcome the gates can read. Against HEAD's `tick` this is the failure
  `assert {'hunt'} == {'economy'}`.
- **The exact live shape** — the carpenter's sale, asserted to start from the frozen state
  §3 recorded (`mkt_phase='sell'`, both finished markers unset) and to retire instead.
- **Both non-general bounds, separately** — the FSM ladder (retires at 17 econ ticks) and
  `expire_due` (`('buy_boards','expired')` against a 180-tick budget).
- **The third bound, three ways** — a plain wedged world releases within the frame's own
  budget and the work agent runs again; the same world's blocking surface is CLOSED and
  the frame then expires clean; and a starved-FSM world with no surface at all still
  releases, so the release cannot be depending on a repair.
- **The report is not gated on the hold** — same starved FSM, coin left in the pack.
- **Death covers the corpse run** — dead ticks are all `hunt`, the frame WAITS rather than
  dying, the corpse leg is reached after resurrection with the economy agent provably not
  ticked during it, and the hold resumes only once the episode closes.
- **The masking cost is gone** — the A/B §3 measured at +30 vs +15 is now **15 in both
  arms**, in an identical world, with the stale-UI repair still firing exactly once.
- **The telemetry** — `!frozen`, `+hold`, `!overdue`, the `@age/budget` clock, and
  `admitted=None` byte-identical for an empty stack; plus a `capsys` test that the
  runner's `FRAME OVERDUE` line is actually printed and throttled, and one that a
  `_ThrottledAgent` passes both self-reports through.

Independent numbers, re-run for this record rather than quoted, all A/B in identical
worlds against a clean `git archive` of HEAD `cc2463c` (and, for the first row, against
the two-bound build as well):

| measurement | HEAD `cc2463c` | two-bound | shipped (three-bound) |
|---|---|---|---|
| the wedged world, gump rows (5 professions, 3000 ticks after the want expires) | a live frame that never retires — `buy_weapon`/`buy_reagent`/`buy_saw`/`buy_tinker_tool` — but the Life keeps hunting (2989 ticks) | **`hunt_after=0`, mode pinned to `economy`, frame live, overdue — the livelock** | `liveFrame=None`, back in hunt, 2812 ticks — **177 spent**, the frame's 180-tick budget |
| 1000 randomized worlds × 900 ticks: ticks in which a LIVE frame's economy clock did not move | 129,376 | — | **12,096** |
| the same corpus: frames actually retired | 9,362 | — | **10,312** |
| the same corpus: work ticks (the hold's price) | 373,601 | — | 358,794 (**−4.0%**) |
| the same corpus: longest consecutive hold | n/a | — | **270 ticks**, and **0 worlds over 320** — the largest capability budget is 300 |
| 600 randomized worlds × 900 ticks reaching `frame_overdue` | n/a | — | 128 worlds, **all 128 self-resolve, 0 still held at the end** |

The −4% is the honest cost of the hold: ticks spent walking a return leg instead of
hunting. It buys a tenfold drop in frames nobody is finishing, ~950 more frames actually
closed, and a bound.

**The honest half — this is OFFLINE ONLY, and it is the half that matters most here.**
*(Kept verbatim as it was written. §6 below supersedes the shard sentence — the shard was
brought back up the same day — but NOT the paragraph's point: the bound this passage is
most worried about is still the one no live run has reached.)*
There is no shard: the local ServUO used for the 2026-08-03 run has been shut down. Every
number above is `MockBody` with production code, and **no live run has exercised any of
it**. That is uncomfortable in a specific way this document should say out loud: **the
defect was found ON a live run, and the fix changes WHICH INNER AGENT IS TICKED — the hot
path of all five professions, on every tick.** The offline evidence is strong on the
shapes it can build and silent on the ones it cannot: MockBody has no vendor NPC and no
`shop_sell`, so the sale's own completion is applied by editing the mock world; it does not
model server-side latency, a gump that reopens, or a vendor that answers slowly — and
"the surface keeps re-opening" is exactly the state `OVERDUE_REPAIRS` was capped for.

**What the next live run should look for**, in priority order:

1. **`!frozen` on a live frame while the character is NOT dead.** That is the regression
   detector for the hold itself — under the fix, only a death episode or a released
   overdue frame produces it. If it appears anywhere else, the hold has been bypassed.
2. **`+hold` windows that do not end.** Legitimate holds are short and end with a
   retirement in the goal history. Watch that `@age` KEEPS MOVING; a `+hold` whose age is
   frozen is the original defect wearing the new marker.
3. **`FRAME OVERDUE` lines, and whether the Life gets its hands back.** The line means
   bounds 1 and 2 both failed. The fix's promise is that the mode returns to hunt within
   the frame's budget anyway — verify that against the `[hunt]`/`[economy]` tag on the
   status line, and check whether `closing an unowned …` printed just before it.
4. **Work throughput against the pre-fix baseline.** The hold costs real work ticks (a
   1000-world offline A/B measured ~4%); a live run should confirm that a Life still
   hunts, banks and restocks at a recognisable rate rather than spending its day walking
   return legs.
5. **A death mid-transaction.** The corpse run is the one path where a mistake costs gear
   rather than time, and it is the path with the least offline fidelity.

Until such a run exists, the correct summary is: **the defect §3 recorded is fixed offline
and the fix is bounded offline; it is unproven live.**

*(Such a run now exists — three of them, the same day, on a shard that came back up. §6
below is the result, and it splits the sentence above in two: the DEFECT is now fixed
live, and the BOUNDS mostly are not — only bound 2, the frame's own deadline, has live
evidence. This paragraph is left standing exactly as written, because it is the claim §6
is answering. The parenthetical itself first said "the THIRD BOUND is still offline-only",
which undercounted: §6.3 also withdraws the claim that bound 1 was exercised. Later the same
day a fourth run — a forced-state gate, not an ordinary one — closed bound 3 as well: §7.
Bound 1 remains the one bound with no live evidence at all — and still does after §8, which
gives it a signature to be recognised BY without producing one.)*

### 6. The hold, LIVE — the defect is gone, and two of its three bounds still are not

*(This heading first read "and one of its three bounds still is not". Corrected in place:
§6.3's own reading now says bound 1 was never distinguishable from ordinary success on
these logs, so only bound 2 is live-proven. The heading is fixed rather than the sections
under it left to disagree with it.)*

*(FORWARD POINTER, added 2026-08-03 after §7: the heading and everything under it remain
TRUE OF THESE THREE RUNS and are left exactly as written. **Bound 3 has since fired live**,
in a purpose-built forced-state gate rather than an ordinary run —
`anima2/live_frame_overdue_gate.py`, §7. So the standing count is now bound 2 and bound 3
live-proven, **bound 1 still unexercised**, and the death override still unexercised.)*

*(SECOND FORWARD POINTER, added 2026-08-03 after §8: **that count is unchanged.** §8.3 makes
bound 1 OBSERVABLE — a retirement now names which branch closed the frame — but observability

Two of three, still.)*

Three runs against the local ServUO at `127.0.0.1:2594` on 2026-08-03, brain at `22bcf10`
(§5's fix plus its doc commit), all with `--monitor`. They are the runs §5's checklist
asked for, and they answer **two** of its five items — item 2 (`+hold` windows that end)
and item 4 (work throughput). *(This sentence first said three of five, counting item 1,
`!frozen`. §6.3 withdraws that: `!frozen` was structurally unreachable on these runs, so
its clean sheet answers nothing.)* Items 1, 3 and 5 are all still open.

#### 6.1 The A/B that closes §3's defect

Same command, same knob, same tick count, before and after. The "before" arm is the run
§3 is written from (`cc2463c`); the "after" arm re-ran it verbatim on `22bcf10`:

| | pre-fix `cc2463c` | post-fix `22bcf10` |
|---|---|---|
| command | `python -m anima2.village --carpenter --knob bank_reserve=400 --ticks 300 --monitor` | identical |
| status samples | 32 | 33 |
| samples naming a capability nothing was executing | **30** — `admitted=sell_furniture` with `furniture=0` | **0** |
| `admitted=None` | 0 | **31** |
| `!frozen` / `!overdue` / `FRAME OVERDUE` | marker did not exist | 0 / 0 / 0 |
| DISAGREEMENT lines | 0 | 0 |
| end state | `net=-36g`, gold 93, banked 0, 277 steps | `net=-36g`, gold 93, banked 0, 279 steps |

It is the same carpenter day in both arms, down to the staging banner (`staged:
Sten@(2609, 474) and 129g seed  (reserve 400)` — §1's knob-channel proof, reproduced):
129g seed → 20 boards for 60g (129→69) → one throne → sold for 24g (69→93) → 93 is below
the 114g restock line, so it stops. Net, gold and banked are identical and the step counts
differ by two, so the hold cost this Life nothing measurable. **The one difference is the
frame.** Pre-fix the
`sell_furniture` frame sat on the stack for the last 272 ticks while its owner agent was
never ticked again; post-fix it is gone by the next sample and the line reads
`admitted=None` for the remaining 31 samples. That is the cleanest evidence in this
document that the defect is fixed: 30 lying lines out of 32 became 0 out of 33, with
nothing else about the run changed.

The new telemetry is readable in a real status line (samples 1 and 2 of the post-fix run):

```
— carpenter [economy] want=buy_boards admitted=buy_boards@3/180 ready=[] saw=yes boards=0 furniture=0 hp=80/80 gold=129 banked=0 net=+0g (+0g/h) —
— carpenter [economy] want=sell_furniture admitted=craft_carpentry@8/300 ready=['sell_furniture'] saw=yes boards=1 furniture=1 hp=80/80 gold=69 banked=0 net=-60g (-26985g/h) —
```

**No `+hold` was sampled in this run, and that is worth stating rather than glossing:** the
sale's frame was created and retired between the `t=19` and `t=28` samples — inside one
~4-second sampling interval — so this arm proves the LIE is gone and shows nothing at all
about the hold WORKING. That took the forge pair (§6.2).

*One thing that did NOT reproduce, recorded because §4 asked for a second sighting:* the
baseline's unexplained 33 hp lost over its last ~85 ticks (80/80 → 47/80). All 33 post-fix
samples read `hp=80/80`. This is not a fix and not evidence of one — the two carpenters
wandered different ground (post-fix it stayed within `(2611, 474)` for its first three
samples and then `(2608..2611, 475)` for the remaining thirty; pre-fix it ranged to
`y=483`) — so the count of sightings stays at ONE. *(The post-fix range first read
`(2608..2611, 475)` alone, which silently dropped the staging tile the run opens on.)*

#### 6.2 The hold, observed — 31 samples of it

`python -m anima2.village --forge-pair --ticks 1800 --monitor`, the miner→tinker chain.
208 status samples; **`+hold` on 31 of them**, the first direct live observation of the
mechanism. One real line, verbatim:

```
— forge pair  craft_tongs(mkt_phase=craft bs_state=open cap_craft_stage=finished) grimm[iron=0 win=0/24 ph=mine]  drop[grimm_sees=0 pim_sees=0]  pim[economy want=None admitted=craft_tongs@146/300+hold ready=[] iron=0 tongs=4 gold=72 banked=23 net=+95g (+1498g/h)] —
```

`want=None admitted=craft_tongs@146/300+hold` is the whole fix in one field: the rule has
stopped wanting it, the orchestrator is finishing it anyway, the frame is 146 econ ticks
into a 300-tick budget, and the clock is MOVING. Pre-fix that same pairing printed with no
age and no marker and meant the opposite thing.

**Frame ages RESET rather than climbing forever** — the `craft_tongs` age series across the
whole run, `|` marking a new frame:

```
7 16 25 | 1 10 19 | 8 17 26 | 3 12 22 | 1 10 19 28 | 8 17 26 35 45 … 292 | 10 19 | 7 17 | 3 12 21 | 5 15 24 | 4 13 23 | …
```

82 `craft_tongs` samples over **19** distinct frames (counted as age decreases, so a lower
bound). The last age SAMPLED before each frame vanished is `25 19 26 22 28 292 19 17 21 24
23 18 17 24 19 24 21 25 19` — every frame but one was last seen between 17 and 28 and gone
by the next sample ≤9 ticks later, against a 300-tick budget. Max age reached per
capability, against that capability's own registry budget:

| capability | budget | max age seen | samples |
|---|---|---|---|
| `craft_tongs` | 300 | **292** | 82 |
| `buy_iron` | 180 | **177** | 61 |
| `bank_gold` | 120 | 11 | 8 |
| `sell_tongs` | 180 | 4 | 8 |
| `fetch_iron` | 180 | 1 | 1 |

Across all three runs: **`!frozen` 0, `!overdue` 0, `FRAME OVERDUE` 0, Traceback 0.** The
§5 checklist's item 1 — the regression detector — never fired, on 306 samples across three
runs and two professions. **Read that count with §6.3's correction, not as an independent
result:** `!frozen` is only PRINTABLE when a death episode is open or a frame is overdue and
unrepaired, and neither happened, so its zero is entailed by the other two zeros on this
line rather than evidence beside them.

*And follow-up 14's prediction came true verbatim:* during the long hold the tinker stopped
walking, and `_run_worker`'s pulse printed `** Pim: NO PROGRESS for 120 … 360 ticks **`
(pulses at 120, 160, 200, 240, 280, 320, 360 — an earlier draft wrote `40 … 360`, but the
40- and 80-tick pulses in that log land BEFORE the first `+hold` sample: Pim had already
been standing still for two pulses when the hold began).
That follow-up called it "correctly, since it IS stuck" — live it is stuck WAITING, which
is what the hold is for, and the reader now has `+hold` on the same screen to say so.

#### 6.3 What these runs did NOT exercise — and it is TWO of the three bounds

State this as plainly as the successes: **only bound 2 is live-proven. Bounds 1 and 3 are
not, for two different reasons.** *(This section first said "the third bound is still
offline-proven only" and credited bound 1 to `sell_tongs`/`bank_gold`'s low ages. Both
readings are corrected below, in place; the original claims are quoted where they were
wrong so the correction is checkable.)*

*(Scope note, 2026-08-03: everything in this section is a statement about THESE THREE RUNS
and stays true of them. **Bound 3 has since been reached live by a forced-state gate — §7.**
Bound 1 has not, by any run, and nothing in §7 changes that.)*

- **Bound 2 (the frame's own deadline) IS live-proven, and it is the one clean bound.**
  `craft_tongs` at 292/300 (4 tongs of a batch of 5, so unachieved) and `buy_iron` at
  177/180 (`iron=1`, so unachieved) both vanished from the next sample. Neither family
  writes `cap_run_finished_goal_id` and neither frame was achieved, so
  `CapabilityGoalComplete` had neither branch available: `expire_due` is the only thing
  that could have closed either. They rode their FULL budgets and retired on the deadline.
- **Bound 1 (the FSM give-up ladder) is UNEXERCISED as far as these logs can tell.** The
  original claim here was *"Bound 1 worked, and only where it exists — `sell_tongs` frames
  peaked at age 4 and `bank_gold` at 11, the two capability families that write
  `cap_run_finished_goal_id`."* That does not follow. All **eight** sampled `sell_tongs`
  frames in the 1800-tick run sit beside a COMPLETED sale — `tongs` → 0 and the tinker's
  gold up by the vendor's price on the adjacent sample, every time — and all **six**
  `bank_gold` frames beside a COMPLETED deposit (`banked` 0 → 23 → 118 → 223 → 328 → 433 →
  503, one per frame) — and `CapabilityGoalComplete.can_run` closes exactly that case by its
  ACHIEVEMENT branch (`profession.py:299`), before it ever reaches the run-finished branch
  at `:311`. The status line shows only that the frame is gone; it cannot name the branch.
  The control is in the same log: a **ladderless** `buy_iron` frame was last seen at age 4
  and gone by the next sample, exactly like `sell_tongs`. **A low max age is therefore not
  a give-up-ladder signature at all**, and nothing in these runs distinguishes bound 1 from
  ordinary success. Proving it live needs a transaction that FAILS — a sale the vendor
  refuses, a bank trip that cannot reach its banker — not another healthy run.
  *(Updated 2026-08-03, and the update is NARROW: **bound 1 is now OBSERVABLE and still
  UNEXERCISED.** §8.3 adds telemetry that names the branch — `FRAME RETIRED <cap>#<id>
  age=<a>/<b> -> giveup (bound 1: the FSM's give-up ladder)` per tick, and a `retired=`
  tally on the status line — by reading `frame.outcome`, which `GoalStack._archive` has
  always stamped. So the sentence "the status line shows only that the frame is gone" is no
  longer true of a FUTURE run. Everything else in this bullet is unchanged: these three runs
  still contain no bound-1 evidence, no run since has produced any, and proving it live
  still needs a transaction that FAILS. Nothing was executed to make it readable and no run
  was performed — see §8.5.)*
- **Bound 3 never fired, and the mechanism is narrower than first written.** `expire_due`
  triggers at `ticks >= deadline_tick` (`agent.py:638`) while `frame_overdue` needs
  `ticks > deadline_tick` (`warrior_life.py:535`), so a frame that CAN yield always retires
  one tick before the marker could print — that part stands. What was WRONG was the
  yield-point half: the original text said *"a terminal trip can yield even with a surface
  open: `_buy_can_yield` ends `(ui_clear or finished)` and `_craft_can_yield` ends
  `(not started or terminal)`."* Only the buy half is right. `(not started or terminal)` is
  `_craft_can_yield`'s SECOND clause (`capabilities.py:648`); the function goes on to
  require `obs.pending_target is None and not obs.gumps and obs.popup is None and
  obs.shop_buy is None and obs.shop_sell is None` (`:654-658`). **A terminal CRAFT trip
  cannot yield with a surface open — craft is precisely the family where a surface at the
  deadline WOULD produce an overdue frame.** The three frames that expired with `ui=shopbuy`
  still open were `buy_iron` frames, and `(ui_clear or finished)` is what let them; the
  292/300 `craft_tongs` frame expired because its sample carried **no `ui=` field at all**,
  so it was terminal AND clear. The conclusion survives — bound 3 did not fire — but by that
  route, not by "terminal is enough".
- **Which means bound 3 is closer to an ordinary run than this section first implied.**
  The blocking surface for a craft frame does not have to be UNOWNED: `not obs.gumps` is
  unconditional, so the craft FSM's own menu blocks its own yield point. These runs had **14
  `ui=gump` samples, every one of them on `craft_tongs`** — the state exists live; it simply
  never coincided with a deadline, because all 14 sat at ages 2–23 against a 300-tick budget.
  An ordinary run whose craft gump happens to be up at tick 300 reaches bound 3 by accident.
- **So `frame_overdue`'s release and `_repair_overdue_frame` have zero live ticks** *(true of
  these three runs; a fourth run on the same day gave them their first — §7: overdue at econ
  tick 301 against `deadline_tick=300`, one gump closed, hold released one tick later)* — the
  exact component whose absence made the FIRST version of this fix livelock the wedged world
  (§5's first refutation, which measured **four of the five Lives pinned in economy mode with
  `hunt_after = 0` for the whole 3000-tick window**; the "24 of 24 worlds" that stood in this
  sentence is unsourced and is corrected in §5's CORRECTION note). Four tests and
  128-of-600 randomized worlds stand behind it, and nothing else.
- **Checklist item 5 was not exercised either:** nobody died in any of the three runs, so
  the death override and the corpse run remain offline-only too.
- **And `!frozen`'s zero is ENTAILED by the two absences above, not independent of them.**
  `life_runner.py:210-215` prints `!frozen` only when a frame is live AND
  `getattr(life, "mode", "economy") != "economy"` (`:212-213`). But `tick`
  (`warrior_life.py:533-541`)
  sets `holding = (mode != "economy" and frame is not None and not
  self._death_episode_open(obs) and (not self.frame_overdue or repaired))` and then forces
  `mode = "economy"` whenever `holding`. So with a frame live, the mode can only be
  non-economy if a death episode is open or the frame is overdue-and-unrepaired — and this
  section has just recorded that NEITHER occurred. `!frozen = 0` was structurally
  unreachable on these runs. It belongs with the things the runs could not test, not with
  the things they proved.

**What a future run should do to reach bound 3 — do not wait for one to happen.** Follow
this document's own method (forced-state gates find FSM bugs) with a `live_*_gate.py` in the
shape of `live_tinker_bank_gate.py`: stage the tinker mid-`craft_tongs`, have the CONTROL
plane put a surface the brain does not own in front of it *while the frame is still
running* (a GM-opened gump is the one with no FSM-level owner — a vendor BUY window is not,
per `8cdd2f0`), and run past the 300-tick craft budget. Assert three things: `FRAME
OVERDUE` prints, `closing an unowned …` prints just before it, and the status tag returns
to `[hunt]` within the frame's own budget. A wounded-character variant (`Survive` starving
the FSM with no surface at all) exercises the release path that cannot depend on the
repair. Both are minutes of live budget, not hours. **A cheaper variant now that the
mechanism is read correctly:** hold the craft menu open past the craft budget — no GM gump
needed, since `_craft_can_yield` refuses on any gump — which the same gate can do by
stalling the FSM's own close step.

***DONE — and this paragraph was right about the shape and wrong about two details; §7 has
the run and the corrections.*** The gate is `anima2/live_frame_overdue_gate.py`, it passed on
its first attempt in ~4 minutes, and it used the "cheaper variant" above. What it had to fix
in the prescription: (1) *"a GM-opened gump is the one with no FSM-level owner"* — no ServUO
command was found that pushes a gump to ANOTHER player's client (`[Props`, `[Bank`,
`[ViewEquip` all send to the COMMANDING mobile), so that route was abandoned unverified
rather than spent on; (2) *"stalling the FSM's own close step"* has no lever — every abort
path in `skills/craft.py::step` ACCELERATES the close, and `max_goal_steps = 240` is below
the 300-tick deadline so the FSM closes its own gump before the deadline anyway. The lever
that works is the wounded-character starve, which this paragraph proposed only for the
no-surface release variant: it turns out to be the enabler for BOTH halves at once. See
§7.1.

#### 6.4 The 292-tick hold, read closely

One `craft_tongs` frame ran from age 8 to 292 — 32 of the run's samples, 31 of them marked
`+hold`, which is every `+hold` in the run. What it actually was:

- **The clock MOVED the whole way**: ages 17, 26, 35, 45, … 292 against Pim's own tick
  counter 402, 411, 420, 430, … — exactly 1:1, because the hold keeps the economy agent
  being ticked. This is the precise opposite of §3's defect, where the age would have been
  pinned. It is not a freeze.
- **The world was static** — precisely: Pim's position `(2608,475)` and `steps=198` are
  unchanged on all 32 samples, and `iron=0 tongs=4 gold=72 banked=23 net=+95g` holds for
  **30 of the 32**. *(This bullet first said the whole line was "unchanged for the entire
  window". It was not: at age 8 the pack read `iron=4 tongs=1` and at age 17 `iron=2
  tongs=2` — the last two crafts of the batch, finishing as the iron ran out. From age 26
  to 292 nothing moves. Gold, banked and net are constant across all 32.)* The tinker was
  waiting on
  the miner — `drop[grimm_sees=0 pim_sees=0]` throughout — and the delivery landed ~75 ticks
  AFTER the frame retired: the miner's reward jumped 107.8 → 176.8 as it reached the drop at
  `(2609,475)`, and the tinker's `iron` went 0 → 69 on the next sample (the drop counters
  stay 0 on both sides because the pickup fits inside one ~9-tick sampling interval).
- **It was terminal-but-unachieved, and that is the finding.** `cap_craft_stage=finished`
  from age 26 on, with `tongs=4`. `TinkerTongs.craft_batch` is 5 (inherited), and the craft
  proof ends `_pack_graphic(ctx, output_graphic) == batch` — so 4 tongs is NOT achieved.
  Craft writes no `cap_run_finished_goal_id`, so `CapabilityGoalComplete.can_run` had
  neither of its two branches. The frame had no exit but its deadline and took it: last
  seen at 292/300, gone by the next sample, no `!overdue`.
- **So the honest label is neither "freeze" nor "slow close": it is bound 2 doing the
  ENTIRE job on a capability family that has no bound 1** — §5's own paragraph 1, live. The
  surrounding `craft_tongs` frames, last seen at ages 17–28, are the ones where the batch
  completed and the achievement proof closed them.
- **The cost was real, and it was not work.** `want=None` for the whole window: iron 0
  (nothing to craft), 4 tongs (below the sell line), 72 gold. The tinker's `work_skill` is
  `None`, so its hunt mode is wandering — the ~275 ticks between the first and last `+hold`
  sample displaced wandering, not production. Pre-fix, those same ticks would have been
  wandering beside a frame frozen at
  age 17, which is the state §3 recorded and which refuses every new proposal — and the
  miner's 69 ingots arrived while that frozen frame would still have been there.
- **What it argues for** is giving the ladderless families a neutral run-finished marker so
  a terminal-but-unachieved craft closes on the tick it becomes terminal instead of 266
  ticks later. Follow-up 15.

#### 6.5 The economics — the flagship chain still runs, and the number flatters it twice

| run | ticks | net | rate at final sample | banked |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| forge pair | 1800 | **+585g** | **+2528g/h** | 0 → 23 → 118 → 223 → 328 → 433 → **503** (six observed deposits) |
| forge pair | 600 | +270g | +3734g/h | 188 |

Against the forge figures this document already records: **+140g at ~+2000g/h** (the first
committed tinker chain, implementation record 6) and **+235g at ~1900g/h** (forge18, the
stale-UI section). The post-fix pair sits above both on rate and well above both on
absolute gold banked.

**This is not a controlled comparison and nothing here should be read as one.** Different
run lengths (600 and 1800 against the earlier runs' own budgets), different vein and
relocation luck, one arm each, and no pre-fix forge-pair run at 1800 ticks to compare
against. The claim these numbers support is exactly one: the hold's **offline-measured**
~4% work-tick cost — the 1000-randomized-world A/B in §5's table, `MockBody` throughout,
with no live equivalent because there is no pre-fix live arm at this length — did not break
the flagship positive-margin loop, which still crafts, sells, banks at six observed deposits
and ends 585g up. *(The `~4%` first stood here unmarked, inside a section otherwise made of
live numbers.)*

**AND THE SUPPLY SIDE STOPPED DEAD AT 43% OF THE RUN, WITH NOTHING FLAGGING IT.** This is the
larger of the two ways the table flatters the run, and it was missing from the first draft
of this section entirely. Grimm's cumulative reward reached `out+176.9` on the **t=765**
sample and never moved again; his `steps` froze at 139 on the same sample; and on all **126
samples that follow** he reads `ph=mine` with `iron=0` — no `smelt` phase, no `deliver`
phase, ever again, after eleven `deliver` and nine `smelt` samples earlier in the run. His
relocation window turns over at the same moment: it read `win=0/24` (zero stuck swings out
of 24) at t=681, and from t=775 on it fills with nothing but stuck swings — `5/6`, `9/10`,
`14/15`, `19/20`, then `n/n` for the rest of the run, i.e. every single swing verdict
unproductive. **So five of the six deposits, and every gold
above the first 23g, came AFTER the miner stopped producing** — the tinker was working
through ONE 69-ingot delivery that landed at t≈756 (the miner's last act: reward 107.8 →
176.8 as he reached the drop at `(2609,475)`, and the tinker's `iron` 0 → 69 on the next
sample). The chain is real and it banked 503g; what it did NOT do is run as a chain for
57% of the run.

**What the log cannot tell you is whether he died**, and that is the finding rather than an
aside. `run_forge_pair`'s status line prints no hp and no death flag for either agent —
`life_runner.hp_readout` exists and returns `DEAD`, but the pair line never calls it — so a
dead miner, a lost pickaxe and a permanently dead vein are indistinguishable from the tape.
He even keeps relocating (position moves; `steps` counts only `Walk`, and a relocation uses
`WalkTo`), which is why nothing looks wrong. This is the same signature, on the same runner,
as the 2026-07-30 health check's adopted item: *"a liveness line for NON-Life agents (the
forge miner died silently — `out+0.0 steps=0` for 72 samples with nothing flagging it)"*.
That item was never done; this run is its second sighting and it is now **follow-up 17**.
The 600-tick run is the control — Grimm's reward there is still moving on the last sample
(`out+87.1`), so this is not an artifact of how the line is printed.

*(The liveness half was built the same day — §8.1, including why `NO PROGRESS` could never
have caught this: its ten pulses in this run all read the identical string `for 40 ticks`,
three of them before the freeze. The hp half was NOT built and is follow-up 18. Neither has
run on a shard.)*

**And the g/h figure flatters the run on the demand side too.** The last 556 of the tinker's 1800 ticks earned
nothing: `banked=503`, `gold=82`, `net=+585g`, position and step count frozen, and
`ui=shopbuy` on all 75 of those samples. That is 36% of the samples. It has nothing to do
with the hold — `+hold` appears nowhere in that window and `want=buy_iron` throughout — and
§6.7 is what it actually was. A rate computed over a run whose last third was wedged is
not the chain's rate.

#### 6.6 The detector and its repair still fire under the hold

The reviewer's specific concern about the hold was that keeping a frame alive longer keeps
`_detect_disagreement`'s no-goal guard closed longer. Live, twice in the 1800-tick run,
both complete and both AFTER the frame had retired:

The second of the two, VERBATIM and CONTIGUOUS — all five consecutive lines of the log, no
elision:

```
— forge pair  grimm[iron=0 ph=mine]  drop[grimm_sees=0 pim_sees=0]  pim[economy want=buy_iron admitted=None ready=[] iron=1 tongs=0 gold=82 banked=503 ui=shopbuy net=+585g (+3005g/h)] —
  Grimm     miner      @(2605,499) t=1496 out+176.9 steps=139 says=0
  Pim       tinker     @(2608,475) t=1613 out+0.3 steps=293 says=3  "Hail, friend. Pim greets you."
  ** Pim: closing an unowned vendor BUY window — it was refusing every capability **
  ** Pim: RULE-vs-GATE DISAGREEMENT — wants 'buy_iron', admission refuses, 10 ticks **
```

*(An earlier draft of this block dropped the two per-agent snapshot lines from the middle
and presented the remaining three as one quotation. They are restored: a quoted block in
this document is contiguous log or it says which lines are missing. The restored lines
happen to carry §6.5's other finding on their face — `Grimm … out+176.9 steps=139`, three
hundred ticks after he last produced anything.)*

`admitted=None` on the sample immediately before each pair is what makes this evidence: the
guard was satisfied, the alarm fired at its configured `disagreement_ticks` (10, printed in
the line), and the repair ran. The delay from retirement to report was the configured
threshold, not the frame's budget — the same conclusion as §3's offline A/B (15 in both
arms), now with two live instances. The hold does not mask the detector.

#### 6.7 What the runs found that is NOT the hold: the vendor BUY window did not clear

The stale-UI section above (2026-07-31) says the "each recurrence costs one goal lifetime"
gap is CLOSED by commit `8cdd2f0`, with the honest half that the FSM-layer half of that fix
had OFFLINE TESTS ONLY and live re-verification outstanding. **These runs are that
outstanding re-verification, and the cost that gap was supposed to close is back.**

- 1800-tick run, from the tinker's t≈1244 to the end: `ui=shopbuy` on **75 consecutive
  samples** (36% of the run's samples), iron stuck at 1, and **three complete `buy_iron`
  frames each riding its FULL 180-tick budget** (ages 6→177, 5→176, 4→176) before expiring.
  Each expiry was followed ~10 ticks later by the repair and the alarm (§6.6), and the
  surface was present again on the very next sample. That is the "one goal lifetime per
  recurrence" shape, three times over.
- **The window was the FIRST trip's own, not a stranger's.** The sample before it
  (`bank(stage=deposit …)`) carries no `ui=` field at all, and the next reads
  `buy_iron(mkt_phase=buy … buy_stage=popup)` with `ui=shopbuy` — the trip opened it, then
  ended at `mkt_phase=craft` with the window still up, `buy_stage` popped and no iron
  bought. That is forge16's shape ("a vendor BUY window left behind by a finished
  `buy_iron` trip"), not `8cdd2f0`'s ("a window already open when a trip starts"), so this
  run gave the FSM-layer fix no observed opportunity to fire: it is still untested live,
  and it is not what failed here.
- **But `8cdd2f0`'s own case DID occur on trips 2 and 3 — it just never reached the stage
  where its fix lives.** Correcting the bullet above, which is right about the FIRST trip
  and was over-generalized in follow-up 16 to "that case never arose": the second wedged
  `buy_iron` frame opens at `@5/180` on a sample whose PREDECESSOR already reads
  `ui=shopbuy` (`want=buy_iron admitted=None … ui=shopbuy`), and the third does the same at
  `@4/180`. Both therefore START against a window that is already open — exactly
  `8cdd2f0`'s premise. What neither of them does is reach the buy FSM's **popup stage**,
  which is where the empty-list cancel is emitted: every one of their samples reads
  `mkt_phase=craft` with `buy_stage` ABSENT, whereas the first trip's opening sample read
  `mkt_phase=buy … buy_stage=popup`. So the honest statement is **no OBSERVED OPPORTUNITY
  to fire**, not "the case never arose" — and that sharpens candidate (b) below from a
  guess into the leading one.
- 600-tick run: the same shape, just starting. Its last 7 samples are `buy_iron` 5→55/180
  with `ui=shopbuy` and gold 82 / banked 188 frozen. **It did not "end healthy, the run
  ended not the agent"** — it ended in the opening of this wedge.
- **Nor did the 1800-tick run end healthy.** It ended with the tinker's budget spent, its
  third wedged `buy_iron` frame just expired, `want=buy_iron admitted=None ready=[]
  ui=shopbuy`, and `NO PROGRESS for 560 ticks`. The last 12 samples show only the miner
  still ticking — *and "ticking" is all he is doing: he had produced nothing since t=765,
  §6.5. Neither agent was healthy at the end of this run.*
- **The mechanism is not resolved by these logs and is not guessed at here.** Two candidates,
  both cheap to falsify offline: (a) the empty-list cancel is emitted but the surface is not
  actually closed — server-side, or because the body's observation keeps a stale `shop_buy`;
  (b) the follow-on trips never re-enter their buy phase at all — every wedged sample reads
  `mkt_phase=craft` with `buy_stage` ABSENT (i.e. popped), which is what a trip that already
  finished for a previous goal id looks like, not one at its popup stage. Follow-up 16.
  *(RESOLVED OFFLINE the same day, in favour of candidate (b) and with a third defect nobody
  had listed — §8.2. `buy_offer_reopens` was never cleaned up between trips, so one unlucky
  trip left every later trip giving up on its FIRST `window` tick: measured offline, trip 1
  gives up at tick 7 and trips 2 and 3 at tick 1. The re-roll also emitted no action, so it
  re-rolled nothing against a window that is a snapshot; and no give-up path closed the
  window at all. All three fixed. **Candidate (a) is untouched and still open**, and the
  composition into these samples is consistency rather than proof — re-read here, exactly ONE
  of the 61 wedged samples carries `buy_stage=popup`, and trips 2 and 3 are already back at
  `mkt_phase=craft` by econ ages 5 and 4, so their whole buy phase fits inside one sampling
  interval. No shard has re-run it.)*
- One thing this DOES confirm of §5: the shopbuy rows RELEASE. Every one of the three frames
  retired on its own deadline and the Life was never pinned. The livelock §5's first
  refutation measured needs a surface with no FSM-level owner, and this is not one.

## 7. Bound 3, LIVE — the overdue release and its repair, on a shard (2026-08-03)

§6.3 ends by saying what a future run should do to reach bound 3 and telling the reader not
to wait for one to happen. **That run exists. `anima2/live_frame_overdue_gate.py`, one
attempt, exit 0, ~4 minutes of live budget against the local ServUO at `127.0.0.1:2594`.**
`frame_overdue` became True on a shard, `_repair_overdue_frame` closed a surface, and the
hold released — the component §6.3 recorded with ZERO live ticks, and the one whose absence
made the first version of this fix livelock the wedged world (§5's first refutation:
*"four of the five Lives were pinned in economy mode with `hunt_after = 0` for the whole
3000-tick window"*). Bound 1 and the death override are still unexercised; §7.4 says so
plainly, because this section is not a licence to sweep them along.

#### 7.1 Why an ordinary run cannot get there, restated as the thing the gate had to defeat

§6.3 already names the one-tick race — `expire_due` fires at `ticks >= deadline_tick`
(`agent.py:638`), `frame_overdue` needs `ticks > deadline_tick` (`warrior_life.py:535`) —
so a frame that CAN yield at its deadline always retires one tick before the marker could
print. Bound 3 is reachable only through a frame that cannot yield at the exact deadline
tick. §6.3's closing paragraph then offered a "cheaper variant": *"hold the craft menu open
past the craft budget — no GM gump needed, since `_craft_can_yield` refuses on any gump."*
**That variant is right about the surface and wrong about it being free**, and the
correction is measured, not argued:

- `CraftItemCapability.max_goal_steps = 240` is BELOW the 300-tick craft deadline. Left
  alone, the craft FSM aborts at ~242 steps, reaches `cap_craft_stage='finished'`, and
  **closes its own gump** before the deadline — `frame_overdue` never becomes True and
