  bound 2 closes the frame. Measured offline as this gate's own bound-2 control, and it is
  what §6.3's own 14 `ui=gump` samples (all ages 2–23) are the live shadow of.
- Every way of breaking the craft mid-flight ACCELERATES the close rather than wedging it:
  `skills/craft.py::step` routes `tool is None`, insufficient ingots, the not-enough-metal
  cliloc, the proximity cliloc and `limit_reached` all to `cap_craft_abort_goal_id` →
  `stage="close"` → `GumpResponse(button=0)`.
- So the surface is the easy half. **The lever is stopping the FSM from being stepped at
  all** — which is why the gate wounds the character: `Survive` is `skills[0]` of the
  capability planner for every profession (`profession.py`), so a wounded character's
  economy agent is ticked every tick while its capability FSM never is. Nothing answers the
  gump, and `expire_due` still runs, because it sits upstream of skill selection in
  `Agent.tick`.

*(A second premise of §6.3's prescription is also corrected here, measured against all 37
installed capabilities: `not obs.gumps` is unconditional in **`_buy_can_yield` too** — it
sits OUTSIDE the `(ui_clear or finished)` disjunction, so the `finished` escape covers
popup and shop windows only, never a gump. `bank_gold` is the one family unconditional on
all four surfaces at a 120-tick deadline, but a bank trip opens a POPUP and a container,
and `_clear_stale_ui` can close only a gump, a vendor BUY and a vendor SELL — so `bank_gold`
can reach the release with nothing for the repair to close. Craft and buy/sell are the only
families whose own trip opens a surface the repair can actually close.)*

#### 7.2 The staging, and what each act is load-bearing for

`anima2/live_frame_overdue_gate.py`, in the shape of `live_tinker_bank_gate.py` (spawn body →
`GmControl.stage` → build the Life → drive `life.tick()` in-process → `print_gate_verdict`),
with the GM connection held open across the drive loop:

1. **A `TinkerLife` with no vendor and no banker staged, and its starter purse deleted** —
   `craft_tongs` is then the only branch of the tinker's rule that can move the mode, so
   taking it away is what makes the hold observable.
2. **`craft_tongs` opens its OWN gump** with one `Use(tool)` — no NPC, no walk, no
   popup→`PopupSelect` handshake (all documented live failure modes; forge16's stale window
   in §6.7 WAS a failed buy trip). Nothing is injected: the surface the repair closes is the
   one the capability itself opened.
3. **The wound: `[Set RawStr 2000` then `[Set Hits 50`.** Raising the ceiling rather than
   lowering the floor is load-bearing and the run proved it — `PlayerMobile.HitsMax` is
   `Str/2 + 50`, so the bar became ~1050 and 50 hits sat at 4.8% against
   `Survive.heal_below_fraction` of 0.40. **HP read 80 at t075 and 70–74 later**: on a
   default 80-point bar that is 100%, the starve would have silently broken, and the run
   would have degraded into an ordinary bound-2 close while still printing a pass. A naive
   `[Set Hits 10` gate is the version of this that reports a reach it does not have.
4. **A teleport off the craft spot** (radius 3 → 8 tiles away), so `decide_mode` answers
   `hunt` while the frame is live and `mode == "economy"` is demonstrably the
   ORCHESTRATOR's doing. The wound does double duty: a wounded character in hunt mode is
   also held by `Survive`, so `Wander` never walks it back onto the spot.
5. **Every tick recorded, not sampled** (`FrameOverdueWatch`). `life_runner` samples every
   4.0 s (~9 ticks) and the `+hold` + `overdue` pairing exists for exactly ONE tick, so a
   sampling runner misses it ~8 times in 9. That is the same instrumentation gap that let
   the §6 runs report four things about themselves that were wrong.

#### 7.3 The verdict, verbatim

```
staged: Pim@(2609,474) iron=20 bandages=50 no vendor, no banker — craft_tongs is the only branch

  t008 admitted=craft_tongs gumps=[2066278152] econ=2 steps=2
  wounding: [Set RawStr 2000 -> HitsMax ~1050, [Set Hits 50
  teleporting off the craft spot: (2617, 474) (radius 3) — the rule now says hunt
  riding the frame to its deadline: budget=300 econ ticks, deadline_tick=300

  t025 econ=27 mode=economy want=None age=27/300 hold=True overdue=False steps=2 gumps=1 hp=51
  t075 econ=77 mode=economy want=None age=77/300 hold=True overdue=False steps=2 gumps=1 hp=80
  t275 econ=277 mode=economy want=None age=277/300 hold=True overdue=False steps=2 gumps=1 hp=51
  ** Pim: closing an unowned gump id=2066278152 — it was refusing every capability **
  t299 ** REPAIR #1 at orch 299 / econ 301 — _repair_overdue_frame closed a surface and the hold is extended one economy tick **
        econ=301 mode=economy want=None age=301/300 hold=True overdue=True steps=2 gumps=1 hp=51
  t300 ** HOLD RELEASED at orch 300 / econ 302 — mode=hunt, frame still live, overdue=True, extension=1 econ tick(s) **
        econ=302 mode=hunt want=None age=302/300 hold=False overdue=True steps=2 gumps=0 hp=51

  final: econ=302 mode=hunt want=None age=302/300 hold=False overdue=True steps=2 gumps=0 hp=51
  moments: overdue=(299, 301) repair=(299, 301) released=(300, 302) extension=1 closes_while_overdue=1 hold_ticks_before_overdue=299 craft_steps_seen=[2]
[FLAG] staged_craft_gump = True
[FLAG] fsm_starved = True
[FLAG] hold_engaged = True
[FLAG] went_overdue = True
[FLAG] repair_closed_surface = True
[FLAG] hold_released = True
[FLAG] not_the_detector = True
[FLAG] FRAME OVERDUE GATE PASSED
```

What that establishes, each clause tied to a field above:

- **`frame_overdue` became True on a shard, at economy tick 301 against `deadline_tick=300`**
  — the `>` comparator, exactly one tick past where `expire_due`'s `>=` would have won if
  the frame could yield. Capability `craft_tongs`, surface the craft FSM's own gump.
- **`_repair_overdue_frame` fired on that same tick**: `closing an unowned gump
  id=2066278152` — the gump `Use(TinkerTools)` opened at t008 — and `gumps` went 1 → 0 in
  the next observation.
- **The repair extended the hold by exactly one economy tick, then the hold released**:
  released at econ 302 = overdue 301 + 1, `closes_while_overdue=1`. Both halves of bound 3
  in consecutive ticks in one run. The extension arithmetic is the numeric signature of the
  repair: with nothing to close, the hold drops on the overdue tick ITSELF (measured offline
  as this gate's release-without-repair control, extension 0, `_stale_ui_closes == 0`).
- **The hold was genuinely the orchestrator's**: `want=None hold=True` for **299 consecutive
  ticks** before the deadline. Bound 3 released a Life the orchestrator would otherwise have
  gone on pinning — which is the whole reason the review had to add it.
- **The starve held**: `cap_craft_steps` was **2 for the entire 300-tick window**
  (`craft_steps_seen=[2]`), so the surface stayed open because the FSM was never stepped,
  not by luck. Without that self-check a passing run would prove nothing.
- **The close is attributable to `_repair_overdue_frame`, not to `_detect_disagreement`'s
  copy of the same repair**: `rule_gate_disagreement` stayed None throughout, and the
  detector's copy requires an EMPTY goal stack while this one requires a live frame.
- **The frame is deliberately NOT retired at the end.** `_craft_can_yield` still refuses on
  `started and not terminal` once the gump is gone, so the run ends with a wounded tinker
  beside a stale frame, `mode=hunt`, hands back. That is §5's documented worst case — *"a
  stale frame, but alive"* — reached live, and it is the correct result, not a failure.

#### 7.4 What this run did NOT establish — the list is longer than the list above

- **Bound 1, the FSM give-up ladder, is STILL unexercised live.** Nothing here touches it;
  §6.3's finding stands unchanged, including its reason (a successful sale closes by
  `CapabilityGoalComplete`'s ACHIEVEMENT branch and the status line cannot name the branch).
  Proving it still needs a transaction that FAILS. This gate deliberately does the opposite
  — it stops the FSM from stepping at all. *(Still true on 2026-08-03 after §8.3. Only the
  REASON's second clause changed: the telemetry can now name the branch. **Observable is not
  exercised** — the count of live-proven bounds is unchanged at two, bound 2 and bound 3.)*
- **The death override is STILL unexercised live.** Nobody died. §5's checklist item 5 is
  open, and it remains the single most valuable run this fix is owed.
- **The `OVERDUE_REPAIRS` cap was not reached.** One close was spent of three. A surface
  that RE-OPENS after the repair — the case the cap exists for — has never been live-tested.
- **The multi-close extension is unproven anywhere but the flag's own predicate.**
  `extension=1` is all that has run; `2 <= extension <= OVERDUE_REPAIRS` has not.
- **`_clear_stale_ui`'s vendor BUY/SELL branches at an overdue frame did not run.** The
  surface here was a gump. Vendor-window closes have fired live before (§6.6), but from
  `_detect_disagreement`, not from this caller.
- **No economy claim.** No gold moved, by construction — no vendor, no banker, purse
  deleted. This is a mechanism proof and nothing else.

#### 7.5 The offline reproduction, and the mutants it kills

`tests/test_live_frame_overdue_gate.py` — **7 tests**, driving the same staging over
`MockBody` through the gate's own `FrameOverdueWatch`, so the judgement the shard published
is one a mutant already died against. Only two things are simulated (MockBody has no
craft-gump server side): the gump, and the wound as a `hits` write.

Mutants applied to `anima2/warrior_life.py`, run, then reverted with the md5 restored to
`c679efbde3fa415ce8ccb8a59d1f0db7` and verified:

| mutant | change | result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| M1 | drop `and (not self.frame_overdue or repaired)` from `holding` — literally the pre-review two-bound version that livelocked | **2 failed**; `released_at` never set, the Life pinned |
| M2 | `repaired = False` — the repair-and-extend removed | **1 failed**; `repair_closed_surface` and `hold_released` both flip False |
| M3 | `frame_overdue` comparator `>` → `>=` | **4 failed**, including the bound-2 control — `went_overdue` goes False because the overdue tick lands ON the deadline instead of one past it |

Three controls make a pass mean something, all measured offline: the **bound-2 near-miss**
(same world, no surface and no starve — the craft FSM aborts at `max_goal_steps=240` inside
its 300-tick budget, `expire_due` closes at econ 300 with outcome `expired`, `frame_overdue`
never True); **release without repair** (starve, no surface — the hold drops on the overdue
tick itself, extension 0, which is why `hold_released` asserts `overdue + 1` rather than
merely "the mode went to hunt"); and the **staging self-check** (no wound — `cap_craft_steps`
climbs and `fsm_starved` goes False even though `went_overdue` is still True).

#### 7.6 Reading it off an ordinary monitored run, when one finally stumbles into bound 3

The gate records every tick; `life_runner` does not. For a sampled run the status line's
`+hold` + `!overdue` pairing is the WRONG primary signal — it exists for one tick and is
missed ~8 times in 9. Use the worker's own unthrottled prints instead:

- **Bound 3 fired at all**: the literal string `FRAME OVERDUE` appears ≥1 time.
  `_run_worker` prints it on `_overdue % _QUIET_TICKS == 0` starting at `_overdue == 0`, so
  the FIRST overdue tick always prints (`village.py:779-784`, `_QUIET_TICKS = 40`).
- **`_repair_overdue_frame` fired**: `** <name>: closing an unowned gump id=<N> — it was
  refusing every capability **` appears IMMEDIATELY BEFORE `FRAME OVERDUE for 1 ticks`, with
  no line between. The ordering is causal — the close prints from inside `life.tick()`, the
  overdue line from `_run_worker` after that tick returns. The caller is pinned by the
  absence of a `RULE-vs-GATE DISAGREEMENT` line, which `_detect_disagreement`'s copy always
  prints alongside its own close.
- **Numeric confirmation of the repair**: `!frozen` first appears at `@302/300` when the
  repair fired and at `@301/300` when it did not, and the age then stops moving, so any
  later sample carries it.
- **Bound 2 fired instead** (the honest near-miss): `admitted=<cap>@<age→budget>` then
  `admitted=None`, `!overdue` never printed, `FRAME OVERDUE` count 0, `goal_stack.history`
  outcome `expired`. This is what §6's forge run produced at 292/300 and 177/180.
- **Regression guard, not a pass condition**: `!frozen` on a live frame with `hp` not `DEAD`
  and WITHOUT `!overdue` on the same field is the hold being defeated, not bound 3.

#### 7.7 Environment note

The shard was reported up at `127.0.0.1:2594` when this work started; it was **not** —
nothing was listening, no ServUO process existed, and the bridge binary was absent. Both
were brought up first (`../servuo/run-kst.sh`; `cargo build -p anima-net`, exit 0,
`target/debug/anima-agent`). Worth recording only because "the shard is up" was an
assumption, and every live claim in this document depends on which shard answered.

## 8. The three findings of §6, answered OFFLINE — and the shard was down (2026-08-03)

§6 and §7 leave three things open, and all three were worked the same day:

1. **The miner that died silently** — follow-up 17, a gap this document had already named on
   2026-07-30 and never acted on, recurring with the same signature.
2. **The vendor BUY window that ate the last 556 ticks** — follow-up 16, whose mechanism §6.7
   deliberately refused to guess at and left as two falsifiable candidates.
3. **Bound 1 of the exit-edge hold**, which §6.3 and §7.4 both record as unexercised — and
   which §6.3 shows no log could even have RECOGNISED had it fired.

**Everything in this section is OFFLINE. No live run happened.** §8.5 says so in the same
detail a passing run would have got, because "it did not happen" is a publishable result and
this session has already had to withdraw four wrong claims about a live run.

State of the tree as this section is written: `HEAD` `d822214`, eleven files modified and
**uncommitted**; `pytest -q` → **1495 passed** (1469 + 26 new); `uvx ruff@0.8.0 check .` and
`uvx ruff check .` both `All checks passed!`. Every number below that is not read off the two
2026-08-03 forge logs was produced by running this tree.

### 8.1 Follow-up 17 — the liveness line, adopted at the SECOND time of asking

**The plainest thing to say about this fix is that it should have existed before the run that
needed it.** The 2026-07-30 post-implementation health check ends: *"Also worth adopting from
the same report: a liveness line for NON-Life agents (the forge miner died silently —
`out+0.0 steps=0` for 72 samples with nothing flagging it)"*. It was not adopted. On
2026-08-03 the same runner produced the same failure for the second recorded time — §6.5,
follow-up 17 — and only then was it built. One sighting was treated as an anecdote; it took
two. That is the process finding, and it outranks the code.

**What the old alarm could not do, measured on the 1800-tick log.** `NO PROGRESS` is
BODY-liveness: reward, steps, speech AND position, so an agent that keeps WALKING resets it
forever. Grimm kept relocating. His ten `NO PROGRESS` pulses land at ticks
`38, 95, 291, 798, 872, 1078, 1325, 1577, 1800, 1800` and every one of them reads the
identical string — `grep -o "Grimm: NO PROGRESS for [0-9]* ticks" | sort | uniq -c` gives
**`10 Grimm: NO PROGRESS for 40 ticks`**. Three of the ten precede the freeze at t=765.
Identical text in the healthy half and the dead half is zero information.

**The signal that works, and what arms it.** `village._work_recorded(agent)` — the agent's own
count of skill outcomes that were terminal OR rewarded, summed over every ledger it owns.
Grimm's death is precisely a skill returning `RUNNING, reward=0` forever, which records
nothing; the counter is monotone, orthogonal to walking, and counts REPEATS rather than
variety, so a miner productively swinging one skill for 3000 ticks bumps it thousands of
times. It is armed by `village._doing_work`, which reads a new pure-telemetry field
`Agent.last_skill_name` (written once per tick after every rejection guard, before
`skill.step`), because **without the gate the alarm is 100% wrong on a healthy agent**: the
default village roster's one `townsfolk` is defined `work_skill=None  # no job — just lives in
town`, so `Wander` is its whole job and it records nothing by design. The review measured that
false positive through `_run_worker` itself — 1000 ticks, alarms at 240/480/720/960, ending
`!stalled  [BUDGET SPENT · STALLED 995]` — and an idle hunter (`Hunt.can_run` false with no
prey, so the planner falls to the same `Wander`) firing twice in 500 ticks. Grimm's dead 1035
ticks read `ph=mine` with his mining window still turning over, so the catch survives the gate.

**240 ticks, measured, not chosen — and reproduced here independently of the implementation.**
Sample cadence in both forge logs is **9 ticks median, 10 max**. Reward-silence gaps for
Grimm, computed from the logs in this session:

| log | healthy gaps ≥ 40 ticks | longest HEALTHY gap | the death gap |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1800-tick | 112, 120, 64 | **120** | **1035** (t=765 → end) |
| 600-tick | 56, 56, 159 | **159** | none — reward still moving at the last sample |

So 240 has **zero false positives on both healthy windows** and is 1.51× the worst healthy
stretch; 160 also scores zero but clears 159 by ONE tick, which is not a margin. Reward-silence
is an upper bound on episode-silence (every reward change implies a record, not conversely), so
those counts are conservative — and t≈1005 for Grimm's death being called is a **PREDICTION off
the reward proxy, not a measurement**: neither log prints an episode count, which is why the
status line now carries one.

**Real output of the new alarm and the new status line**, produced by running this tree over
Grimm's shape (a walker whose ledger stops at t=99):

```
  ** Grimm: NO OUTPUT for 240 ticks (eps=11 unchanged since t=99, skill=mine) — no skill has finished or paid since **
  ** Grimm: NO OUTPUT for 480 ticks (eps=11 unchanged since t=99, skill=mine) — no skill has finished or paid since **
Grimm     miner      @(635,50) t=585 out+0.0 eps=11 steps=585 says=0 !stalled  [BUDGET SPENT · STALLED 486]
```

Three surfaces, deliberately: the escalating alarm (240 → 480 → …, unmistakably unlike ten
identical `40`s), `!stalled` on the ~4s status line while it holds, and the stall folded into
the terminal suffix — because Grimm's terminal line was, verbatim from the log,

```
  Grimm     miner      @(2593,499) t=1800 out+176.9 steps=139 says=0  [BUDGET SPENT]
```

the most misleading possible summary of an agent that had produced nothing for 1035 ticks.

**EVERY agent, Life or not — and the first draft of this alarm got that wrong.** A hunt-only
proxy is not weak for a Life, it is a constant: Pim's `out+0.3` is unchanged on **all 208
samples** of the 1800-tick run — 1789 ticks — while he banked 503g over six deposits, so a
`mode is None` gate excluding Lives was necessary *for that proxy*. But it left
`run_supply_pair` (WoodsmanLife + throttled CarpenterLife) and `run_warrior_village` (all
WarriorLife) with **zero** work-liveness coverage — a Bjorn dying the way Grimm died, on the
runner that feeds the board chain, still silent. `_work_recorded` sums the hunt AND economy
ledgers instead. Re-measured here on a healthy 4000-tick offline `CarpenterLife`: hunt ledger
**0**, economy ledger **234**, and the worst COMBINED silence is **17 ticks** in steady state
(23 counting the opening stretch before the first record) — an order of magnitude under 240.

**Follow-up 17 is only PARTLY discharged, and the remaining half is the one it named first.**
`run_forge_pair`'s status line still prints **no hp and no death flag for either agent**:
`life_runner.hp_readout` exists and returns `DEAD`, the pair line still never calls it
(`village.py`'s forge-pair print block passes `p_obs` to `telemetry_line` and never touches
`hp_readout`). So the tape still cannot say WHETHER GRIMM DIED — a death, a lost pickaxe and a
dead vein remain indistinguishable. What changed is that the tape now says he STOPPED. See
follow-up 18.

**What the proxy still misses, stated because the argument above is not a proof of detection.**
It is orthogonal to WALKING, not to every zero-reward terminal skill. `RecoverDeath`
(`skills/recovery.py`) and `Survive`'s bandage-confirm both return terminal statuses with no
reward, so an agent whose work is dead but which cycles death/resurrection once per 240 ticks
keeps this counter moving and stays silent. That failure shape has never been observed; it is
what this alarm would still miss.

Six new tests in `tests/test_forge_relocation.py`, including the anti-requirement one — a
miner finishing a skill only every **159 ticks** (the measured worst healthy case) for 1000
ticks must produce no alarm and no `!stalled`.

### 8.2 Follow-up 16 — the wedge: fixed offline, and the mechanism named

§6.7 lists two falsifiable candidates for why a re-entering `buy_iron` trip never reaches its
own popup stage and refuses to guess between them. Offline reproduction found **three**
defects, not one, and they compose into exactly the shape the log shows. All three are fixed in
`anima2/skills/market.py`, mirrored to the tool-buy FSM (same machine, different memory
namespace, so it had the same defects and gets the same fixes rather than waiting to be caught
on its own run).

**(a) `buy_offer_reopens` outlived its trip — this is candidate (b), sharpened.** The counter
that decides how fast the partial-subset re-roll gives up was written and read in `_buy_step`
and **nowhere else in the repo**: not in `_CLEANUP_KEYS`, not in `_begin_goal`'s pop list, not
in the live gate's transaction keys. It therefore lived in the economy agent's memory for the
life of the process, and one unlucky trip that exhausted it left **every later trip giving up
on its first `window` tick, forever**. Measured offline on the FSM alone: trip 1 gives up at
tick 7 with the counter at `OFFER_REOPEN_ATTEMPTS`; trips 2 and 3, fresh goal ids and the same
memory, give up on tick 1. Fix: both counters added to `_CLEANUP_KEYS`.

**(b) The "re-roll" re-rolled nothing.** On an unlucky window the FSM flipped its stage back to
`popup` and emitted **no action**. A vendor window is a SNAPSHOT, so the subset cannot change
while it stays open — and `8cdd2f0`'s entry-edge branch at the popup stage then recognises the
still-open window as ours and flips straight back to `window`, re-reading the identical entry
list. Four attempts burnt in about four ticks. Fix: the re-roll emits
`BuyItems(vendor=…, items=[])`, ServUO's EndVendorBuy, so the next opening is a freshly rolled
subset.

**(c) No give-up path closed the window the trip had opened — the EXIT edge.** Seven
`return None` give-ups in `_buy_step` and not one cancelled the window, so the trip walked home
leaving a `ui=shopbuy` that refuses all sixteen capability gates (they share the idle-UI
clause). `8cdd2f0` fixed the ENTRY edge and §6.7 measured it working as designed; it never
touched this one. Fix: `BlacksmithMarket._close_own_vendor_window`, called on the give-up
transition of both buy capabilities.

**The safety argument for (c) is in the helper's own docstring, and it is the argument this
area exists to make.** It is **not a repair**: it is the OWNER cleaning up, emitted from inside
the trip's own FSM on the tick that FSM has itself decided the trip is over, so no other goal's
transaction can be in flight behind it. It closes ONE surface and only if `obs.shop_buy.vendor`
equals the serial **this trip** recorded (read BEFORE `_CLEANUP_KEYS` pops it); a stranger's
window is left alone. It never scans, and never reads `shop_sell`, `gumps` or `pending_target`.
And it is not `WarriorLife._clear_stale_ui` moved earlier — that repair's empty-stack /
fully-overdue preconditions are untouched, because they are the safety property.

**How the three compose into the observed log, and how far the log actually constrains it.**
Re-read from `bcb080hac.output` in this session: the run carries 61 `admitted=buy_iron@`
samples, **60 of them inside the `ui=shopbuy` tail** — the three wedged frames, ages 6→177,
5→176 and 4→176 — and the string `buy_stage=popup` appears **exactly once in the entire
1800-tick log**: the first wedged trip at age 6, with `ui=shopbuy` already up. By age 15 that
same trip is back at `mkt_phase=craft`, and every one of the other 59 wedged samples reads
`mkt_phase=craft` with `buy_stage` absent. Trip 2's FIRST sample is age 5 and trip 3's is age 4,
both already at `mkt_phase=craft`: their entire buy phase fitted inside one ~9-tick sampling
interval. That is what (a) predicts and it is very fast for a healthy trip. **It is
consistency, not proof.** No re-run has confirmed the chain on a shard, the sampling interval
hides the FSM's actual path, and §6.7's candidate (a) — the cancel is emitted but the surface
does not close, server-side or through a stale `shop_buy` — is untouched by any of this and
remains open. What can be said flatly is that (a), (b) and (c) are each REAL defects
independent of the log, each with an offline reproduction, and (c) alone means no trip can now
leave a window behind on any give-up path, including the confirm-success one.

**A regression the review caught before it shipped, worth recording because it is the cost of
this fix.** The two live acceptance gates count `BuyItems` addressed to the vendor and require
`len(buys) == 1`. An empty-list cancel IS a `BuyItems`, so after (b) and (c) a **correct**
purchase — 15 iron at the quoted price, exact gold delta — scored
`transaction_actions_once=False, only_iron_bought=False`. Both gates now filter through a named
`market.is_vendor_cancel` predicate, keep the cancels inside `transaction_indices` (they ARE
capability actions and `no_extra_capability_actions` must keep seeing them), and allow one
popup cycle per window opening up to `1 + OFFER_REOPEN_ATTEMPTS`. Their detail lines gained
`opens=` and `cancels=`. **Neither gate has been run** — they need the shard (§8.5).

Six new tests in `tests/test_market.py`, including the two that hold the safety argument
(`…never_closes_a_window_it_does_not_own`, `…gave_up_with_no_window_open_emits_no_cancel`) and
`test_a_SUCCESSFUL_buy_also_leaves_no_window_behind`. Two pre-existing tests changed, in one
direction only: `assert not isinstance(res.action, BuyItems)` became
`assert not (isinstance(res.action, BuyItems) and res.action.items)` — which preserves the
intent they were written for, "never mis-buys the shield" — and each gained a positive
assertion that the close IS emitted. No test was weakened or deleted.

### 8.3 Bound 1 is now OBSERVABLE. It is still UNEXERCISED.

**These are two different things and this section will not blur them.** §6.3's finding stands
word for word: no run has ever exercised the FSM's give-up ladder in a way any log could
recognise, and proving it still needs a transaction that FAILS on a shard. What changed is
only that a future run CAN recognise it.

The reason it could not is §6.3's: `CapabilityGoalComplete` closes a frame through two
branches, the achievement branch (`profession.py:299`) returning SUCCESS and — reached only
after it — the give-up-ladder branch keyed on `cap_run_finished_goal_id` (`:311`) returning
FAILURE. Every low-age frame in the logs sat beside a successful sale or deposit, a ladderless
`buy_iron` frame closed just as fast at age 4, and the status line showed only that the frame
was gone.

**`life_runner.retirement_reason(frame)` reads the FRAME ALONE**, and nothing new is written to
make it readable. `GoalStack._archive` has stamped `frame.outcome` on every retirement since
the goal stack was written: `SUCCESS → achieved`, `FAILURE → giveup` (bound 1),
`EXPIRED → expired` (bound 2).

**A load-bearing claim was falsified before anything was built on it, and the direction of the
error matters.** The design this started from proposed testing the `cap_run_finished_goal_id`
marker, on the argument that a stale value can only equal an OLDER frame's id and so is sound
at any later read time. It is a single memory slot every later transaction overwrites — and
measured on a 2000-tick offline `CarpenterLife`, **117 of 117 FAILURE closes classify as
`giveup` at retirement time and 116 of the 117 flip to "no ladder ran" when the same history is
re-read at the end.** The error direction is precisely the one that ERASES bound-1 evidence. A
per-tick alarm would have survived it; the ~4s tally would have lied. Reading `frame.outcome`
is what makes the answer independent of WHEN it is read.

Collapsing FAILURE to `giveup` is sound because on a `kind == "capability"` frame only
`CapabilityGoalComplete` can consume the goal, and its other FAILURE path — the `not can_run`
fail-closed guard — is unreachable through the planner, since `Agent._isolated_planner_context`
deep-copies memory and observation so the selecting `can_run` and the stepping `can_run` read
identical content in the same tick.

**Three surfaces, all telemetry.** An unthrottled per-tick edge alarm in `_run_worker` (a
retirement is an EDGE, one per transaction, unlike `frame_overdue`'s LEVEL signal that printed
3,881 identical lines); a `retired=` tally on the ~4s `telemetry_line`; and the
`frame_retirements` projection both of them read. Real output from this tree, on the exact
carpenter give-up `docs/CARPENTER.md` records as offline-only *because the status line could
not name the branch*:

```
  ** Sten: FRAME RETIRED sell_furniture#1 age=17/180 -> giveup (bound 1: the FSM's give-up ladder) **
want=None admitted=None ready=[] retired=1:1g
```

`age=17/180` is the same 17 economy ticks that document already records offline. The line now
NAMES the branch.

**Two review findings on the report itself, both fixed, both about scope and honesty:**

- `frame_retirements` read only `life.econ_agent`, so it returned `()` for the entire
  `run_village --capability-goals` fleet — plain `Agent`s with a real `CapabilityPolicy` whose
  capability frames retire on `agent.goal_stack`. A blacksmith `bank_gold` frame closed through
  the ladder and the report said nothing. It now falls back to the object itself. The reason
  the tests missed it is worth keeping: `tests/test_capabilities.py::_retire_life` wrapped that
  very agent in a `type("_Life", (), {"econ_agent": agent})()` shim — an attribute no production
  code gives it — so three tests exercised a shape the runner never builds. The new test drives
  the unwrapped agent.
- The `retired=` count is a projection of BOUNDED history (`history_limit=128`) and now says
  so. Re-measured here on the 4000-tick offline carpenter: the per-tick edge reader banks
  **58 / 117 / 176 / 234** retirements at t=1000/2000/3000/4000, while history saturates at
  128 from world tick **2182**. Unmarked, the status line would have read `retired=128` on a
  3000-tick run where 176 frames actually retired — a silent 27% under-report of a field
  printed as a count. It now reads `retired>=128:128g` once the cap binds. The edge alarm
  remains the exact per-frame record.

Four new tests in `tests/test_capabilities.py` and six in `tests/test_life_frame_hold.py`,
including a real give-up ladder end to end, a real deadline retirement (bound 2, so the two
cannot be confused), and the read-time-invariance test that kills the marker-based design —
it drives a real `CarpenterLife` for 4000 ticks, asserts the edge reader and the level reader
agree exactly on all 117 frames retired by tick 2000, and then rides past the 128-frame cap to
assert the survivors are a SUFFIX of what the edge reader banked and that the lifetime total
is not readable off the saturated tally.

### 8.4 What this buys the next live run — and what it still does not

- **Bound 1 gets a signature it never had.** `-> giveup (bound 1: the FSM's give-up ladder)`
  on a shard, once, is the evidence §6.3 and §7.4 both say is missing. Nothing here produces
  it; it needs a transaction that FAILS.
- **Bound 2 gets a second, cheaper reading.** `-> expired (bound 2: the frame's own deadline)`
  is the same conclusion §6.3 reached by hand from ages and pack contents.
- **The wedge gets a cheap verdict.** A forge pair that still wedges will now say which of the
  two it is: `ui=shopbuy` persisting WITH `FRAME RETIRED buy_iron#N … -> expired` is the same
  180-tick burn as before; without a stale window it is fixed.
- **A silently dead non-Life agent stops being silent** — but only if its work skill is the
  one that died. See §8.1's stated limits.
- **It does NOT tell you whether an agent is alive.** No hp, no death flag, still, on the pair
  line. Follow-up 18.

### 8.5 The live run that did NOT happen

**The shard at `127.0.0.1:2594` is DOWN.** Four independent probes in this session:
`nc -z -w 3 127.0.0.1 2594` → rc 1; `lsof -nP -iTCP:2594 -sTCP:LISTEN` → no output, rc 1;
`lsof -nP -i:2594` (any state) → nothing; a direct socket connect → `ConnectionRefusedError:
[Errno 61] Connection refused`, three times. `ps aux | grep -iE "servuo|mono|dotnet"` shows no
shard process. Connection *refused* rather than timed out means nothing is bound to the port at
all. Per this project's rule the agent did not start it.

Both live targets hard-code that endpoint and neither has an offline mode —
`village.run_forge_pair(..., port: int = 2594, ...)` and
`live_frame_overdue_gate.py`'s `HOST, PORT = "127.0.0.1", 2594`. The bound-3 gate additionally
needs GM command execution, a teleport and a real craft gump the FSM opens itself; its
docstring is explicit that nothing is injected.

So, stated as flatly as a result:

| question the run would have answered | status |
|---|---|
| Does the work-liveness line fire on a real forge pair, and truly or falsely? | **NO DATA.** Zero live samples. |
| Is the shopbuy wedge gone? `ui=shopbuy` count, full-budget `buy_iron` frames, `NO PROGRESS` pulses | **NO DATA.** |
| Does `FRAME RETIRED … -> giveup` ever print on a shard (bound 1)? | **NO DATA.** Bound 1 remains unexercised. |
| Does bound 3 still pass (`live_frame_overdue_gate.py`)? | **NOT RUN. No verdict string exists.** Neither confirmed nor regressed. |
| Do `live_buy_goal.py` / `live_toolbuy_goal.py` still pass with the new cancel-filtering? | **NOT RUN.** The regression in §8.2 was found by reading and by an offline simulator, and the repaired gates have never executed. |
| Economics — net gold, banked, productivity to the end | **NO DATA.** |

No new defect was found by a live run, because there was no live run.

**One correction to the state this work was handed.** It was described as "working tree clean,
1469 tests pass". `HEAD` matched (`d822214`), but the tree had eleven modified files and the
suite stood at **1495**. The guardrail is met and exceeded — 1495 ≥ 1469, zero failures, both
ruff clean — and the extra 26 tests are the ones this section describes. Recorded because a
stale baseline is exactly how a "clean tree" claim becomes a wrong one.

### 8.6 Deliberately not done — new follow-ups

18. ~~**`hp_readout` on the non-Life half of every pair status line — the OTHER half of
    follow-up 17, still not done.**~~ **DONE 2026-08-05 — see §9.** It was named on
    2026-07-30, again as follow-up 17, and again here; the fourth entry is this one, closing
    it. It landed WIDER than described below and the widening is the finding, so the original
    text is kept: *"`run_forge_pair`'s print block already holds `m_obs = ...` for the miner —
    so it is one `f"hp={hp_readout(m_obs)} "` inside the `grimm[…]` group. Rank it above
    everything else on this list."* One group on one runner is what that buys. §9 puts it in
    `_run_worker`, which all six runners drive, and adds the EDGE reading the pair line was
    structurally unable to carry.
19. **MEASURED 2026-08-07 and no longer blocked — see §17.4.** *(Text kept.)* The
    deferral reason below is "a behaviour change on that path that nobody has measured".
    It is measured: on a live 1200-tick forge run, THREE `buy_iron` frames gave up and
    then burned their full 180-tick deadline each — **540 dead economy ticks, 45% of the
    run** — with the frame admitted, the gate ready and nothing executing on 55 samples.
    `market.py` sets the marker on the SELL (:1681) and BANK (:2072) give-up paths and on
    neither buy path.
    **`cap_run_finished_goal_id` on the buy / tool-buy give-up return branch.** This is
    follow-up 15's shape applied to the two families §8.2 just touched: it would close a
    given-up buy frame through bound 1 in a few ticks instead of burning 180 dead ones, and —
    the reason it is named HERE — **it would give bound 1 its first live exercise on the
    flagship economy path.** Not taken: it is a behaviour change on that path that nobody has
    measured, and §8.3 is deliberately telemetry-only. It is now cheap to validate, because a
    run carrying it prints `FRAME RETIRED buy_iron#N … -> giveup` where today it prints nothing.
20. **CLOSED 2026-08-07 — see §13.** *(Text kept. Measuring it changed the fix: the
    asymmetry was NOT one-sided. `_toolbuy_step`, having no pre-check, got a genuinely
    FRESH window per re-roll, while `_buy_step` re-adopted the one it had just
    cancelled — so mirroring the branch alone would have taken the tool buy's good half
    away. The conditional remedy this entry names at the end is therefore mandatory, not
    optional, and both stages are one method now.)*
    **`8cdd2f0`'s entry-edge branch was never mirrored into `_toolbuy_step`.** The tool-buy
    popup stage still has no already-open-window handling. With §8.2(c) on both paths no trip
    leaves a window behind, so the gap is much narrower than it was — but it is a real
    asymmetry between two copies of the same FSM, and it is unmeasured, so it was not guessed
    at. Related: §8.2(b)'s close costs one re-roll attempt PER CYCLE rather than per trip,
    because the popup pre-check re-adopts a window that has not gone yet; measured on the FSM
    against a simulated shard at 4 / 2 / 1 / 1 fresh openings for observation lags of
    0 / 1 / 2 / 3 ticks. Still bounded, still strictly better than the zero fresh openings it
    replaced. The fix, if one or two ever proves too few, is a marker saying THIS trip just
    cancelled this window so the pre-check refuses to re-adopt it.
21. **The retirement report is scoped to objects with a goal stack of their own or an
    `econ_agent`.** That is every Life and every capability villager after §8.3's fallback, and
    it is not hunt agents. Broadening it would change what `frame_retirements` means; bound 1
    lives in the Lives.
22. **CLOSED 2026-08-09 — see §20.** *(Text kept.)* The deferral below held until two
    live artifacts stood on the other side of it: §8.2's Life-level reproduction ran
    from a scratch harness that no longer exists, and §19's bound-1 gate shipped with
    NO offline reproduction where the bound-3 gate has seven. `MockBody`'s own
    docstring calls it "a test double", so "production code" was always the weaker
    half of the framing.
    **No `MockBody` vendor.** The Life-level reproduction of the wedge needed one, and it was
    run from a scratch harness instead. Adding a vendor to `MockBody` is a production-code
    change for a test's benefit; the wedge is covered by FSM-level tests plus that harness.
    Named so the next person who wants a Life-level buy test knows why there isn't one.

## 9. Follow-up 18 — whether the agent DIED, answered at the fourth time of asking (2026-08-05)

**The process finding first, because it is the same one §8.1 recorded and it has now
repeated.** This document named a work-liveness/hp line for non-Life agents on 2026-07-30,
did not act, and paid for it with a second silent miner on 2026-08-03. §8.1 built the
"did it stop" half and explicitly wrote down that the "is it alive" half was still missing,
ranking it *"above everything else on this list"*. It sat for two more days. The entry that
closes it is the fourth. Nothing about the work was hard — the ratio of times-named to
lines-of-production-code is 4 : ~30.

### 9.1 What the tape could not say

§8.1's own summary: *"the tape now says the miner STOPPED and still cannot say whether he
DIED"*. A death, a lost pickaxe and a dead vein all produce the same evidence — `out+176.9`
frozen, `eps=45` frozen, position still moving because the miner keeps relocating. The three
have completely different fixes and the log could not separate them.

### 9.2 Two readings, because the obvious one alone is useless here

- **`hp=` is a LEVEL** and it decays. It answers "is this agent a corpse right now" on a
  ~4s sample. A death the agent was resurrected from before the next sample leaves no trace
  in it at all.
- **`deaths=N` is an EDGE count read per TICK**, so it survives the sampling gap the same way
  §8.3's retirement report does.

The second is not a hypothetical refinement of the first. §8.1 named its own known hole:
*"an agent whose work is dead but which cycles death/resurrection once per 240 ticks keeps
this counter moving and stays silent"* — because `RecoverDeath` and `Survive`'s
bandage-confirm return terminal statuses with no reward, which `_work_recorded` counts. An
agent in exactly that state prints a healthy `hp=` on every sample and a moving `eps=`. Only
the edge count sees it.

Measured, two runs of the same frozen-miner shape through the real `_run_worker`, differing
only in whether deaths were staged. Every other field on the line is identical:

```
Grimm     miner      @(395,50) t=345 hp=80/80 deaths=0 out+0.0 eps=11 steps=345 says=0 !stalled  [BUDGET SPENT · STALLED 246]
Grimm     miner      @(395,50) t=345 hp=80/80 deaths=2 out+0.0 eps=11 steps=345 says=0 !stalled  [BUDGET SPENT · STALLED 246]
```

and the second run's alarms, which the sampled line above cannot show:

```
  ** Grimm: DIED at (150,50) — death #1 **
  ** Grimm: BACK ALIVE at (180,50) after 30 ticks dead (death #1) **
  ** Grimm: DIED at (250,50) — death #2 **
  ** Grimm: BACK ALIVE at (264,50) after 14 ticks dead (death #2) **
```

Both runs end `hp=80/80`. The level signal alone would have reported nothing.

### 9.3 Where it landed, and why not where the follow-up said

Follow-up 18 specified `f"hp={hp_readout(m_obs)} "` inside `run_forge_pair`'s `grimm[…]`
group. That is one group on one runner, and it cannot carry an edge — the runner's status
loop samples every 4.0 s and a death edge is one tick wide.

It is in **`village._run_worker`** instead, which every runner drives: the forge pair, the
supply pair, the warrior village, the artisan+mage pipeline, `run_village`, and
`LifeRunner.run`. Every one of them already prints this worker's `status[idx]` snapshot
directly beneath its own aggregate line, so the reading lands at the same sample and in the
same place on screen that the follow-up asked for — for six runners rather than one. The
worker also already holds the tick's own observation (`obs`, the one `p = obs.player.pos`
is read from), so the level costs no extra pump.

The one divergent copy is gone with it: `_pipeline_line` re-derived
`"DEAD" if p.dead else f"{p.hits}/{p.hits_max}"` inline for the mage, which was the only hp
on any village status line and was unreachable from anywhere else. It calls `hp_readout` now
— and the ARTISAN standing beside it in the same string, the half of that pipeline that
earns the gold, had no hp at all and now has one.

`deaths=` prints at 0, unlike `!stalled` beside it. An absent field is ambiguous — no
deaths, or a build that cannot count them — and the entire value of the field is making a
frozen `eps=` beside `deaths=0` a different diagnosis from the same frozen `eps=` beside
`deaths=3`.

### 9.4 The obvious implementation is wrong, and it was measured rather than reasoned about

`Agent.tick` has maintained `memory["death_episode"]` — a monotone count of alive→dead edges
— since the recovery work, and nothing reads it for telemetry. Reading it is the one-line
version of this change.

It does not work for a **Life**, which owns two `Agent`s with separate memories and ticks
exactly one per orchestrator tick, each keeping its own `death_observed_dead` flag. Both
reductions were run on a real `CarpenterLife` over `MockBody`:

| staged | hunt marker | econ marker | `sum` | `max` |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ONE death, seen by the economy agent, then by the hunt agent under the death override | 1 | 1 | **2** ✗ | 1 ✓ |
| TWO deaths, each seen by one agent only | 1 | 1 | 2 ✓ | **1** ✗ |

A sum double-counts and a max under-counts, so neither is a death counter. One body has one
death; the one worker watching that body counts it once. This is `_work_recorded`'s lesson
— a Life's telemetry cannot be read off one of its two ledgers — reaching the OPPOSITE
conclusion, because episodes genuinely add across the two agents and deaths do not.

Both reductions are pinned as mutants: implemented against this tree, each fails **4 of the
18** tests in `tests/test_forge_relocation.py`, and each fails on the assertion written for
it — the `sum` mutant prints `deaths=2` where the one-death fixture demands `deaths=1`, the
`max` mutant prints `deaths=1` where the two-death fixture demands `deaths=2`. Both fixtures
are one worker run over a real `CarpenterLife`, so neither mutant can be rescued by summing
across workers instead.

### 9.5 What this does NOT establish

- **Never run on a shard.** `127.0.0.1:2594` is still down (`nc -z -w 3` → rc 1), the same as
  §8.5. Every line above is offline output.
- **No forge log has ever contained a death**, so the false-positive question this line
  raises has no live evidence either way. The alarm cannot fire on a healthy agent by
  construction (it reads `obs.player.dead`, not a heuristic), which is the one thing that
  distinguishes it from §8.1's alarm and its townsfolk false-fire.
- **It does not say whether `hp=DEAD` will ever appear on a live sample.** Ghost stretches
  may be shorter than the 4.0 s sampling interval, which is exactly why `deaths=` is read
  per tick — but that is a prediction, not a measurement.
- **It says nothing about WHY the agent died**, and nothing about §8.1's remaining hole
  in the other direction: an agent whose work is dead for a reason that is not death still
  needs `NO OUTPUT` to catch it.

5 new tests in `tests/test_forge_relocation.py` (1500 total, +5), ruff clean.

### 9.6 Follow-ups 19-22 are untouched

None of them was addressed here. 19 (a give-up marker on the buy FSM, which would give
bound 1 its first live exercise) remains the highest-value one and remains a behaviour
change on the flagship economy path with no live budget to validate it, which is why it
is still not taken.

## 10. Follow-up 2 — the other five construction sites, wired (2026-08-05)

Precondition (a) of CLAUDE.md's "Two roadmaps, one decision" is *"the genome's axes can
steer a full Life"*. On 2026-08-02 two of seven Life-construction sites could be steered;
follow-up 2 named the other five and singled out `run_forge_pair` as *"the closest
candidate — and it is also the one that matters most: the flagship miner→tinker chain, the
positive-margin loop a fitness run would actually measure"*. All five are wired now.

### 10.1 What was actually missing, and it was not the argument

Adding `knobs=` to four runner signatures is the easy part and it is not what follow-up 2
warned about. Its stated hazard was *"threading a dict through hand-written construction
per runner (no single point that stays true)"* — five independent copies of a check whose
whole value is that everyone performs it identically. So the change is three shared things
and four thin call sites:

- **`life_runner.validate_knobs(knobs, knob_names, *, label)`** — the check and its refusal
  text, lifted out of `LifeSpec.__post_init__`, which now delegates. One definition of
  "is this a knob" for the spec path and the inline path both.
- **`life_runner.build_tuned_life(life_cls, knobs, /, **kwargs)`** — `LifeRunner.build_life`
  for the runners that have no spec, and it exists for the reason that one's docstring
  already gives: those runners need a shard, so without a named seam the only way to prove
  a tuned value reaches the Life is to re-implement the construction line inside a test,
  which is the exact shape of an asserted-but-not-wired channel. It reads `life_cls.KNOBS`
  off the class, so unlike `LifeSpec` (whose factory is a lambda and must be TOLD its
  allowlist via `knob_names`) no site can declare the wrong one.
- **`village._route_knobs`** — the CLI's role routing, replacing the blanket
  `--knob needs --carpenter or --woodsman` guard, which was an allowlist of the two wired
  runners rather than a property.

### 10.2 The placement is the part a shared helper cannot choose for you

`LifeSpec` checks at spec construction and gets "before the first packet" for free. An
inline runner builds its Lives only after the logins, the GM staging, the provenance
gold-wipe and the seed grant — so each one calls `validate_knobs` at the TOP of the
function, above `ResilientIpcBody.spawn`, and `build_tuned_life` checks again at the point
of use. Same check, run twice, deliberately.

**Mutation-tested, and the mutant is worse than expected.** Removing `run_forge_pair`'s
pre-flight call — leaving only `build_tuned_life`'s check, i.e. checking at construction —
does not merely move a traceback later. The runner's login loop catches per-role
exceptions, so the measured output of a one-character typo becomes:

```
raising the forge pair at 127.0.0.1:2594
  animaforgem947000 (miner): login failed (...)
  animaforget947000 (tinker): login failed (...)
the pair needs both; aborting
```

Two spawned accounts, and a message blaming the shard for a typo in an argument. The test
that catches this asserts on the FIRST network call exploding, so a check that drifts later
fails loudly rather than silently.

### 10.3 The CLI, with the ambiguity a multi-Life runner has

`--knob [ROLE:]KEY=VALUE`. Real output from this tree:

```
$ python -m anima2.village --forge-pair --knob bank_trip_surplus=90
  -> run_forge_pair({'knobs': {'bank_trip_surplus': 90}})

$ python -m anima2.village --supply-pair --knob bank_reserve=400
  ! --knob bank_reserve=400 is ambiguous on --supply-pair, which builds 2 Lives (woodsman, carpenter). Say which: --knob woodsman:bank_reserve=400

$ python -m anima2.village --supply-pair --knob woodsman:bank_reserve=1 --knob carpenter:bank_reserve=2
  -> run_supply_pair({'woodsman_knobs': {'bank_reserve': 1}, 'carpenter_knobs': {'bank_reserve': 2}})

$ python -m anima2.village --supply-pair --knob tinker:bank_reserve=400
  ! --knob tinker:bank_reserve=400: --supply-pair has no 'tinker'. Roles here: woodsman, carpenter

$ python -m anima2.village --miners 1 --knob bank_reserve=400
  ! --knob does not apply to the trade-village roster: it builds no Life, and every tuning knob is a Life threshold. Ignoring it silently would misreport what the run actually ran.
```

The bare form stays optional where there is one Life — requiring the prefix everywhere
would break every shipped `--carpenter --knob K=V` invocation for no gain. `--supply-pair`
refuses it because both its Lives have a `bank_reserve` and picking one silently is the
same misreporting the roster refusal exists to stop.

### 10.4 Two smaller things the wiring forced, both of them single-source repairs

- **`tinker_life.bank_trip_surplus(memory)`** now exists. It was a bare `knob_int(...)`
  call inside `decide_mode` while `decide_mode` was the only reader; `run_forge_pair`'s
  staged banner is the second, and the choice was a named read point or a second copy of
  the clamp. `knobs.py`'s docstring is about what the second copy costs.
- **`run_supply_pair` prints its reserves at all.** It printed none — defensible while
  nothing could tune it, and not now. Both are read off the BUILT Lives through
  `market._bank_reserve`, the same shape as `LifeRunner.staged_line`, so the banner cannot
  drift from what the Life will actually do. `run_forge_pair`'s banner needed NO change to
  survive becoming tunable, which is the payoff of its own comment's bet that reading the
  built Life "stays true the day something does".

### 10.5 What this does NOT establish — and it is the half §E is actually about

- **The searcher end is untouched, and it is a design question, not wiring.**
  `Genome`'s four axes still map onto no knob. Three of them cannot: `profession` is
  identity and the allowlist REFUSES it by design (see `WarriorLife.KNOBS` for what it
  cost when it did not), `sociability` is a `Persona` field, `cognition_tier` builds an
  LLM client at construction. Only `deliver_threshold` is threshold-shaped, and it is a
  `MineSmeltDeliver` parameter rather than a Life knob. A genome→Life bridge has to decide
  what the other three mean for a Life before any of this can be searched.
- **`foundry/eval.py::_build_agent` builds a bare `Agent`, never a Life.** The fitness
  harness has never measured a Life at all, so "gold per life" is not currently something
  `run_eval` computes for anything with an orchestrator.
- **Still only four knobs exist.** Wiring seven sites does not add an axis; it means an
  axis that exists can now reach any of them.
- **Nothing live.** `127.0.0.1:2594` is down (`nc -z -w 3` → rc 1), as in §8.5 and §9.5.
  The five new sites have never carried a value on a shard, and **no tuned knob has ever
  changed a live trajectory on any site** — which is §E's criterion word for word, and the
  reason precondition (a) is still not met.
- **Offline coverage is real but bounded**: `build_tuned_life` is tested against all five
  Life classes with both knob shapes, and the pre-flight placement is tested for all four
  inline runners. What no offline test can reach is the runners' own post-construction
  staging, exactly as `LifeRunner.run()` is not tested and for the same reason.

1500 → **1506 tests**, ruff clean. Follow-up 2 is closed; 3, 4 and 19-22 are untouched.

## 11. Follow-up 4 — the knob that rode a second channel, and §E's first real axis (2026-08-05)

Follow-up 4, verbatim: *"`wander_leash` is the cheapest remaining axis (§E's 'exploration
radius') and needs no Life work — but it arrives through `Staged.leash`, a DIFFERENT
channel, and `skills/movement.py::Wander._homeward` clamps it its own way (a malformed
value falls back to the class default rather than to a floor). Unifying the two is small;
noting the divergence matters more."*

`knobs.py` carried the carve-out in its own opening paragraph — a single-source module
whose docstring had to name the thing that was not single-source.

### 11.1 Which way the unification went, and why the other way was wrong

Two rules were in play for a present-but-malformed write:

| | `wander_leash=-1` | `=0` | `=1` | `=2` |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| class-default fallback (before) | **8** | 0 | 1 | 2 |
| floor clamp (now, `LEASH_FLOOR=1`) | 1 | 1 | 1 | 2 |

The fallback is **discontinuous**, and that is disqualifying for a knob whose entire
purpose is to be SEARCHED: a mutation stepping one below the floor leaps the behaviour to
the shipped default instead of resting on the boundary. Every other knob is monotone at
its floor, and a monotone boundary is what makes an axis searchable at all. So `_homeward`
now reads `skills/movement.py::wander_leash(memory, default)` — the key's one read point,
delegating to `knob_int`, in `market._bank_reserve`'s shape.

**The floor is 1, not `knob_int`'s natural 0, for `disagreement_ticks`' reason:** 0 is
catastrophic for THIS threshold. The only tile inside a 0-leash is `wander_home` itself,
so a leashed agent steers home on every tick it is not standing on it and never wanders —
the skill disabled by a malformed write rather than tuned by it.

**One stated behaviour change:** a stored `0` used to be honoured as a zero-tile leash and
now clamps to 1. No caller passes 0 — every production and gate call site passes >= 1.

### 11.2 The axis, and the precedence question it is the only knob to have

`wander_leash` is now in `WarriorLife.KNOBS`, so all five Lives take it and all seven
construction sites and all six Life-bearing runners carry it (§10). It travels as a MEMORY
KEY rather than an instance attribute, because `Wander` is a skill reading `ctx.memory` and
an attribute would be invisible to the only thing that uses it — the `bank_reserve` shape,
written raw and clamped by its reader.

**It is the only knob something else already writes**, and that makes it the only one with
a precedence question. Every runner calls `set_leash(home, derived)` AFTER construction:
`LifeRunner.run` with `staged.leash`, `run_supply_pair` with
`min(max(1, shop_reach), PICKUP_RADIUS - 1)` (live-caught, after Sten drifted three tiles
off his own supply drop and walked into a wall with `fetch_boards` correctly admitted the
whole time). Left alone, that later write would silently overwrite the tuned value — **a
channel that reports success and changes nothing, which is the exact defect §10 and the
2026-08-02 entry have been closing, wearing a knob that looks wired.**

So the TUNED value wins and `_leash_tuned` records the difference between "nobody set this"
and "a caller chose it". The derived value keeps exactly its old authority when untuned,
which matters because it is a live-caught fix. Mutation-tested: restoring the
unconditional write fails 2 tests.

The `home` is written unconditionally in both cases. It is not a knob and has no contest.

### 11.3 Three banners that would have started lying

A tuned leash is invisible unless the runner reports the EFFECTIVE value, and this is the
third time in three days that a banner has had to be moved off a local or a module
constant onto a read of the built Life:

- `LifeRunner.staged_line` gains `leash N` beside `reserve N`. It needs the read MORE than
  the reserve does, because with two writers neither input alone tells an operator what the
  Life will wander under.
- `run_supply_pair` printed its local `sten_leash`; it now prints what is in force and says
  `TUNED from a derived N` when they differ.
- `run_forge_pair`'s banner gains it alongside the reserve and the trip surplus.

### 11.4 What this does NOT establish

- **Offline only.** `127.0.0.1:2594` is down; no leash knob has been carried on a shard.
- **It is an axis nothing searches.** Same as §10.5: `Genome` still maps onto no knob, and
  `wander_leash` being §E's named "exploration radius" does not by itself connect the two —
  `Genome` has no leash field, and adding one is the genome→Life bridge that §10.5 says is
  a design question.
- **Untested live, and it CAN break a chain.** A leash above `PICKUP_RADIUS - 1` puts a
  carpenter out of reach of his own deliveries. That is correct behaviour for a searched
  axis — a bad configuration should score badly, not be refused — but it means the first
  live sweep over this axis should expect chain failures and not read them as defects.
- **The 240-tick work-liveness alarm is what would catch such a failure**, and it has one
  live sighting (§8.1's 1200-tick run). It has never been exercised against a leash-induced
  stall specifically.

1506 → **1510 tests**, ruff clean. Follow-up 4 is closed; 3, 3b, 5, 6, 7 and 19-22 remain.

## 12. Follow-up 6 — three constants defined twice, merged (2026-08-07)

Follow-up 6 called these *"the audit's own headline defect class, in constant form"*, and
the framing is right: the concordance lattice catches a rule and a gate disagreeing about
a VALUE, and structurally cannot catch them disagreeing about a DEFINITION, because a
constant written twice is numerically locked until somebody edits one copy — and the edit
IS the failure.

### 12.1 The three, and they are not the same kind of problem

| pair | both sides read | could they diverge numerically? |
|---|---|---|
| `warrior_life.UPGRADE_RESERVE` ↔ `capabilities._UPGRADE_RESERVE` | `BuyArmor.tool_price_estimate` | **No** — same expression |
| `carpenter_life.BOARDS_PER_ITEM` ↔ `capabilities._FETCH_BOARDS_THRESHOLD` | `CarpenterCraft.craft_material_per_item` | **No** — same expression |
| `skills/survival.BANDAGE_GRAPHICS` ↔ `skills/warrior.BANDAGE_GRAPHIC` | a frozenset, and **the literal `0x0E21` typed again** | **Yes** |

The first two are one DECISION recorded twice — `_UPGRADE_RESERVE`'s comment in
`capabilities.py` was near word-for-word the one in `warrior_life.py`, and the fetch gate's
comment said it read the class attribute *"so it stays in lockstep with the craft gate that
consumes it"*, which is a property no comment can hold. Both now live in the skills layer,
which is the only place both readers can import from: `capabilities` importing a Life would
be a cycle, since the Lives import `CapabilityPolicy`.

### 12.2 The third pair had already produced a latent standoff

`BANDAGE_GRAPHIC = 0x0E21` sat three lines below `from .survival import BANDAGE_GRAPHICS`,
which is `frozenset({0x0E21})`. And the two were not decoration — they were **read by
opposite sides of the same decision**: `WarriorLife.decide` counted the SINGLE art while
`buy_bandage`'s gate counts `BuyBandage.buy_material_graphics`, the whole FAMILY.

Measured on one observation carrying 3 of the known art and 20 of a hypothetical second,
with the family grown to include it:

```
reorder line            : 10
pre-merge RULE counts   :  3  -> wants buy_bandage? True
GATE counts             : 23  -> admits buy_bandage?  False
merged RULE counts      : 23  -> wants buy_bandage? False
```

A permanent want-vs-refuse standoff, one added graphic away, in the exact shape the
concordance suite was built for — and invisible to that suite, because with a singleton
family the two sides agree at every lattice point. The rule reads the family now, so
the disagreement is not merely absent but unrepresentable: both names are bound to the
same frozenset object.

`BANDAGE_GRAPHIC` still exists, because a vendor OFFER is placed against one art and not a
family — a real distinction, not duplication. It is `min(BANDAGE_GRAPHICS)`, not
`next(iter(...))`: a frozenset has no order, so the iterator form would pick a different
graphic between runs the moment the family grew, and a buy offer that changes identity
between runs is the worst available failure here.

### 12.3 The tests are identity assertions, and they are mutation-checked

Equality would pass again the moment a second definition happened to compute the same
number, which is precisely the state being removed. So the three tests assert `is`, assert
the deleted private names are `hasattr`-absent, and — for the bandage pair — walk the AST
of `skills/warrior.py` to reject a bare literal, because `BANDAGE_GRAPHIC = 0x0E21`
satisfies an `in BANDAGE_GRAPHICS` check just as well as the derived form does.

Both mutants were implemented and both fail exactly one test: restating the bandage literal,
and reintroducing `capabilities._UPGRADE_RESERVE`.

### 12.4 What this does NOT establish

- **Offline only, and nothing behavioural changed.** Every number is identical today; the
  bandage family is still a singleton, so the rule's new family count returns what the old
  single-art count returned on every real observation. There is nothing for a live run to
  confirm and no live evidence is claimed.
- **The standoff above is a CONSTRUCTED scenario, not an observed one.** No shard has ever
  had a second bandage art. What the measurement shows is that the split was load-bearing,
  not that it had already fired.
- **Follow-up 5 and 7 are untouched and are not this.** ~20 shared cost/threshold constants
  still cannot become knobs mechanically (each is captured into a closure at import time
  inside a frozen binding in a `MappingProxyType` registry that declares itself an authority
  boundary), and §E's retreat/rest thresholds still cannot while the manifest validator
  forbids per-instance survival state. Both are authority-boundary decisions, not merges.
- **No sweep was done for a FOURTH pair.** Follow-up 6 named three; three were merged.
  Whether others exist is unmeasured — the AST guard added here is bound to the one name
  that was a literal, not a general "no constant is restated" check.

1510 → **1513 tests**, ruff clean. Follow-up 6 is closed; 3, 3b, 5, 7 and 19-22 remain.

## 13. Follow-up 20 — the asymmetry, and why mirroring it would have been a regression (2026-08-07)

Follow-up 20: *"`8cdd2f0`'s entry-edge branch was never mirrored into `_toolbuy_step`. The
tool-buy popup stage still has no already-open-window handling ... a real asymmetry between
two copies of the same FSM, and it is unmeasured, so it was not guessed at."*

It was right to say unmeasured. Measuring it changed the fix.

### 13.1 The asymmetry was not one-sided

`_buy_step` had the entry-edge branch and `_toolbuy_step` did not, so the obvious reading is
"one FSM is missing a fix". The re-roll path says otherwise. After its own cancel,
`_toolbuy_step` had nothing to re-adopt the window with, so the popup stage simply waited and
re-requested — **each re-roll got a genuinely FRESH window**. `_buy_step`, with the branch,
re-adopts the window it just cancelled while the observation lag lasts, re-reads an identical
snapshot (a window IS a snapshot) and spends an attempt on it. That is the 4/2/1/1
fresh-openings-by-lag table already recorded in its own re-roll comment.

So each FSM had one half. Mirroring the branch alone would have taken the tool buy's good
half away — which is why follow-up 20's own conditional remedy (*"the fix, if one or two ever
proves too few, is a marker saying THIS trip just cancelled this window so the pre-check
refuses to re-adopt it"*) is not optional here. It is what stops the mirror being a
regression.

Measured by driving the REAL `_buy_step` / `_toolbuy_step` against a simulated shard whose
window never carries the item, counting FRESH window openings per trip (1 initial + up to
`OFFER_REOPEN_ATTEMPTS`=4 re-rolls, so 5 is the whole budget):

| lag (ticks) | 0 | 1 | 2 | 3 | 5 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| entry-edge branch, no marker | 3 | 3 | 2 | 2 | 1 |
| shipped (branch + marker) | **5** | **5** | **5** | **5** | **5** |

Identical on both FSMs, which is the point. The lag costs ticks now (19/19/23/27/35 for the
same rows) and no attempts.

### 13.2 One method, not two copies

The stage is now `BlacksmithMarket._buy_popup_stage(ctx, ns, vendor_serial, reward)`, called
by both FSMs with their own memory namespace, returning `_ADOPT_WINDOW`, `None` (give the
trip up) or a `SkillResult`. Copying the branch would have produced a third thing to keep in
step; two copies of one FSM with one fix applied to one of them is this project's headline
defect class, and follow-up 20 is that class with the roles reversed from §12's.

### 13.3 An unbounded loop, found by moving one line

The `POPUP_TIMEOUT` count now runs ABOVE the window branch. It used to run below, and
`_buy_step`'s foreign-window branch returned its cancel before reaching it — so **a foreign
window that never closed produced a cancel every tick forever**, bounded only by the frame
deadline outside this FSM and never by the stage timeout that exists to bound it. Whether it
ever fired live is unknown: a stale window that outlives a full `POPUP_TIMEOUT` is exactly the
forge15-18 shape, but no log distinguishes "cancelled repeatedly" from "waiting". It is
bounded now, and the foreign window is cancelled ONCE and then waited on rather than
re-cancelled every tick.

### 13.4 Mutants

Both were implemented against this tree:

- **Mirror the branch WITHOUT the marker** — literally what follow-up 20 proposed. Fails 4
  tests, including the re-roll budget on BOTH namespaces.
- **Revert `_toolbuy_step` to its own popup stage** (the pre-merge asymmetry). Fails 3 tests,
  all on the `toolbuy` parametrization and none on `buy` — which is the asymmetry itself,
  visible as a test signature.

`{ns}_closing_window` went into `_CLEANUP_KEYS` in the same change that introduced it, with a
test pinning it, because `buy_offer_reopens` is in that tuple only because it was NOT and one
unlucky trip poisoned every later trip for the life of the process (§8.2).

### 13.5 The trade-off the marker buys, found by reviewing the change afterwards

`{ns}_popup_total` is cumulative across a trip (deliberately — it bounds the TOTAL time in
this stage across every re-request cycle) and it now counts the ticks spent waiting for a
cancel to land, which it did not before: those ticks used to be spent inside the `window`
stage, because the pre-check adopted the window instead of waiting. So the stage's own
timeout binds sooner. Swept against the same simulated shard:

| lag (ticks) | 1 | 2 | 3 | 5 | 8 | 10 | 12 | 15 | 20 | 30 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| pre-change `_buy_step` (branch, no marker) | 3 | 2 | 2 | 1 | 1 | 1 | 1 | 1 | 1 | 1 |
| shipped | 5 | 5 | 5 | 5 | 5 | 5 | 4 | 3 | 3 | 2 |

At lag ≥ 12 the trip no longer spends its whole re-roll budget, and at ≥ 15 it ends on
`POPUP_TIMEOUT` rather than on the budget. **It is still better than what it replaced at
every lag measured**, so this is a smaller ideal, not a regression — and the realistic band
at `pump_ms=400` is 1-3 ticks (a 15-tick lag is six seconds for a window to close on a
loopback shard). Recorded because a future reader measuring only the shipped column at lag
15 would reasonably think something was broken.

Not "fixed" by exempting the wait from the counter: that exemption is exactly the unbounded
loop §13.3 removes.

### 13.6 What this does NOT establish

- **Offline only.** The shard is down; neither FSM has run a tuned or untuned trip on a
  shard since this change. The lag table is a simulation, not a live measurement — its lag
  parameter stands in for a real `pump_ms=400` round trip that nobody has instrumented.
- **The entry edge has still never been observed firing on the TOOL path.** `buy_tinker_tool`
  is a rare capability (a tool is bought when one is lost), so the window it would adopt is
  one some OTHER trip left behind. §8.2(c) made every trip close its own window on the way
  out, so the residual case is a trip that never got to run its give-up path at all — a frame
  expiring mid-trip, or a mode switch. That is a real shape (bound 2 fires; §6.3) but it has
  not been caught with a tool buy on the other side.
- **`OFFER_REOPEN_ATTEMPTS=4` is unchanged and unvalidated.** The marker means all four
  attempts now buy four distinct snapshots; whether four is the right number for the
  ~15-of-45-entry pairing bug is still the estimate it always was.
- **Nothing here touches follow-up 19**, the give-up marker that would let a buy frame retire
  through bound 1 instead of burning its deadline. Bound 1 remains unexercised live.

1513 → **1521 tests**, ruff clean. Follow-up 20 is closed; 3, 3b, 5, 7, 19, 21 and 22 remain.

## 14. Reviewing the week's own work — a knob I added that could not bind (2026-08-07)

Five commits of hot-path change landed offline with no live budget to validate any of them,
so the next step was to read them rather than write more. Three findings, all in this
session's own work, and the third is the one worth the entry.

### 14.1 The `POPUP_TIMEOUT` tightening (§13.5)

Recorded there. Measured, bounded, never worse than what it replaced, and now written down
so a future reader measuring only the shipped column at an unrealistic lag does not
misdiagnose it.

### 14.2 The sixth runner still had no readout

`run_artisan_mage_village` printed neither the mage's reserve nor its leash. It is the last
of the six Life-bearing runners, and the only one that could still carry a tuned value an
operator could not see.

### 14.3 A knob on every Life that provably cannot bind on one runner

`wander_leash` went into `WarriorLife.KNOBS` (§11), and `KNOBS` is INHERITED — so all five
Lives, all seven construction sites and all six runners accept it. **`run_warrior_village`
never calls `set_leash`**, deliberately: a warrior should roam while hunting. And
`Wander._homeward` returns `None` on a missing `wander_home` *before it ever reads the
leash*. Measured:

```
leash reads as    : 3
with a home       : steers home? True
NO home (warriors): steers home? False
```

So a `--warriors 5 --knob wander_leash=3` run accepts the knob, stores it, reads it back as
3, and steers nothing. That is a channel reporting a success that cannot happen — the exact
defect §10, §11 and §13 are all instances of — **introduced by this session's own fix for
it**, three days after the fix, through the one property nobody checked: that adding a knob
to a base class adds it to runners that cannot use it.

The repair is `skills/movement.py::leash_readout`, the single read of what the leash will
ACTUALLY do rather than what it is set to: `3` when a home exists, `3 (inert: no
wander_home)` when not. Every banner uses it. `wander_home` became a named read at the same
time, so `_homeward`'s shape check and the readout's cannot drift — a 3-tuple, a string or
a `(True, False)` is a malformed write and not a home, on both sides, by construction.

**Not "fixed" by giving warriors a home.** The absence is the design; the banner was the
thing that was lying.

### 14.4 Five occurrences, so the class is now a test

`tests/test_knobs.py::test_every_life_bearing_runner_reports_what_it_will_actually_run_under`
walks the AST of `village.py` and asserts every inline runner calls one of the four clamped
read points, and that the two `LifeSpec` runners still reach `LifeRunner.staged_line`, which
is asserted to read both knobs it prints. The count that justifies it: `run_carpenter_life`'s
baked `BANK_RESERVE` (the reason `staged_line` reads the built Life at all), `run_supply_pair`
(printed no reserve), `run_forge_pair` (needed the leash and the trip surplus),
`run_artisan_mage_village` (printed neither), `run_warrior_village` (printed nothing tunable
at all). Mutation-checked: reverting the warrior banner fails it.

It asserts READING, not printing, because a printed module constant is accurate exactly
until somebody tunes that runner — which is the moment the number starts to matter.

### 14.5 What the review did NOT find, and what it could not

- **No correctness defect in the merged buy popup stage, the death telemetry, the constant
  merges or the knob routing** — beyond §13.5's trade-off. That is a claim about a reading,
  not a proof.
- **All 100 modules import clean**, including the `live_*` gate scripts, which have no test
  coverage and which this work touched through `set_leash`, the buy FSM and four constants.
  All five gates construct their Lives untuned, so their explicit `set_leash(..., 2)` still
  wins exactly as before.
- **Nothing here is live.** The shard is down. Every finding above is from reading and from
  offline measurement, and the whole week's work remains unvalidated on a shard.

1521 → **1523 tests**, ruff clean.

## 15. An independent review of §§9-14, and the five defects it found (2026-08-07)

§14 was a SELF-review and found three things. An independent pass over the same range
(`1d3381c..HEAD`, high effort) found five more, all real, all reproduced before being
fixed. That ratio is the entry's point: reading your own diff is worth doing and is not a
substitute.

### 15.1 The five

**1. `wander_leash` is clamped downward only, and it overrides leashes that are
correctness constraints rather than preferences.** `run_forge_pair` and `run_supply_pair`
both derive `min(max(1, shop_reach), PICKUP_RADIUS - 1)` so the agent stays inside pickup
range of its own ground drop — the live-caught defect Sten's leash exists to prevent. §11
made a tuned value outrank that, so `--knob wander_leash=40` lets Pim wander 40 tiles from
the forge and the flagship chain stops closing with no line saying why. §11.4 recorded the
hazard and called it correct-for-a-searched-axis; what it missed is that it is SILENT.

*Not fixed with a ceiling.* An "exploration radius" that cannot explore upward is not an
axis. `leash_readout(memory, life)` now names the override:
`40 (TUNED over a derived 5 — beyond the range this runner computed for reaching its own
drop; deliveries may go unfetched)`. `set_leash` remembers what it yielded to so the
banner can say it.

**2. `set_leash`'s new precedence applied to every call, not just the staging one.**
`live_frame_overdue_gate.py` teleports the tinker and re-leashes it to the new spot, with
a stated invariant — *"if `Survive` ever let go, `Wander` would walk the tinker back onto
its craft spot and the rule would want the economy again on its OWN account, silently
un-engaging the hold"*. Against a tuned Life that re-leash became a no-op and the gate
could report a bogus pass. Latent, not live: nothing tunes that gate today.

Fixed by splitting what the precedence is FOR. A runner's staging call passes a value
DERIVED from the world as a default and yields to tuning; a later call is a runtime
decision and wins. `_leash_tuned` is consumed by the first explicit leash.

**3. The foreign-window cancel was sent exactly once and never retried.** §13.3 replaced
an unbounded every-tick re-send with one cancel and a wait. A cancel is a packet and a
packet can be dropped — and on the give-up path `_close_own_vendor_window` *deliberately*
refuses a window belonging to another vendor, so nothing else would close it either. The
trip would end on `POPUP_TIMEOUT` leaving open the exact surface that refuses all sixteen
capability gates, which is forge15-18's own failure. The old code was unbounded but
self-healing; the new one was bounded but not. It is now both: re-sent on `_popup_click`'s
own `ASK_RETRY` cadence.

**4. The per-trip counters survive a frame torn down MID-trip.** `_CLEANUP_KEYS` is popped
when a trip ends NORMALLY — the step function returns `None` and the phase switches to the
walk home. A frame that expires on its deadline never reaches that line, and bound 2 makes
that a MEASURED shape rather than a hypothetical (§6.3 recorded `buy_iron` frames closing
exactly that way on a shard). So a spent `offer_reopens` made the next trip give up on its
first `window` tick, and a stale `closing_window` made its popup stage wait out
`POPUP_TIMEOUT` for a window nobody was closing. `buy_offer_reopens` had this exposure
already; §13's key inherited it.

Fixed in `_begin_goal` for the two buy families, **narrowly**: the three per-trip counters
only. The first attempt popped all of `_CLEANUP_KEYS` for all four families, which is more
correct in principle and broke six bank tests — those fixtures seed mid-FSM state without
`cap_bank_goal_id`, a state production cannot reach, so they were arguably wrong. It was
reverted anyway: clearing the STAGE keys is a wider behaviour change on the flagship path
that nothing offline can validate, and this project's own standard for that (follow-ups
3b and 19) is to name it rather than guess. Follow-up 23.

**5. `_route_knobs` silently dropped a duplicated knob.** `--knob bank_reserve=1 --knob
carpenter:bank_reserve=2` are different parser keys landing on the same role and knob;
last one won and the other vanished. The function was refusing the ambiguous and the
unknown while quietly dropping the duplicated — the one failure its own docstring says it
exists to prevent. Now a `SystemExit` naming both values.

### 15.2 All five are mutation-checked

Each fix was reverted in turn against the full suite:

| reverted | tests failing |
|---|---|
| the duplicate-knob refusal | 1 |
| the staging-only leash precedence | 1 |
| the tuned-over-derived readout | 1 |
| the cancel re-send | 2 |
| the per-trip counter clear | 1 |

### 15.3 What the review confirmed clean
