Going in, the explanation I most expected was: ServUO ore banks are 8x8 tiles (§27.1), so
if `find_mine_spots` packs its stands closer than 8 apart, several "different stands" sit
inside ONE bank and relocating between them is a walk to the same empty pool. It would
have explained the freeze AND why raising `MINE_POOL_SPOTS` 6 -> 12 did nothing (§25).

It is wrong, and the check is four lines:

```
spots returned            : 12
distinct stand banks      : 12
min pairwise spacing      : 8   (exactly the bank width — `find_mine_spots(spacing=8)`)
```

Reproduced independently three times and then by me. The spacing parameter does exactly
what its docstring claims. **That part of the system was already right**, and §25's lesson
— a confident diagnosis refuted by its own evidence — came within one commit of repeating.

### 34.2 What was actually wrong: three defects, one shape

**(1) `Mine` never cycled its node list. Not rarely — never.**

`harvest_idx` advanced only on `NODE_DEPLETED_CLILOC = 500493`, and 500493 is
LUMBERJACKING's `NoResourcesMessage` (`Lumberjacking.cs`). Mining's is 503040
(`Mine.no_resource_clilocs`). The two ids never meet, so a miner standing on a surveyed
stand targeted `nodes[0]`, and only ever `nodes[0]`, no matter how many times the shard
said there was no metal there.

The cost, measured off the real map at the forge seed:

| | banks |
|---|---|
| distinct banks the 12 stands' node lists span | **14** |
| distinct banks `nodes[0]` alone can ever reach | **12** |

4 of the 12 stands straddle a bank boundary, and the second bank — a full 10-34 ore, one
tile further along — was unreachable by construction. And the worse case is not the
arithmetic: a stand whose first node happened to be *untargetable* ("You can't mine that",
LOS-blocked, out of range) was a **total loss** with up to 14 good tiles beside it, because
every one of those verdicts is a failure the window counts and none of them moved the index.

The rule is DERIVED now (`node_exhausted_clilocs` = the union of the sets each subclass
already declares), so the two sides cannot drift apart again — this is the audit's headline
defect class in its purest form, one fact spelled twice in two different vocabularies.
`pack_full_clilocs` is deliberately excluded: a full pack is a fact about US, not the tile.

**(2) A blind relocation kept aiming at the ground it had just condemned.**

Three paths leave `_relocate_step`. The surveyed-pool arrival installs the new spot's rock;
the stall-giveup drops the old list, with a comment giving the reason — *the old nodes
belong to the ground just condemned*. The third, a blind `RELOCATE_OFFSETS` arrival taken
once the pool is spent, did NEITHER: it left the old list installed, so `_current_node` went
on naming rock 12 tiles behind us and every swing answered **"That is too far away"** —
which is a FAILURE verdict, so the window filled, so it relocated again, forever, mining
nothing. Note that fix (1) does not rescue this: cycling walks the index through nodes that
are ALL too far.

This is the only one of the three with a positive live fingerprint. Both pool-6 runs
recorded "pool stands actually stood on: 6 of 6" — pool exhausted, which is precisely this
path's trigger — and a final tile of `(2604,499)`, not one of the surveyed stands.

Guarded on out-of-REACH rather than dropped unconditionally: a woodsman's exact tree nodes
cannot be re-probed blind, so dropping those would trade one wedge for another. A mutant
using `any()` instead of `all()` is killed by a test.

**(3) `nodes[0]` was the far corner of the reach box, and relocation arrives one tile short
on purpose.**

`find_mine_spots` built its node list in raster order, so `nodes[0]` sat at chebyshev
**exactly 2** for all 12 stands — the corner. And `_relocate_step` deliberately accepts
arrival ONE tile short of a surveyed stand, because a wall-side stand is exactly where the
greedy mover gets deflected on its last step (forge12). Corner + one tile short = chebyshev
3, past ServUO's `MaxRange = 2`. **The first swing of the relocation the pool exists for
answered "too far away."** Nearest-first now: every `nodes[0]` at chebyshev 1, verified to
keep all 12 stand banks and all 14 node banks.

### 34.3 The instrument that could not have told us any of this

`win=23/24` collapses five distinguishable verdicts — no metal / can't mine that / can't
see that / too far away / pack full — into a single `1`. That collapse is why five audit
sections (§8.1, §17, §22.4, §25, §27.3) could describe the dead tail without once telling
an exhausted bank apart from a tile the miner could not hit.

Two counters now, both cumulative, both telemetry-only:

- `nores=N inval=N packfull=N` — a **partition**, one tick charged once. (The `break` that
  makes it a partition survived its first mutant: the test only put one cliloc per tick. A
  tick really can carry two, and a tally would then report more failures than were ever
  recorded.) ~~*"so the counts sum to the window's own `1`s and a reader can check the
  arithmetic"*~~ — **struck 2026-08-12, it was false**: the causes are cumulative over the
  run while `win=` is a rolling window that `_start_relocate` empties at every hop, and
  ordinary deque eviction at `maxlen` breaks the identity a second way. Two tests asserted
  it and passed only because five samples never fill a window of twenty-four. See §35.
- `banks=N` — distinct 8x8 cells the shard gave a **verdict** about. *(Was "actually swung
  at", which counted aiming; corrected in §35 — by §27 this number IS a mining day's output
  ceiling, so overstating it is the one direction that misleads.)*

### 34.4 What this does NOT settle

- **Which of the three the live dead tail actually was.** No live journal exists in-repo,
  so the causal story is unattributed; all three are verified defects reachable in the
  code, and that is a different claim. The cause split exists to collect exactly this and
  rides the next ordinary forge day rather than asking for one.
- **The predicted gain.** 12 -> 14 banks is nominal map arithmetic, not a measured yield,
  and the invalid-target rescue has no measured frequency at all.
- Whether the staged miner is on map 1 (Trammel, `ConsumedPerHarvest = 1`) or 2 (Felucca,
  which halves every bank); whether `TargetGround`'s z survives the bridge intact (a z
  mismatch would be a further source of 500237/501862); whether the shard was restarted
  between the 2026-08-10 runs. None is recorded anywhere.

**Write the prediction down before the next forge day:** `nores=` should dominate `inval=`
on healthy early stands. If `inval=` dominates in the dead tail instead, then follow-up
28's premise — "more stands reached = more ore" — is false and the lever is geometry, not
give-up speed.

### 34.5 Follow-up 28 is CLOSED, and the answer is "do not"

The question was whether the 24-sample dead-vein window can be shortened. It cannot, and
this is the most valuable negative result of the pass.

Measured off the shard's own map, for all 12 pool stands: invalid tiles per probe ring run
**9/24 to 20/24**, longest contiguous invalid run **3 to 13**, and the per-stand
false-fire floor — the shortest window that would NOT abandon a healthy stand — comes out
`4.0, 4.0, 5.3, 5.3, 6.7, 8.0, 9.3, 10.7, 12.0, 12.0, 12.0, **17.3**`. **Any k <= 17
false-fires somewhere in the pool.** With the ring at 24 and `stuck_window_rotations` an
integer multiple of it, 24 is already Mine's floor.

Two supporting claims were also destroyed, and both had been mine:

- **`recov=none peak=24` is far weaker evidence than three days of it reads.** The counter
  is biased toward "none" (a tick carrying both a productive and a stuck cliloc loses the
  recovery, since `stuck_this_tick` wins the tie), and **`peak=24` is the mechanism's own
  ceiling, not a survived streak** — relocation fires the moment the deque fills, so 24
  testifies to nothing above 24. Rule of three on ~27 stand-trials bounds per-stand
  recovery probability at only **~11%**, not the ~0 the histogram suggests.
- **"A failure-then-success at a fixed tile is impossible by construction" is false.**
  `HarvestBank.Consume` arms `m_NextRespawn` only inside `if (m_Current == m_Maximum)`, and
  the banks are in-memory server state that persists across runs (§2 recorded a restart
  resetting them). The forge miner's home bank is the same bank every run, so a bank
  drained in run N carries an armed respawn into run N+1 and CAN refill mid-visit. The true
  statement is the narrower "impossible within a visit that opened the bank full."

An early give-up on consecutive no-metal replies was also proposed and refuted: because
`harvest_idx` never advanced, 503040 proved only that `nodes[0]`'s bank was empty, never
the stand's — so a 3-sample give-up would have abandoned a full bank one tile away on 5 of
12 stands. Fix (1) removes the premise: a stand is now naturally bounded at <= `len(nodes)`
no-metal samples, with no new constant.

### 34.6 Follow-up 29: the age-8 signature is arithmetic, and the fix is refuted

`sell_tongs` retiring at age exactly 8, 203 times in one day, is
**`BlacksmithMarket.stall_limit` (6) + 2** — reproduced across three independently built
mock worlds by varying only that constant: `3->5, 4->6, 5->7, 6->8, 8->10, 12->14`. The
trip's greedy walk is refused for `stall_limit` consecutive ticks, gives up, finds its
return leg free because the agent is standing exactly on `bs_stand`, and is closed on the
8th. `sell_stage` is never written because it is only written after `_walk_route` returns
`_ARRIVED`. The signature is unique: every other `return None` in `_sell_step` writes
`sell_stage` first, and the `find_vendor` timeout costs 11, not 8.

**The proposed fix — anchoring `bs_stand` to `craft_spot` — was measured by both skeptics,
in two separately built worlds, to turn the 8-tick livelock into a 14-tick livelock** with
zero gold banked, whenever the anchor is not walk-reachable from the drift tile. That case
is live-precedented (`warrior_life.py`: a carpenter that drifted three tiles off its supply
drop onto ground it could not walk back from). Not shipped.

Two things worth recording that the investigation turned up on the way:

- **The repo contradicts itself about the load-bearing tile.** `profession.py` says every
  direction from `(2609,474)` except due east is "walled rock, confirmed by probing all 8
  directions live"; `village.py` says `MAGE_DROP = (2609,476)` is "greedy-reachable from
  BOTH stands (probed)", which from `(2609,474)` runs due south. A land-tile dump settles
  neither — `(2609,475)` is ordinary land at the same z as the stand, not mountain — but it
  does establish that the "walled rock" cannot be LAND rock in 3 of the 4 directions.
  Statics and tile flags are invisible to `land_cells`, so this needs a live 8-direction
  `Walk` probe: minutes, not a forge day.
- **`run_forge_pair` discards the curated `TRADE_HUB` waypoint on both legs** — it passes
  only `VENDOR_SPOT[-1]` to `stage_shops`, and `life_runner` then overwrites the route with
  the single tile the mobile is standing on. In a corridor whose own docstring says a
  target off the east-west line is unreachable in one greedy leg, that is a plausible real
  fix. Unproven; recorded as follow-up 31 rather than shipped on a hunch.

### 34.7 Follow-up 26: the dead write is real, and the obvious fix is dangerous

Confirmed by grep, three times over: `_dry_until` has exactly three production sites —
two writes (`buy_dry_until`, `toolbuy_dry_until`) and ONE read, and all four `vendor_dry(`
call sites take the default `ns="buy"`. **`toolbuy_dry_until` is written and never read.**

But the one-line fix `not vendor_dry(memory, ns="toolbuy")` **passes the entire 1555-test
suite and is wrong**: with the marker set, an unarmed warrior's `decide_mode` flips from
`("economy", "buy_weapon")` to `("hunt", None)` — 180 ticks of bare-handed hunting because
a *discretionary* tool trip found a vendor dry. All six tool buys share one hardcoded
namespace. And keying the namespace on `(vendor_spot_key, offer_graphic)` reinstates the
same hazard by a different route: `UpgradeWeapon` inherits both fields from `BuyWeapon`, so
an upgrade trip's dry reading would suppress the life-critical re-arm.

Also learned, and it is the reason this stays unshipped rather than half-shipped: **the
concordance suite is vacuous on this axis** — instrumentation counted 3316 `vendor_dry`
calls across the suite with **zero** `*_dry_until` keys ever present in any fixture. The
suite currently compares the rule with itself. Any dry clause must arrive together with
fixtures that plant the marker, or the tests give false comfort. Follow-up 33.

### 34.8 Follow-ups opened

30. **Attribute the miner's dead tail live.** Needs one ordinary forge day, no special
    setup, and shares that day with everything else here. Prediction written in §34.4.
31. **`run_forge_pair` discards the `TRADE_HUB` waypoint** (§34.6). Plausible fix for
    follow-up 29; unproven.
32. **The sell wedge needs position on the status line** — `pos=`, `stand=`, `route=`,
    `d=`. No per-tick coordinate is printed anywhere today, which is why a 203-give-up day
    was undiagnosable. Not shipped this pass.
33. **`toolbuy_dry_until` per-class namespace, reads on non-life-critical branches ONLY**,
    and only alongside concordance fixtures that plant the marker (§34.7).
34. **A live `MockVendor` sell exchange.** The mock models only the BUY side, so there is
    still no positive control for a working sale offline (sibling of follow-up 22).

1555 -> **1572 tests**, ruff clean. Follow-up 28 is closed as "do not"; 3, 3b, 5, 7, 21,
26, 29 remain, joined by 30-34.

## 35. The review of §34's own commit — four defects, two of them mine, in code one hour old (2026-08-12)

§34 shipped three fixes to the flagship chain's supply side and none of them had run live.
The same adversarial method was pointed at that commit. It found four defects. **Two are
regressions the commit itself introduced, and in both cases the commit's own comment states
the correct rule immediately above code that breaks it** — which is worth recording as a
pattern, not just as two bugs: prose that reasons its way to a rule is not the same thing as
code that follows it, and the reviewer who catches the gap has to read them against each
other rather than in sequence.

### 35.1 The stall-giveup half was never fixed, and the comment said it was

The §34 fix dropped condemned nodes on the relocation ARRIVAL branch, and its comment read:
*"The stall-giveup branch below already dropped them."* That branch's drop is guarded on
`harvest_pending_nodes`, which only a POOL hop sets. So on a **blind** hop — the exact path
the fix existed for, the one taken once the surveyed pool is spent — a relocation that
stalls short kept the condemned stand's rock, and the "too far away forever" wedge survived
on the stalling half. Reproduced independently twice.

Both exits call one helper now. The reason lives in one place because having it in one of
two branches is what the whole defect was.

### 35.2 The fix destroyed a woodsman's grove, in the case its own comment named

The §34 guard was `nodes_are_all_out_of_reach`, chosen — and explained in a comment — so
that *"a woodsman's exact tree nodes are not re-probeable; dropping those would trade one
wedge for another."* The guard cannot spare them:

```
min chebyshev over RELOCATE_OFFSETS : 9
Mine / Chop reach (probe ring)      : 2
```

**Unconditionally true after any blind hop.** And `Chop` cannot re-probe: trees are statics,
the probe branch emits `TargetGround(graphic=0)` at the player's z, and nothing in
`harvest.py` re-seeds `harvest_nodes` — the Control plane writes it once, at staging. So for
a woodsman the drop is not a fallback, it is the end of the trade, and unlike the stale-node
state it replaced it is **unrecoverable** (stale nodes come back when the leash walks him
into reach; dropped ones never do).

Reachable, not theoretical: `Chop` sets `pack_full_clilocs`, so a woodsman whose pack fills
mid-grove fills a window of failures and relocates. **Unmeasured: whether a live woodsman
ever sustains that** — no woodsman day has been run with this instrument.

It is a class fact now, `nodes_are_reprobeable`, read by both exits: TRUE for terrain
(`Mine`, `Fish` — a probe ring finds ore and water on its own), FALSE for `Chop`.

### 35.3 A telemetry invariant I asserted three times, false in two independent ways

§34 said of the cause split: *"the counts sum to the window's own `1`s and a reader can
check the arithmetic."* The counters are cumulative over the run; `win=` is a rolling window
that `_start_relocate` empties at every hop. Ordinary deque eviction at `maxlen` breaks it
again, independently. **Two tests asserted the identity and passed only because five samples
never fill a window of twenty-four** — a test can pin a false invariant if the fixture never
reaches the case that refutes it, which is the same shape as the `stuck_window_rotations`
lesson in §27.4.

The claim existed in three places (`harvest.py`, `village.py`, this document). All three are
corrected, because fixing one copy of a fact stated three times is the defect class this
audit is named after. A test now pins the DIVERGENCE.

### 35.4 `banks=` credited aiming rather than a verdict

§34 credited a bank when a node in it was TARGETED. But "You can't mine that" and "That is
too far away" prove only that the miner failed to *address* that bank — and `banks=` is
published, in two places, as a mining day's OUTPUT CEILING, so inflating it is the one
direction that misleads. A skeptic measured the general form of this on a real pool stand:
60 probe swings all answering 501862, true cells holding ore in reach = 1.

Credit now lands on a verdict ABOUT the bank — productive, or "there is no metal here"
(an empty bank is still a bank reached, and that is the number §27 is about). A mutant
crediting only productive replies is killed, and so is one crediting at target-emission.

### 35.5 What the review got wrong, recorded so the method stays honest

- **The `set` in agent memory was a false alarm** — the thing I most expected to be a real
  bug. `harvest_banks_touched` is never serialized; nothing JSON-encodes agent memory.
- **A proposed `banks=` rework was itself refuted by a skeptic** (it credited probe swings,
  4x-inflating the count in a state producing nothing) — the reviewer's fix was worse than
  the defect it named, while the defect was real. Both halves matter.
- **A proposed `far=` bucket splitting 500446 out of `inval=` is DEFERRED**, for a reason
  worth naming: §34.4 pre-registers a prediction phrased in terms of `nores=` vs `inval=`,
  and redefining `inval=` before that forge day would quietly invalidate a
  written-down-beforehand prediction. It lands after the day, or with the prediction restated.

### 35.6 Still open from this pass

- **The MockVendor SELL exchange** (follow-up 34) was built and verified offline by three
  agents — a real `TinkerLife` retiring a `sell_tongs` frame `achieved`, 1568 -> 1583 tests,
  10/10 mutants killed. NOT applied here, because the artifact lives outside this tree and
  an unreviewed 15-test import is not something to take on trust at the end of a pass.
- **Follow-up 31's `TRADE_HUB` waypoint is FROZEN, and now for a measured reason.** `routes`
  values are indexed `v[0]` in THREE places, two of them `shop_reach` derivations that would
  then measure to the waypoint instead of the shop — halving Pim's leash in exactly the
  configuration follow-up 31 exists to fix. The full suite passes under the naive patch.
  Also refuted: "exactly one tile wedges" (two do, and opening a third makes it vanish).
- **Follow-up 33's dry-axis probe SHIPPED the same day** (test-only). The measurement that
  justified it, run here rather than taken on report — teach the tool-buy GATE to read a
  marker its rules do not read, which is the standoff this file exists to catch:

  | | result |
  |---|---|
  | gate mutant, dry axis disabled | **1578 passed** |
  | gate mutant, dry axis enabled | **4 failed**, 1574 passed |

  Both production keys are planted, including `toolbuy_dry_until` (written, read nowhere),
  so a future reader's disagreement fails on the day it is written instead of on a shard.
  The liveness half is an EQUALITY against `_DRY_CHANGES_A_DECISION`, for the same reason
  `_NOT_DRIVEN_BY_THE_LIFE` is one. Shipped alongside it: `VENDOR_DRY_BACKOFF` pinned to a
  buy frame's own deadline off the registry — all 11 buy bindings are 180 while `bank_gold`
  is 120, so it is a derivation and not a house style. **The dry READ itself stays unwired**;
  the one-liner disarms a warrior for 180 ticks, and the suite can now see that.
- **Follow-up 32's telemetry needs eight corrections** before it lands, including a field
  name that collides with the miner's existing `walk=` and an unsnapshotted dict iteration
  that would collapse the whole status line under a concurrent mutation.

1572 -> **1578 tests**, ruff clean. Seven mutants, all killed — two of them being the code
§34 shipped, verbatim.

## 36. Follow-up 32 shipped — and its own premise was the first thing refuted (2026-08-13)

The follow-up read: *"The sell wedge needs position on the status line — `pos=`, `stand=`,
`route=`, `d=`. No per-tick coordinate is printed anywhere today, which is why a 203-give-up
day was undiagnosable."*

**The second sentence is false, and it had been false for six weeks.** `git log -S` on
`village._run_worker`'s status line returns `6f279a7` (2026-06-30): `@(x,y)` has been on every
agent's line, on every runner, since the working village shipped. It was on all 208 samples of
the day §30.2 is about. (An early draft of the fix credited `61768a1`/2026-07-30 — the commit
that last *touched* the line, which is what a search for its current form finds. Corrected in
review; the wrong date would have understated how long the gap stood.)

So the gap was never the coordinate. It is that a coordinate has **nothing to compare against**:
where the walk is *trying* to get to, and how close it has to be. Those two facts turn `@(2609,474)`
from a number into a diagnosis, and neither was printed anywhere.

### 36.1 What shipped

`market.walk_readout` — a read-time projection, **zero new memory keys** — rendering
`trip=sell to=(2611,473) d=4>2 stall=3/6`, mounted on `_run_worker`'s per-agent line beside the
`@(x,y)` it is the counterpart to. Full field semantics in `docs/MONITORING.md`.

Three design commitments, each one a defect that was proposed and rejected first:

1. **`route[leg]`, not `route[-1]`.** `_walk_route` reach-tests the FINAL waypoint first and
   then steps toward `route[leg]`, at reach 0 on intermediate legs and `final_reach` on the
   last. A readout aimed at the final waypoint reports the wrong tile *and* the wrong
   threshold. This is not theoretical: `profession.VENDOR_SPOT` is `[TRADE_HUB, (2610, 473)]`,
   a two-leg route through a walled corridor, installed verbatim into `run_village`'s trade
   blacksmith, while `stage_shops` produces single-waypoint routes. Both are live, and
   follow-up 31 proposes restoring the hub waypoint on the forge pair — i.e. on the chain this
   instrument exists to diagnose.
2. **The comparator is on the line.** `d=4>2`, not `d=4`. The `!overdue` rule: a field that
   needs a reader to hold two numbers and subtract is a field nobody reads.
3. **No state renders blank.** `trip=none` / `trip=craft` / `to=?` / `trip=?` — the `deaths=`
   rule, that an absent field is ambiguous between "nothing happened" and "a build that could
   not compute one".

Also fixed, because it is a defect in the SHIPPED code and not merely a hazard for the new
field: the forge pair's stage-key group was `" ".join(f"{k}={m[k]}" for k in stage_keys if k in m)`
against **live** agent memory, read from the main thread while the worker thread ticks that same
agent and the market FSMs pop `_CLEANUP_KEYS` mid-trip. The filter and the subscript are
separated by a generator resumption. Those status loops have no `except` and their workers are
daemons, so the `KeyError` ends the run rather than the line. Now `village.stage_key_readout`,
which snapshots — one place, with a test that fails without it.

### 36.2 The offline reproduction §31.4 said was out of reach

§31.4 named the one remaining hypothesis it could not test: *"a blocked approach the mock cannot
model (a real NPC occupying a tile, or terrain)"*. `MockBody.blocked` **is** that, and the trip
dies before the vendor is ever addressed — so no `MockVendor` was needed, which is why this was
reachable while follow-up 22's work was not.

Driving a real `CarpenterLife` into a walled approach reproduces the live arithmetic exactly:

```
retirements: (1,'sell_furniture',8,180,'giveup') (2,…,8,180,'giveup') (3,…,8,180,'giveup') …
line       : trip=sell to=(10,10) d=5>2 stall=0/6 → 1/6 → 2/6 → 3/6 → 4/6 → 5/6, position frozen
```

**Age 8 against a 180-tick budget, repeating** — §30.2's *"203 sell_tongs give-ups, EVERY ONE
at age 8"*, offline, through production code. It confirms §34.6's arithmetic derivation of the
age-8 signature empirically, and adds that a blocked approach is *sufficient* to produce it.

**It does NOT establish that the live cause was a blocked tile.** That still needs a forge day.

### 36.3 The review found twelve, and the three that mattered were mine

The implemented diff went through the same adversarial method: five review lenses, every finding
independently refuted by a skeptic. **12 confirmed, 7 refuted.** Three were load-bearing:

- **The instrument was mounted where it could not see its own justification.** The first version
  rode `life_runner.telemetry_line`, which requires a Life (`life.econ_agent`). `run_village`'s
  trade blacksmith — the *only* production agent carrying the multi-waypoint route that the
  `route[leg]` design exists for — is a plain `Agent`. The design's headline correction and the
  configuration it could observe were disjoint. Moved to `_run_worker`.
- **The concurrency test pinned nothing.** It handed `walk_readout` a `dict()` copy *made inside
  the test*, and `walk_readout` reads only through `Mapping.get`, so it has no check-then-get to
  race. Reverting **either** production snapshot left the suite fully green. Worse, the skeptic
  measured `dict(d)` at 568,178 copies under 4 mutator threads with 0 errors: a threads-and-sleeps
  test of this **cannot fail**, which is the §35.3 defect wearing the clothes of a proof. Replaced
  with a `Mapping` that pops the key its own `__contains__` was just asked about — the race made
  deterministic, and it kills the unsnapshotted mutant every time.
- **An unguarded `econ.memory` in the hot line.** `_run_worker` composes its status line outside
  any `except`. `tests/test_forge_relocation` already ships an `econ_agent` stand-in with no
  `memory`, and the first version raised `AttributeError` on it — from a daemon worker's parent
  thread, i.e. ending the run. Now `village.agent_walk_readout`, guarded, rendering `trip=?`.

Two docstring claims were also false and are corrected in place: the commit attribution above,
and `stall=-`, which was documented as meaning "arrived, or just abandoned". The arrival half is
wrong — `_walk_route` returns `_ARRIVED` from its final-reach test without touching the counter,
so an ordinary arrival renders `stall=0/6`. A reader who took `stall=-` for "not walking" would
have read a wedge as an idle agent, which is the exact inversion the field exists to prevent.

### 36.4 Two mutants that survived, and why they are worth recording

The first mutation pass reported nine of nine killed. **It was measuring nothing.** Two anchors
(`tx, ty = route[leg]`, `reach = final_reach if last_leg else 0`) appear *twice* in `market.py` —
once in `_walk_route` and once, byte-identical, in the readout that deliberately mirrors it — and
a first-occurrence replace mutated the production walk instead. A third anchor matched the new
docstring, which quotes the rule verbatim. Scoping the mutation to the function body *past its
docstring* turned three "kills" into survivors. **Mirroring code makes text-level mutation
ambiguous; a mutation harness that cannot say which copy it hit is not evidence.**

Of the genuine survivors, two are worth naming because both are §35.3 fixtures:

- **`reversed(route[:-1])` on a two-waypoint route is a no-op**, so the obvious return-leg
  fixture pinned the reversal while passing. Needs three waypoints.
- **The `<=` arrival boundary is *equivalent* on a single-waypoint route** — the leg branch
  renders an identical string — so `<=` → `<` survived. Only a multi-waypoint fixture at exactly
  `final_reach` can see it, and that is the one distance where the answer changes.

Final: **21 mutants over `walk_readout`, `stage_key_readout`, `agent_walk_readout` and the worker
line, all killed.**

### 36.5 The prediction, written down before the run

On the next ordinary forge day, on the tinker's line:

- `trip=` should spend the large majority of samples in `craft` (the idle phase between trips),
  with `sell` / `bank` / `buy` appearing in bursts.
- On a HEALTHY trip, `d=` should **fall** across consecutive samples and end `d=<n><=2`, with
  `stall=` at or near 0.
- If the §30.2 wedge recurs, the signature is `d=` **frozen above its reach** while `stall=`
  climbs to `5/6`, `to=` naming a tile the agent never gets closer to, and `sell_stage` absent —
  and `FRAME RETIRED sell_tongs#n age=8/180 -> giveup` beside it.
- **If instead `d=` falls to within reach and the frame still gives up at age 8, the walk is not
  the cause and §31.1's relocation of the failure into `_walk_route` is wrong.** That is the
  outcome that would refute the reading this instrument was built on, and it is the reason for
  writing this down first.

### 36.6 What this does NOT settle

- **Which cause the live dead trips had.** Follow-up 30's forge day, unchanged.
- **`trip=` has never printed on a shard.** Everything above is offline.
- **The miner's own walk is still unreported.** `walk_readout` covers the four MARKET families;
  `MineSmeltDeliver._walk_toward` and the relocation hop maintain their own stall counters and
  are not on this line. A miner renders `trip=none` all day, which is honest and uninformative.
- **The 203-give-up day itself cannot be re-read.** No per-tick log survives, so the new field
  can only help the NEXT occurrence.

### 36.7 Follow-ups opened

35. **`NO PROGRESS` is structurally blind to a wedged walk — the alarm that should have caught
    this day, and could not.** `village.py`'s freeze pulse is
    `(reward, steps, says, p.x, p.y)`, and `steps += isinstance(action, Walk)` counts **emitted**
    walks. `_market_walk_toward` emits a `Walk` every tick while wedged, so `steps` climbs, the
    pulse differs, `_quiet` resets, and an alarm whose text reads *"reward/steps/speech/position
    all frozen"* can never fire during exactly this failure. That is why the day was silent on
    **both** instruments rather than one. NOT fixed here: it is a behaviour change to a
    threshold-tuned alarm and belongs in its own commit with its own measurement — shipping it
    beside a telemetry addition is the "fix the fix" shape §35 is about.
36. **A miner/woodsman equivalent of `trip=`** (§36.6). The harvest walks have the same
    diagnostic gap and their own stall counters; nothing reads them.

1591 -> **1600 tests**, ruff clean. 21 mutants, all killed.

## 37. Follow-up 35 — the fix I would have shipped moved a measured threshold (2026-08-13)

§36.7 named the defect correctly and named the wrong fix. The defect: `NO PROGRESS` cannot
fire during a wedged walk, because the freeze pulse carries `steps`, `steps` counts EMITTED
walks (`steps += isinstance(action, Walk)`), and `market._market_walk_toward` returns a `Walk`
on every tick of a greedy approach. An agent hammering a blocked tile bumps `steps` forever
while standing still, so the pulse always differs and the counter resets every tick.

Measured on §30.2's shape — a real `CarpenterLife` walled off from its vendor, 400 ticks,
frozen on one tile the whole time:

```
NO PROGRESS : 0 fires        NO OUTPUT : 0 fires
```

**Both alarms, silent.** That is why 2026-08-11 produced 203 give-ups, 0 gold and no warning.

### 37.1 The obvious fix works, and is wrong

Dropping `steps` from the pulse detects it: **0 → 9 fires**, with zero new false fires across
five healthy Lives at 3000 ticks each. I implemented it, measured it, wrote the argument that
it costs no detection — which is sound, and which I still believe:

> `_quiet` advances iff the pulse repeats, so the two versions differ in EXACTLY ONE state:
> a `Walk` was emitted AND the position did not change. `steps` moves only when a Walk is
> emitted, and a Walk that works moves the character — so wherever `steps` froze, the position
> had frozen too. It was redundant or masking, never a detector.

**What is false is the safety half.** I claimed 40 still held because "a single wedged LEG
cannot reach it — `_market_walk_toward` gives up at `stall_limit = 6`", and offered a transient
grid of 7 / 20 / 39 / 41 ticks, all firing 0 times.

A leg give-up **does not reset the counter**. The position is still frozen through the give-up,
the retry and the next wedge, so nothing about `stall_limit` bounds anything. The real first
firing point:

```
obstruction cleared at  41 ticks -> 0 fires
obstruction cleared at  42 ticks -> 1 fire
```

**My grid stopped one tick below it.** A transient obstruction of 42 worker ticks — ~18s at the
2026-08-03 forge cadence, an NPC standing on a tile — would have started alarming, on the alarm
this file already records false-firing "ten times at exactly 40 ticks, three of them in the
HEALTHY first half". Three independent review lenses caught this; none of my own measurements
would have, because I chose the grid.

### 37.2 What shipped instead

`WEDGED WALK`, a THIRD alarm with its own threshold, and `NO PROGRESS` left **byte-identical**
(`git diff` touches neither its pulse nor its print). The change is therefore strictly
ADDITIVE: no measured threshold moves. It is also the rule `NO OUTPUT` already established
here — two different failures must never share one line of text.

`_WEDGE_TICKS = 240` is **derived**: it is `_STALL_TICKS`, already measured against the longest
healthy silence any live log contains (159 ticks). Both alarms answer *"is this stretch longer
than any healthy agent explains?"*, and this one can afford at least as much patience, because
the failure it names is PERMANENT — §30.2's wedge lasted the whole day, so detection latency
costs nothing while a false fire costs the operator's trust.

```
transient cleared at 42 / 60 / 120 / 200 / 239 ticks -> 0 fires, agent recovers
never cleared                                        -> fires, throttled, names the tile
** Sten: WEDGED WALK — 182 walk actions emitted over 240 ticks and the position
   never changed (t=241, @(5,5)) **
```

Not measured against: a live obstruction-duration distribution. No log records how long a real
NPC stands on a tile.

### 37.3 Three mutants that survived, all the same shape

Every one was a fixture that could not reach the case it named — §35.3, for the third pass
running. Worth recording because the pattern is now clearly *mine* rather than incidental:

- **The baseline rebase.** `_tried = steps - _still_steps` must rebase per stretch, or an agent
  that walked earlier and then died reports its old walks as a wedge — geometry blamed for a
  lost tool. My idle fixture never walked, so `steps` was 0 under both rules.
- **The "any walks attempted?" guard.** Deleting it makes a fully idle agent print
  `WEDGED WALK — 0 walk actions`. My dead-agent fixture ran 85 ticks, below the 240 threshold,
  so it could not reach the state.
- **The throttle.** `_still % _WEDGE_TICKS == 0` → `>= _WEDGE_TICKS` prints every tick
  thereafter; a presence-only assertion cannot see it. This file has paid for that before:
  unthrottled, `FRAME OVERDUE` measured 3,881 identical lines in a 4,000-tick run.

11 mutants, all killed.

### 37.4 What this does NOT fix

- **`NO OUTPUT` is blind to the same failure, and stays blind.** A wedge retires a capability
  frame roughly every 8 ticks; each retirement is a terminal outcome, so `_work_recorded`
  advances and work-liveness reads a permanently stuck agent as productive. Verified in
  review. **Follow-up 37.**
- **Both alarms live only in `village._run_worker`.** Any agent driven by another loop has
  neither. **Follow-up 38.**
- **`WEDGED WALK` has never printed on a shard.** Everything above is offline.
- The wedge is INFERRED at the worker from position, not detected where it happens. The walk
  already knows — `{tag}_stall` reaches `stall_limit` — and a skill-side signal would be exact
  rather than inferential. Not done: it would touch the hot path of every market skill.

1604 tests, ruff clean.

## 38. Follow-up 37 — the alarm nobody had: "busy, and completing nothing" (2026-08-13)

§37.4 left `NO OUTPUT` blind to the wedge and named it follow-up 37. Measured first, before
anything was designed, because §37's own description of the mechanism came from review rather
than from a run. On the wedge fixture (a real `CarpenterLife` walled off from its vendor):

```
_work_recorded advanced 74 times in 600 ticks   (a give-up every 8 ticks)
econ total_reward                               = 0.000 exactly
episode kinds / rewards                         = all ('skill', 0.0)
```

Confirmed, and the shape is worse than "one alarm has a hole". The three existing alarms all
answer *has this agent stopped?* — and this failure is an agent that has **not** stopped.

### 38.1 The failure the other three cannot see, and why

§22.2 is the cleanest instance, and it is not the wedge: a tinker whose vendor had sold out
re-admitted `buy_iron` **49 more times**, each trip walking to the shop, opening the window,
re-rolling its budget and coming back empty. Every one of those trips was CORRECT. That agent
**walked** — so `NO PROGRESS` and `WEDGED WALK` both reset on its position — and **recorded** —
so `NO OUTPUT` read it as productive. Three alarms, all structurally silent, on a loop that
earned nothing for 49 consecutive transactions.

The signal that does separate them is the retirement REASON, which `frame_retirements` already
computes and `_run_worker` already drains every tick. `achieved` is the only reason meaning a
transaction completed; `giveup` / `expired` / `replaced` / `cancelled` all mean the opposite,
however healthy the agent looks.

### 38.2 Reward was the obvious signal and it is unusable

Two independent reasons, both measured rather than argued:

- `Episodes.total_reward()` sums a **bounded 500-entry deque**, so it is not monotone and a
  frozen-reward test drifts as old episodes fall out. `_run_worker`'s own `_STALL_TICKS`
  comment had already rejected it for exactly this, which I re-derived instead of reading.
- **Offline it does not discriminate at all.** All five healthy Lives run 3000 ticks with ZERO
  paid events, because `MockBody` has no vendor to sell to. A signal whose healthy case cannot
  be constructed cannot have a threshold measured against it.

### 38.3 The threshold was wrong, and this time it was caught before shipping

`_THRASH_TICKS = _STALL_TICKS` was written down, justified by analogy to `_WEDGE_TICKS`, and is
**false**. A healthy day is not a steady drip of achievements: §17's 1800-tick forge run banked
six times and did it as one early 23g deposit and then five more only after t≈756, because the
tinker was working through a single 69-ingot delivery. **At 240 ticks the alarm fires
repeatedly across that gap, on a run that banked 503g.**

That is follow-up 35's mistake — a threshold inherited by analogy instead of measured — and the
only reason it did not ship twice is that the gap happens to be written down in this file.

Re-derived the way `_STALL_TICKS` itself was: longest measured healthy silence × the same ~1.5
safety factor that turned 159 into 240. 756 × 1.5 = 1134, rounded to **1200** (5 ×
`_STALL_TICKS`). Measured after:

| fixture | fires |
|---|---|
| healthy: buy achieves, 1800 ticks (`landed=1/1`) | **0** |
| healthy-gap: 6 give-ups spanning >240 ticks, then a purchase (`landed=1/7`) | **0** |
| §22.2: vendor dry, 2400 ticks (`landed=0/12+12`) | 1 |
| §30.2: wedged sell, 1800 ticks (`landed=0/224+224`) | 1 |

**It rests on ONE observation and is provisional.** No live log records the distribution of
gaps between achievements, because the tape has never carried the streak. That is what
`landed=` is for.

### 38.4 The prediction, written down before the run

`landed=<achieved>/<retired>+<streak>` rides every agent's line so the next ordinary forge day
supplies the number §38.3 should have been derived from. Before that run:

- A healthy forge tinker should end with `achieved` **> 0** and a final streak well under 12,
  with the largest mid-run streak below the ~30 transactions 1200 ticks buys.
- `NOTHING LANDS` should fire **0 times** on a day that banks at all.
- **If it fires on a day that banked**, 1200 is too low and the honest correction is to raise
  it to the measured streak plus the same 1.5 factor — not to weaken the signal.
- **If a day banks nothing and it does NOT fire**, the guard is wrong: retirements stopped for
  240 ticks somewhere, which would mean the failure is closer to `NO OUTPUT`'s shape than to
  this one.
- `landed=` on the MINER should read `0/0` all day — he retires no capability frames — and if
  it does not, something is admitting capabilities on an agent nobody thought had them.

### 38.5 Ten mutants, and the fixtures that could not reach their own cases

Five survived the first pass. All five were §35.3 — the fourth consecutive pass with that
shape, which is now a documented habit rather than an accident:

- **The achieved-resets-the-streak rule.** The obvious fixture (a few dry windows, then stock)
  produces ONE achieved retirement and no streak at all, because the mock's windows are
  consumed by a single trip's own re-rolls. Needs 40 dry windows to make eight real give-ups
  first.
- **The idle guard.** `MockVendor`'s window list cycles, so trips never stop coming — and the
  guard is only reachable when they do. Needed a hand-built agent that retires three frames and
  then goes silent.
- **The throttle**, invisible to a presence-only assertion, as in §37.3.
- **Both threshold mutants** (240 and 2400), each needing a fixture whose unproductive stretch
  falls between them — which is the healthy-gap case in §38.3's table.

### 38.6 What this does NOT settle

- **`NOTHING LANDS` has never printed on a shard.** Everything above is offline.
- **No healthy SELL control exists offline.** `MockVendor` models the buy side only, so the
  only achieving fixture in this repo is a purchase (follow-up 34 remains the blocker).
- **The threshold is anchored on a single observation** (§38.3).
- **Both new alarms still live only in `village._run_worker`** — follow-up 38, unchanged.

1604 -> **1610 tests**, ruff clean. 10 mutants, all killed.

## 39. The forge day the instruments were built for — one prediction refuted, one alarm wrong (2026-08-13)

Four instruments shipped today and none had printed on a shard. This is their first day, and
it is also the ordinary forge day follow-up 30 has been waiting for since §34. Full tape kept
(`~/forge-*.log`), which §36.6 named as the thing the 2026-08-11 day lacked.

```
python -m anima2.village --forge-pair --ticks 1800 --monitor
banked=1483  net=+1565g (+6865g/h)  16 achieved bank deposits  retired>=128:126a/2g
```

**The best forge day on record**, against 0 / 473 / 503 / 573 / 818 on the same command. The
economics remain unattributable in the sense §30.3 means — one run does not survive that spread
— and nothing here claims the day's gold was caused by anything shipped today.

### 39.1 §34.4's pre-registered prediction fired on its REFUTING branch

Written down before the run, quoted verbatim from §34.4:

> `nores=` should dominate `inval=` on healthy early stands. If `inval=` dominates in the dead
> tail instead, then follow-up 28's premise — "more stands reached = more ore" — is false and
> the lever is geometry, not give-up speed.

The counters are CUMULATIVE and `win=` is a rolling window, so this can only be read by DIFFING
consecutive samples — end-of-run totals answer a different question. Over 205 pair samples:

| | inval | nores | inval share |
|---|---|---|---|
| first third | 3 | 15 | **17%** |
| middle | 5 | 13 | 28% |
| last third | **81** | 26 | **76%** |

**Both halves landed as written.** And the asymmetry runs the right way: the partition iterates
`nores -> inval -> packfull` with a `break` (`harvest.py`), so a tick carrying BOTH clilocs is
charged to `nores`. Ties go to `nores`, which makes `nores` dominating early WEAK evidence and
`inval` dominating late STRONG. The reading this licenses is the one §34.4 named in advance:
**follow-up 28's premise is false, and the lever is geometry rather than give-up speed.**

Context that bounds it: `banks=5` reached of 12 surveyed stands, 8 hops, `recov=none peak=24`,
`walk=98 swing=1033`, `NO-TOOL!` never fired — so the pick budget confound (`_pickaxes_for`
clamps to 8 picks ≈ 800 swing-ticks) is ruled out for this day rather than assumed away.

### 39.2 Three instruments behaved; the fourth was wrong, and it was mine

| instrument | first live verdict |
|---|---|
| `trip=` / `to=` / `d=` / `stall=` | **worked.** `trip=?` zero times. 216 `none` (miner), 161 `craft`, 16 `sell`, 12 `bank`, 5 `buy`. |
| `landed=` | **worked.** `129/131+1` on the tinker, `0/0` on the miner — both §38.4 predictions, confirmed. |
| `NOTHING LANDS` | **worked.** 0 fires on a day that banked 1483g, which is exactly what §38.4 said would validate the threshold. |
| `WEDGED WALK` | **FALSE-FIRED six times.** |

**`NOTHING LANDS` is the one to note.** §38.3 first wrote its threshold as `_STALL_TICKS` and
re-derived it to 1200 only because §17's 756-tick gap happened to be written down. At 240 it
would have fired repeatedly on this run. The re-derivation is what kept a productive day quiet.

### 39.3 The `WEDGED WALK` defect, and why the first guard was wrong

Six fires on Pim — who banked 1483 gold — printing:

```
** Pim: WEDGED WALK — 3 walk actions emitted over 1440 ticks and the position never
   changed (t=1511, @(2609,474)) **
```

(2609,474) is his own craft tile. The guard was `_tried > 0`: *were any walks emitted during
this stretch?* Three stray walks across 1440 ticks reclassified a stationary crafter as wedged,
and the line reads absurd on its face — the defect class this project treats as worse than
silence, because a reader who believes it goes looking for geometry.

**It gets worse with time, which is the part worth naming.** `_tried` is fixed once the strays
are emitted while `_still` keeps climbing, so the evidence RATIO DECAYS while the alarm keeps
repeating: 1.25% at the first fire, 0.21% at the sixth.

Measured separation, the live false positive against the offline §30.2 reproduction:

```
productive tinker (false) : 1.25% -> 0.21% of ticks were walk attempts
real wedge (true)         : 75.8% -> 75.1%, stable across every fire
```

A ~60x gap. The fix is a MAJORITY rule — `_tried * 2 >= _still` — deliberately not a percentage
constant: it states what a wedge IS (an agent walking, every tick, into the same tile) rather
than a number someone must later re-derive. The share is now printed so a reader can judge the
evidence: `182 walk actions over 240 ticks (75% of them)`.

Four mutants killed, including the shipped defect itself. **One survives and is recorded rather
than papered over:** a 10% threshold behaves identically on every case measured (it still
excludes 1.25% and admits 75%). Inside a 60x gap that is an equivalent mutant, and pinning an
arbitrary point in the middle would be a test asserting a number nothing measured.

### 39.4 A correction to §36.5, which this run could not test

§36.5 predicted a healthy trip's `d=` should FALL across samples and end `<=2`. **Unobservable
on this runner, by staging invariant.** `run_forge_pair` calls `stage_shops` with the default
`strict=True`, which RAISES above `SELL_REACH=2` (`life_runner.py`), and writes single-waypoint
routes — and `walk_readout` reach-tests the final waypoint first. Measured: **33 `d=` samples,
all `<=2`, none above reach.** The run cannot start unless both shops are already in reach.

So bullets 2 and 3 of §36.5 are struck, not scored. What the 33 samples DO establish is the
inverse and it is worth keeping: `d=` above 2 is reachable only if the tinker DRIFTS off his
stand, which is §34.6's livelock mechanism — and it never happened. `grep 'd=[0-9]*>'` selects
those samples on any future tape.

The staged geometry, for the record: `Tinker: at (2610,473), 1 from the stand`,
`Banker: at (2610,474), 1 from the stand` — the Banker settled on `TRADE_HUB` rather than its
requested `(2610,475)`. Sixteen `bank_gold -> achieved` retirements at `age=8/120` say the
identity pin held anyway, which also retires (for this run) the risk that a third-party GM
client connected during the run could be resolved as a shop.

### 39.5 An older alarm this day indicted, and it is not new

`NO PROGRESS` fired **39 times on Pim and 5 on Grimm** — on a day that banked 1483 gold. That
alarm is byte-identical to what it was before today (§37 deliberately left it untouched), so
this is not a regression; it is the first tape showing that it is noisy on a stationary
crafter, whose reward, speech, position AND emitted-walk count all sit still between
completions. **Follow-up 39.** Note it is the same shape as §39.3 with the opposite sign: one
alarm calls a still crafter wedged, the other calls it dead, and neither is true.

### 39.6 What this day did NOT settle

- **The five-run freeze is not attributed.** §34's three defects are all reachable in code and
  the cause split now points at geometry, but no line says which defect the dead tail WAS.
- **`WEDGED WALK` has still never fired truly on a shard.** Its six live fires were all false.
  The fix is verified offline only.
- **One run does not settle the economics** (§30.3's six-sample spread).
- **`banks=5` of 12** — the pool is reached, not exhausted.

**CORRECTION (2026-08-14, §41).** This section originally added: *"§28.4's ceiling still wants
halving for Felucca (`ConsumedPerFeluccaHarvest = 2`)"*, on the pre-flight's reading that each
bank yields 5-17 successful swings rather than 10-34. **That is wrong, and it was taken on
report rather than verified.** `HarvestSystem.cs:199` calls `bank.Consume(amount, from)` with
`amount = def.ConsumedPerHarvest = 1`; `feluccaAmount` is used ONLY to set `item.Amount`
(lines 177-191), i.e. how much ore lands in the pack per dig. So a Felucca bank is still
**10-34 successful digs**, yielding 20-68 ore. The ceiling needed no halving, and §28.4 stands
as written.

1610 -> **1611 tests**, ruff clean.

## 40. Follow-up 34 — a sale that completes, offline, for the first time (2026-08-14)

`MockVendor` modelled the BUY side only. The cost was not tidiness, and it was compounding:

- §30.2 spent a live day on a sell loop that never completed, with **no offline control**
  to compare a working one against.
- §38 could baseline `NOTHING LANDS` only against a BUY that achieves, and had to record
  "not measured against a healthy sell loop" as a standing gap.
- Every Life fixture here that cannot buy retires nothing but give-ups — all five, measured
  at 3000 ticks — so "a healthy Life stays quiet" was unshowable by construction.

The result, from a real `TinkerLife` through the real `_sell_step`, nothing injected:

```
retired: (1, 'sell_tongs', 7, 180, 'achieved')
tongs 8 -> 0,  gold 10 -> 98   (8 x 11, the vendor's own price)
```

**The first `achieved` sale this project has offline.** It closes through
`CapabilityGoalComplete`'s achievement branch, at age 7 of a 180-tick budget.

### 40.1 The asymmetry with `windows`, which is the protocol's and not a shortcut

`buys` is a flat `{graphic: price}` map where `windows` is a list of openings, and the
difference is load-bearing. A BUY window is the vendor's own stock, which ServUO shows in
partial subsets — the whole reason `windows` cycles and the reason `OFFER_REOPEN_ATTEMPTS`
exists. A SELL window (0x9E `SellList`) is a list of **our own pack items** the vendor
recognises, rebuilt from the pack every time it opens. There is no per-opening subset to
model: what varies between openings is what we are carrying, and the pack already says
that. Modelling it as openings would have invented a failure mode the server does not have.

Two consequences the tests pin rather than assume: a sale that empties the pack yields an
EMPTY window on the next opening (which `_sell_step` reads as "the vendor does not
recognise this" and gives up — a real live shape), and the popup grows a Sell entry only
for a vendor that buys something, mirroring `BaseVendor.AddCustomContextEntries` and
keeping every pre-existing fixture's menu byte-identical.

### 40.2 What it buys immediately

- **A give-up and a sale are now distinguishable offline.** A vendor that does not buy the
  item retires `giveup` with the pack and coin UNTOUCHED — so a give-up can never be
  mistaken for a sale that paid nothing.
- **§38's gap is HALF closed.** An achieved SALE exists now, so "the only achieving
  fixture in this repo is a purchase" is retired. A **sustained** healthy sell loop still
  does not exist: the tinker sells its stock once, then has nothing to sell and no iron to
  buy, so the rest of the run is genuinely unproductive and `NOTHING LANDS` genuinely
  fires. Measured: `landed=1/10+9` over 1800 ticks. The control is the achievement, not
  the day, and `_THRASH_TICKS` is therefore still baselined on one observation (§38.3).

### 40.3 Ten mutants, all killed — and the one that needed a better fixture

Nine fell immediately, including paying without taking the goods, taking without paying,
buying an item not on the list, ignoring the popup index (which would open a BUY window for
a sell trip — indistinguishable from the live wedge this double exists to rule out), and
not surfacing `shop_sell` at all.

The survivor was "keep whatever window is already open instead of rebuilding it". The
rebuild test could not see it: the sale it performs CLOSES the window, so re-opening always
found `None` and the stale branch never ran. §35.3 for the fifth pass — the fixture has to
re-open **without a cancel in between**, which is now what it does.

### 40.4 What this does NOT settle

- **No sustained sell loop** (§40.2), so §38.3's threshold is unchanged and still
  provisional.
- **`MockVendor` still models no bank.** `bank_gold` achieves offline only through the
  BankBox item path, not a banker exchange.
- **Nothing here is live.** It is a double; its value is as a control, and a double that
  drifted from the shard would be worse than none.

1611 -> **1619 tests**, ruff clean.

## 41. The dead tail was a cliff — the miner was aiming above his own eye (2026-08-14)

§39.1's refuting branch said the mining lever was geometry. This is what the geometry was.

### 41.1 The mechanism, from the server's own source

ServUO runs the line-of-sight gate in `Target.Invoke` (`Target.cs`), which is **upstream of**
`OnTarget` → `StartHarvesting`, where `GetDefinition` (501862), `CheckRange` (500446) and
`CheckResources` (503040) live. `HarvestTarget` is constructed `base(-1, true, …)`, so its
range check is skipped and the LOS gate is the live one. The precedence is therefore

```
500237 (LOS)  >  501862 (not mineable)  >  500446 (range)  >  503040 (no metal)
```

**A tile the miner cannot SEE is refused before the ore bank is ever consulted.** That is why a
dead stand reports invalid-target replies forever with `nores=0` and `banks=` frozen: it is not
an exhausted vein, it is a vein he cannot look at. The eye sits at `Mobile.Z + 14` (`Map.cs`)
and a LandTarget's LOS destination is `GetAverageZ(x,y).top + 1`, so ore whose surface rises far
enough above the stand is unreachable no matter how long he swings at it.

### 41.2 Measured on the real map, and it predicts the tape

`find_mine_spots` returned **12 stands, of which 5 had EVERY node 20 or more above the stand**:
(2601,487), (2591,498), (2583,500), (2575,499), (2581,490). Reproduced here directly off the
map files — the same 12 the tape's survey line prints.

The three of those the 2026-08-13 run actually visited each produced a 24-sample all-invalid
window with zero ore, and together they are the dead tail. The workflow's LOS port predicted the
per-stand inval share against the tape's own segmentation and hit **seven of eight positions
within 6 points**, including 100%/100% at each of the three dead ones — with no free parameters.

### 41.3 The fix, and why the threshold cannot matter

`uomap.find_mine_spots` grows `max_rise=13`, dropping nodes whose 4-corner surface sits more
than that above the stand, applied at **candidate-generation time — before the spacing dedupe**.
That placement is the whole value: a blind stand is not deleted, it is **replaced** by a live
neighbour that then wins the dedupe.

```
before : 12 stands, 5 blind,  69 live nodes, 14 distinct ore banks
after  : 10 stands, 0 blind,  94 live nodes, 14 distinct ore banks
```

**The filter costs no ore.** The same 14 banks are covered; four live neighbours replaced five
blind stands ((2591,500), (2583,502), (2575,503), (2576,494)), and usable nodes rose 36%.

`13` is `Mobile.Z + 14` minus one — "at or below eye level" — but the choice is *insensitive*
and that is pinned: every value from 11 to 19 yields the byte-identical stand list, because each
dead stand's nodes are all ≥ 20 above it. A threshold whose exact value cannot change the answer
is the kind worth having, and the test asserts exactly that.

`_corner_top` reads all four corners like `Map.GetAverageZ` does, not the single map cell —
tested on a hand-built map, because the trade pool happens not to separate the two readings and
the mutant survived there (§35.3, sixth pass).

### 41.4 What the survey guard did, correctly

`test_the_mine_pool_is_not_smaller_than_the_survey_it_draws_from` asserted `len(found) >= 12`
with the message *"the pool size below needs re-deriving rather than assuming"*. It fired, and
it was **right to**: the size was re-derived rather than renumbered, which is how the "costs no
ore" result above was found at all. Three pinned tests changed; none was weakened.

### 41.5 The instrument that adjudicates the next run — and the deferral that expired

`500446` is now its own `far=` bucket, leaving `500237 + 501862` in `inval=`. §35.5 deferred
exactly this change, for a good reason: redefining `inval` would have invalidated §34.4's
written-down-beforehand prediction. **That prediction was adjudicated on 2026-08-13, so the
reason for the deferral is spent.**

**Pre-registered, before the next forge day:** the dead-tail invals were line-of-sight refusals,
not range refusals. So on a repeat of that itinerary, `far=` should stay SMALL while `inval=`
carries the tail. Sharpest case: at a stalled-return position the workflow's model says 100%
`500237` and **zero** `500446`, while two of its own input measurements predicted the opposite.
`far=` adjudicates between them. If `far=` dominates a dead stand, this diagnosis is wrong about
the precedence and the lever is the walk, not the survey.

### 41.6 What is NOT fixed, and one thing that was refuted

- **Fix 2 was refuted and is NOT applied.** The workflow proposed also dropping a stale node
  list on non-relocation legs, worth "23 invals". A skeptic showed the counterfactual is
  identical — at that position the replacement node list is equally blind, so it saves nothing —
  and that its suggested shortcut (`_nodes_all_out_of_reach`) is precisely the change §35.2
  records as destroying a woodsman's grove. Recorded because a plausible fix that measures to
  zero is worth knowing about.
- **The last 113 ticks at (2583,500) remain unexplained.** 116 swings, `walk` frozen, `ph=mine`,
  and **zero verdicts of any kind** — no inval, no nores, no reward, and `win=None`. The model
  predicts 8/8 LOS refusals there and a hop within ~45 ticks; it got silence. A position that
  produces no verdicts feeds the stuck window nothing, so `_start_relocate` can never fire and
  the miner is stranded permanently. That is a **second, distinct defect**, it is 42% of the
  day's dead time, and `max_rise` does not address it. **Follow-up 40.**
- **Nothing here is live.** The pool change is offline-measured; `max_rise` has never run on a
  shard, and only 2 of the 5 blind stands have live corroboration.
- **`village.LUMBER_MAP = 1` versus Felucca's fileIndex 0** is a real latent mismatch the
  workflow found. It is NOT this run's cause — `find_mine_spots` returns an identical pool on
  both facets — but it is unrecorded anywhere else. **Follow-up 41.**

1622 tests, ruff clean. 7 mutants over the filter, 6 killed; the box-widening one is equivalent
on this terrain at every radius from 6 to 40 and is recorded rather than papered over.

## 42. §41.5's prediction, adjudicated — and the defect moves off the survey (2026-08-14)

Second forge day, same command, with three things that had never run on a shard: the 10-stand
`max_rise` pool, the `far=` split, and `WEDGED WALK`'s majority rule. Tape kept
(`~/anima-logs/forge-20260814-1239.log`, 766 lines).

### 42.1 The pre-registered prediction was right, on its sharpest case

§41.5, written before the run:

> at a stalled-return position the model says 100% `500237` and **zero** `500446`, while two of
> its own input measurements predicted the opposite. `far=` adjudicates between them.

Measured at the dead position: **`far=0`, `inval=19`, `nores=0`.** Zero range refusals. The
LOS-precedence reading holds and the two measurements that predicted 500446 were wrong. This is
what the split was built for, one run after it was built.

### 42.2 `max_rise` is live-proven, at the stand level

**Every SURVEYED stand produced.** Per-stand, diffed sample to sample:

| stand | far | inval | nores | invalid-family |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| (2611,474) home | 0 | 0 | 18 | **0%** |
| (2610,481) | 3 | 2 | 13 | 28% |
| (2609,489) | 4 | 1 | 8 | 38% |
| (2607,497) | 3 | 0 | 15 | 17% |
| (2592,500) | 0 | 0 | 2 | 0% |
| **(2608,476)** | **0** | **19** | **0** | **100%** — *not a surveyed stand* |

Yesterday three surveyed stands were 100% dead; today none is. Totals: invalid-family **89 → 39**
(-56%), `nores` 54 → 59, `banks` 5 → 6. And `recov=2(1)` — **the first non-zero vein recovery
this project has ever recorded**, against `recov=none` on both prior days.

### 42.3 The remaining defect is the ITINERARY, not the survey

The one dead position is not a stand at all. The sequence:

```
t=1312 @(2599,496) ph=deliver   inval=3
t=1339 @(2609,481) ph=return
t=1347 @(2609,475) ph=mine      inval=3    <- resumes mining HERE
t=1356 @(2608,476) ph=mine      inval=8
t=1408 @(2610,478) ph=mine      inval=27   <- 24 invals, zero nores
t=1432 @(2593,499) ph=mine      banks=5    <- relocates away, stops
```

**The miner resumes `ph=mine` the instant the return leg flips phase, two to three tiles short of
the home stand (2611,474)**, and works dead ground until the stuck window fills and relocates
him. 24 of the day's 39 invalid-family verdicts came from that one stretch, in 5 of 203 samples.

`far=0` throughout is the informative part: these are NOT range refusals. He is not reaching past
ore he could work — he is standing where there is none, and probing it.

This is the competing explanation the `alt` skeptic built during §41's review — *"the miner mines
from wherever he is standing, and the itinerary drops him somewhere dead"* — now with live
support. It also explains why that skeptic was right to refute §41's Fix 2: dropping the stale
node list there changes nothing, because the ground itself is barren. **The lever is arriving at
a stand before resuming, not which nodes are installed. Follow-up 42.**

### 42.4 The instruments

- **`WEDGED WALK`: 0 fires**, against 6 false ones yesterday. The majority rule holds on the exact
  agent that defeated the first version. It has STILL never fired truly on a shard.
- **`NOTHING LANDS`: 0**, on a day that banked — §38.4's prediction again.
- **`trip=?`: 0.** **`FRAME OVERDUE`, `NO-TOOL`, `DIED`: 0.**
- **`NO OUTPUT`: 1**, on Pim, `craft_tongs`, 240 ticks — a real craft stall, correctly caught.
- **`NO PROGRESS`: 34.** Still noisy on a stationary crafter; follow-up 39 unchanged.

### 42.5 The economics, NOT attributed

`banked=958  net=+1040g  10 deposits`, against yesterday's 1483 / 16. **Lower**, and no claim is
made either way: the same-command series is now 0 / 473 / 503 / 573 / 818 / 1483 / 958, and one
run cannot survive that spread (§30.3). This run also ended mid-buy (`admitted=buy_iron@3/180`,
`iron=4`, `tongs=0`), so its final gold is a snapshot of an unfinished cycle.

The cause-split ratios are a different kind of number — within-run structure rather than a daily
total — which is why §42.2 leans on them and not on gold.

### 42.6 What is still open

- **The last-third invalid share is 61%**, down from 76% but not gone — and §42.3 says why.
- **Follow-up 40 did not recur**: no position produced zero verdicts this run. Unfixed and
  unobserved is not fixed.
- **One run.** `max_rise` has one day; the per-stand table is its evidence, not the gold.

1622 tests, ruff clean (no code change in this section — it is a measurement).

## 43. Follow-up 42 — do not mine the smithy apron (2026-08-17)

§42.3 named the lever: arrive at a stand before resuming. The return leg did not.

### 43.1 The mechanism, from the code the tape already implied

`MineSmeltDeliver._return_step` treated three `None`s as the same fact — no home
recorded, standing on `miner_home`, and `_walk_toward` giving up. `step()` flips
`smelt_phase` to `mine` on any of them and calls `Mine.step` the same tick. A
greedy walk that wedges two tiles short of `(2611, 474)` is the live resume at
`(2609, 475)`: the smithy apron, no ore, `far=0` / `inval=19` until the stuck
window relocates him.

Two other paths drop him on the same apron even when the greedy walk does not
stall. A pool relocate counts one tile short as arrived (`Harvest.near_enough`)
and wrote that short tile into `miner_home`, so the next haul walked back to the
apron rather than the surveyed stand. And a delivery that fired *during* an
in-flight relocate left `miner_home` on the condemned tile — the hop never
finished, so the return had no stand to walk to. The 2026-08-14 sequence
(`ph=deliver` at `(2599, 496)` → `ph=return` at `(2609, 481)` → `ph=mine` at
`(2609, 475)`) is consistent with all three; the tests pin each of them rather
than guessing which one the tape was.

### 43.2 What changed

- **Return stall is not arrival.** `_return_step` yields `None` only on the
  stand (exact tile, or one tile short when `harvest_nodes` are installed — the
  same slack relocation uses). A wedged greedy walk issues `WalkTo` and stays
  in `return`. A route that still cannot reach the stand after
  `return_route_retries` (3, `GoTo.walkto_max_retries`) calls `_start_relocate`
  instead of swinging where it died.
- **`miner_home` is the surveyed stand.** `_clear_relocate(...,
  reached_stand=True)` writes `harvest_relocated_stand`; the wrapper prefers
  that over the arrival tile. A stall-giveup does not claim the target it
  failed to reach. An in-flight pool hop also commits `miner_home` to the
  target the moment the walk starts.
- **Delivery waits for the hop.** `mine → deliver` does not fire while
  `harvest_relocating` is set, so a haul mid-hop cannot send him home to the
  condemned tile.

The live coordinates are the fixture, not a sketch: `(2609, 475)` vs
`(2611, 474)` is chebyshev 2. One tile short of a noded stand still resumes;
two tiles does not. That is the line that separates a walled face from the
apron.

### 43.3 Mutants

- **Return stall still yields `None`** — literally the pre-fix `_walk_toward`
  give-up. Fails `test_return_stall_on_the_smithy_apron_does_not_resume_mining`.
- **`near_enough = 2`** — the live apron counts as a stand. Fails
  `test_return_two_tiles_short_is_not_a_stand_even_with_nodes`.
- **Deliver still fires mid-relocate** — `miner_home` stays on the condemned
  tile. Fails `test_delivery_waits_for_an_in_flight_relocation_to_reach_a_stand`.
- **`miner_home` stays the one-tile-short arrival** — next haul walks back to
  the apron. Fails `test_one_tile_short_pool_arrival_rehomes_delivery_to_the_stand`.
- **Exhausted `WalkTo` mines in place** — the defect with a longer walk.
  Fails `test_return_route_that_cannot_reach_home_relocates_instead_of_mining_here`.

### 43.4 What this does NOT settle

- **Nothing here is live.** The 2026-08-14 tape is the fixture. A forge day
  after this change is what says the apron stretch is gone.
  ***SCORED 2026-08-18 — PASS, see §47.** Grimm crossed `(2609, 475)` only
  while relocating or returning (`this rock is dead` / `heading back to my
  rock`). No `ph=mine` stretch swinging on the apron.
- **Follow-up 40 is untouched** (a position that produces no verdicts at all).
- **Follow-up 41 is untouched** (`village.LUMBER_MAP = 1` vs Felucca fileIndex 0).
- **`DeliverBoards._return_step` still treats a greedy stall as arrival.** The
  board chain is economically frozen (`docs/CARPENTER.md`); this change stays
  on the flagship miner. The shape is the same and will bit-rot if copied
  later without the stand check.
- **Which of the three paths the tape actually took is still not known.** The
  tests close all three; a live run can only say the apron stretch did or did
  not recur.

1631 tests, ruff clean.

## 44. Follow-up 40 — a stand that never replies must hop (2026-08-17)

§41.6 named a second defect next to the cliff: 113 ticks at `(2583, 500)`, 116
swings, `walk` frozen, `ph=mine`, **zero verdicts**, `win=None`. The stuck
window is outcome-only — silence appends nothing, which is the dilution fix
`test_reply_less_ticks_carry_no_verdict_and_do_not_dilute` exists to keep.
A stand that never replies therefore never fills the window, so
`_start_relocate` cannot fire and the miner is stranded. That is 42% of that
day's dead time. `max_rise` does not address it. §42.6 recorded that it did
not recur the next day; unobserved is not fixed.

### 44.1 The lever

Do not put silence back into the window (that is the dilution defect). Count
it separately. `harvest_silent` increments on a no-verdict tick while a tool
or cursor is present, resets on any real sample, and relocates at two full
windows (48 ticks for `Mine`: `24 * silence_relocate_windows=2`). That is
above the 10-tick lag burst the dilution test allows and below the 113-tick
stranding.

`Chop.silence_relocate_windows` stays 0. A silent grove is not a reason to
drop the trees (§35.2).

`silent=` prints on the miner line when the counter is non-zero, so the next
forge day can see the watchdog working or see that it is looking at the wrong
ticks.

### 44.2 Mutants

- **Silence still appends `0` to the window** — the dilution test still fails
  that, unchanged.
- **A 10-tick lag burst relocates** — the same test now also forbids a
  `WalkTo` after the burst.
- **48 silent swings do not hop** — fails
  `test_a_silent_stand_relocates_before_a_hundred_ticks`.
- **A verdict does not reset the counter** — fails
  `test_a_verdict_resets_the_silence_counter`.
- **Chop hops on silence** — fails `test_chop_does_not_relocate_on_silence`.

### 44.3 What this does NOT settle

- **Nothing here is live.** The 113-tick stand is the fixture. If a future
  tape shows `win=None` and `silent=0` on a stranded miner, the watchdog is
  counting the wrong ticks.
  ***SCORED 2026-08-18 — instrument PASS, hop was the nores window not
  silence, see §47.** `silent=` printed, peaked ~14, reset on verdicts.
  First hop narrate `relocating: 0/0 ... failures` is the window having
  already cleared — not a 48-tick silence hop. The stranded-no-verdict
  stand did not recur.
- **Why that stand was silent is still unknown.** The hop leaves it; it does
  not explain the missing clilocs.
- **Follow-up 40's non-recurrence on 2026-08-14 is unchanged.** One more
  absence is not a close.

  (Felucca), not the `LUMBER_MAP=1` fallback.
- **`LUMBER_MAP = 1` remains the fallback** for the moment before the first
  observation. Tests that call `find_mine_spots(LUMBER_MAP, ...)` directly
  are unchanged.

## 48. Stale `bs_stand` — return to yesterday's craft tile (2026-08-18)

Fixture: `~/anima-logs/forge-20260818-0039.log` (the FU15-craft verify day).

### 48.1 What the tape said

Early day, sells left from `@(2611,473)` and reached the vendor at
`d=1<=2`. After Pim settled at craft_spot `@(2609,474)`, every late
`sell_return` read `to=(2611,473) d=2>0` — **20** samples — and
**15** `sell_tongs` frames retired `age=11/180 -> giveup` with tongs
already 0 (gold taken). **5** `bank_gold` frames the same shape at
age 14. Day ended on that return wedge with
`want=fetch_iron admitted=sell_tongs@9/180`.

Cause: `setdefault("bs_stand", here)` froze the first craft tile of the
day. Opening a later trip from a drifted stand still returned to the
frozen exact-reach-0 home. §34.6 already refuted anchoring home to
`craft_spot` alone (that made a livelock worse); the lever is "home =
where THIS trip left," not "home = workplace."

### 48.2 The lever

`skills/market.py::_refresh_bs_stand_on_trip_open`:

- On trip **open**, if still inside `craft_spot`'s radius (or no pin
  yet), overwrite `bs_stand` to the current tile.
- Mid-trip / return: `setdefault` only.
- Vendor-sequence opens (no `craft_spot`, already at the shop) keep a
  seeded home — unconditional overwrite made `*_return` a no-op in the
  offline suite.

`BlacksmithMarket.step` already pinned on craft→sell/bank; the
capability wrappers (forge-pair path) now share the same rule.

Mutants:

- Opening sell at craft with a stale pin does not refresh — fails
  `test_opening_a_sell_trip_repins_bs_stand_to_where_it_left` /
  `test_capability_sell_repins_bs_stand_on_open_not_mid_return`.
- Mid-return overwrites home — fails the mid-return half of the
  capability test and `test_sell_wedged_walk_...`.

Two facts, one lever — a dry grove hops to the next surveyed grove,
the miner's `harvest_spot_pool` path, which this profession had never
wired:

1. `Chop` left `no_resource_clilocs` empty so 500493 only cycled

   trees (`abandoned=True`); dropping a grove is the end of the trade.
