## 52. Three woodsman defects, closed in parallel by dispatched workers (2026-08-23)

Three §51 leftovers, each independent and each in its own production file, closed test-first by
three workers on separate worktrees with this session coordinating. Recorded together because
the coordination itself produced two of the findings.

### 52.1 `Chop` now samples the range refusal — and that explains the missing `win=`

§51.2 asked for it: *"'too far' and 'nodes never installed' are indistinguishable: Chop does not
sample 500446."* Confirmed in code — the base `invalid_target_clilocs` is empty and only `Mine`
fills it, so `stuck_this_tick` never saw 500446 for a woodsman.

**The task spec I wrote was wrong and the worker refused it.** I asked for "reporting only,
exactly as Mine does it". For `Mine` the split was free because 500446 was already sampled; for
`Chop` nothing is, so ANY wiring changes the window. Worse, `node_exhausted_clilocs` is DERIVED
(`no_resource | invalid_target | 500493`), so mirroring Mine literally would also have advanced
`harvest_idx` on a too-far reply — a Chop node-handling change, which is precisely what §35.2
records as destroying a woodsman's grove.

Ruled: sample it, do NOT cycle on it. `Chop.too_far_clilocs = {500446}` outside
`invalid_target_clilocs`, with `stuck_this_tick` unioning `too_far_clilocs` explicitly
(byte-identical for `Mine`, whose far nests, and `Fish`, whose far is empty). That option A was
never available is not an opinion: mutant M3, the literal Mine shape, is killed by the
PRE-EXISTING `test_chop_is_unchanged_by_the_widened_cycling_rule`.

**The diagnostic by-product is the real prize.** The two defect pins failed with
`KeyError: 'harvest_recent_stuck'` — not a wrong value, the key did not exist. Twenty-four range
refusals used to leave Chop's window **completely empty**, which is exactly the `win=`-absent
tape of woodsman-20260822-1225. The silent second stand may simply have been out of range all
along, and `far=` is now what would say so.

`village.stuck_cause_readout` is one formatter that both the miner and the woodsman line print,
so `far=` reaches a woodsman tape at all. 8 of 9 mutants killed; **M9 survives** — the miner's
call site sits inside `run_forge_pair`'s live thread loop with no offline seam, and that status
block has never had one.

**BEHAVIOUR DELTA, carried deliberately:** Chop's window now samples range refusals, so 24 of
them hop where before they did nothing. §51.3's prediction was written BEFORE this. **Do not
score the next woodsman tape as though Chop's window were unchanged.**

### 52.2 `SELL_BOARDS_AT` was off-grid by one harvest quantum

§51 recorded "leftover 10 boards below SELL_BOARDS_AT=20 still do not sell" as unsettled. I asked
whether the number was wrong rather than the rule shape. The measurement answered something
better than the question.

T2A means `Core.ML` is false, so no racial bonus and a single plain-Log branch;
`ConsumedPerHarvest=10`, `ConsumedPerFeluccaHarvest=20`, and `HarvestBank.Consume` always takes
10. **A chop therefore yields exactly 10 or exactly 20 logs, `ProcessLogs` is 1:1, and every
board count a woodsman can hold is a multiple of ten.** Corroborated on three tapes / ~200
samples: the only `logs=` values ever printed are 0/10/20/30 and the only `boards=` are
0/10/20/40.

Per-bank magnitude is 30-80 logs (mean 55.8) — comfortably over 20, which by the letter said
"the threshold is right". But **the last harvest of every 20..45-log bank yields exactly 10,
universally.** So 20 is the smallest threshold that can strand a whole quantum and 10 is the
largest that structurally cannot. Both dead days ended `logs=0 boards=10` and held it for 51/67
and 47/69 samples. The old comment called 20 "a generous threshold"; it was a round number, the
same shape as the `BANK_ABOVE=300` mistake already recorded in that file.

`SellBoards.sell_threshold = LOGS_PER_HARVEST`. One constant, derived. The gate follows because
it reads the same attribute — and that coupling is now tested by driving `ready_capability_ids`
at T-1 and T rather than by restating a literal. It was proven twice: changing the constant broke
a pre-existing gate-side pin that hardcoded 19/20, and that failure IS the coupling.

**The interaction nobody asked about, surfaced by the worker:** `deliver_boards` (threshold 19)
outranks sell, so a 10-residue is HELD today and merges into the next delivery; at T=10 it is
sold instead. On a 60-log bank (20,20,10,10) that is 60 delivered before, 40 delivered + 20 sold
after — a ~33% supply trim on the lumberjack→carpenter chain for `run_supply_pair`.
`run_woodsman_life`, which both measured tapes used, is unaffected.

Accepted, because `docs/CARPENTER.md` records that every carpentry recipe destroys value at
vendor prices — boards sold beat boards turned into furniture. But CLAUDE.md calls that chain a
MECHANISM PROOF, so what is at risk is the demonstration, not gold: a trim leaves it intact,
starvation would not. **Nothing live has scored it**, so §52.4 pre-registers the check.

A conditional threshold (T=10 only when no carpenter drop is configured) was considered and
REJECTED: it makes the woodsman behave differently in two runners and adds its own unmeasured
failure mode — a configured-but-absent carpenter strands boards forever, which is the defect
being fixed.

### 52.3 The grove pool hops to the nearest grove, not the richest

§51.3 left it: *"grove_spot_pool is richest-first, so the first hop was 51 tiles (pool[3] is 17).
Nearest-first is a second lever, recorded not taken."* Taken now, as `find_mine_spots`' own
documented rule reaching the lumber side rather than a new policy.

The trade-off was measured on the real 138-grove Yew survey rather than asserted, and it does
cost quality:

| | richest-first | nearest-first |
|---|---|---|
| trees in the 12-slot pool | 49 | 26 |
| **distinct 4x3 lumber banks** | **32** | **25** |
| first hop | 51 tiles / 83 ticks | 6 tiles / 10 ticks |
| 12-stand itinerary | 616 tiles (167% of a 600-tick day) | 170 tiles (46%) |
| banks per walk tile | 0.052 | 0.149 (**2.9x**) |
| mean distance from home | 44.4 tiles | 9.9 |

Trees is the wrong denominator — several of a rich grove's trees share one 4x3 bank, so richness
buys 7 extra banks for 23 extra trees. The last row is what decides it: the shops are staged AT
the home stand, so mean distance from home IS the sell trip, and a `sell_boards` round trip drops
from ~145 ticks to ~32. The tempting middle option — keep richest-first's twelve and merely
re-sort them by distance — is measured and rejected, because re-ordering cannot move mean
distance from home; it is mutant M3 and the suite kills it. 6/6 mutants killed.

### 52.4 Pre-registered, before the next runs

**Next `--supply-pair` day** (§52.2's unscored trim):

| Pred | Refute |
|---|---|
| Boards delivered per day falls ~33% while woodsman gold rises, and the carpenter still completes furniture frames | The carpenter retires `fetch_boards`/craft frames as give-ups for want of stock — the cut starved the mechanism rather than trimming it |
| `logs=`/`boards=` stay multiples of ten | Any other value appears — `Core.ML` is on after all, a Human draws `ceil(20*1.1)=22`, and 10 stops being the largest non-stranding threshold |

**Next `--woodsman` day** (§52.1 and §52.3): §51.3's prediction still stands EXCEPT that Chop's
window now samples range refusals, so a hop may come sooner than that prediction assumed. New:
`far=` climbing at a silent stand means the trees are out of range (a WALK problem); `far=0` with
a live `tree=`/`d=<=2` means a stand that genuinely does not answer. The first hop should now be
single-digit tiles.

### 52.5 What this does NOT settle

- **No live evidence for any of the three.** All offline: ServUO source, the real `.mul` survey,
  and three existing tapes. `far=` has never appeared on a woodsman tape.
- **§51.2's 270 dead ticks are still unattributed** — §52.1 built the instrument that would
  attribute them, and proximity (§52.3) makes hops cheap, not productive: that hop landed on a
  5-tree grove and produced nothing, so richness did not protect it and nearness will not either.
- **The original lever for §52.2 is untouched and recorded for whoever takes it:** `decide_mode`
  reads ECON memory while every grove signal lives in HUNT memory, so "the grove is not
  producing" is structurally invisible to the rule. It would need a hunt→econ mirror, for which
  `warrior_life.py`'s existing `life_tick` write is the seam — not a subclass `tick` override.
- **M9 (§52.1) survives** for want of an offline seam on `run_forge_pair`'s status block.
- Distance in §52.3 is measured from home ONCE at survey time (the miner's exact shape), so the
  second hop is not re-optimised from the new position.

1652 -> **1664 tests**, ruff clean.

## 53. The sword warrior's first eight live days — it could not reach its own brain (2026-08-24)

A `/goal` to make the swordsman work well. Nine live runs, and for the first three the tape
could not say why anything happened: `run_warrior_village` printed
`— warrior village [Bram0:hunt] —` and nothing else, while every other runner in `village.py`
calls `telemetry_line`. The profession the goal was about had the least observability in the
repo.

### 53.1 The readout, and a defect it contained

`warrior_readout` prints, in `decide_mode`'s own priority order:
`blade=0x13FFr5 plate=6/6(pack 0) skill=hunt bandages=103 gold=50 banked=0 kills=1`. The blade
is shown by RANK because the upgrade rule is keyed on `SWORD_RANK`, so a refused upgrade is
readable where it happens.

**Its first version was wrong, and the test agreed with it.** `PLATE_ARMOR_LAYERS` maps
graphic → layer; both the readout and its test destructured the pair backwards, so the count
evaluated `worn[graphic].graphic == layer` — which can only ever be 0. Three tapes read
`plate=0/6`, I diagnosed "the warrior fights naked", and that was my instrument, not the world.
The production code (`warrior.py`, `capabilities.py`) indexes correctly and was never involved.
Corrected, and the corrected count immediately read `plate=6/6` — the suit had been on the whole
time. **A test that shares its subject's mistake proves the mistake.**

`(pack N)` was added for the same reason the swap survived: `plate=0/6` is equally consistent
with "never delivered" and "delivered and refused", two causes with opposite fixes.

### 53.2 Two staging deaths, in order of discovery

**Prey before equipment.** `gm.stage` puts the suit and blade in the PACK; `EquipWeapon` and
`EquipArmor` are agent reflexes that cannot run until the worker thread starts — after four
`stage_npc` calls at ~0.8s each. The runner spawned two pinned Ettins adjacent BEFORE that. So
an unarmed, unarmoured character stood in melee while the code dressed it. Removed; the monitor
already tops prey up, now behind a `_ready_to_fight` gate. The next run produced this project's
first warrior kill (`kills=1`, gold 50 → 222).

**Staged into an uncleared pocket.** PASS 1 teleported the warrior in and THEN wiped. Prey is
pinned and nothing removes it at exit, so pockets accumulate across runs: a tape opened
`DEAD at first observation ... before this worker's first tick`, killed by the previous run's
Ettins. Wipe now precedes placement, centred on the requested tile rather than the arrival —
possible only because `LandTarget` overwrites a ground target's z (§41), so there is no
character z to wait for.

### 53.3 `[Set HitsMax` never did anything

`PlayerMobile.HitsMax` is a getter-only override returning `RawStr / 2 + 50` on a non-AOS shard.
Str 150 therefore capped the warrior at exactly the 125/125 four tapes recorded while the runner
asked for 150. Str 200 delivers it; **measured live at 150/150**. Order matters and is now
commented: raise Str first, or the `[Set Hits` top-up clamps to the old maximum.

### 53.4 The death loop's other half

`WarriorSurvive` already existed and already carried `heal_until_fraction = 0.75`, added because
a warrior "re-engages Ettins at ~41% HP and is bursted down again". But the retreat half was
still stock: `Survive.flee_hostile_count = 3` against the runner's `prey_target = 2`. Two
constants in two files disagreeing about "outnumbered", and nobody had checked. At exactly two
attackers the warrior never stepped out of melee — it bandaged in place and the damage
out-paced the heal, five days running, the same trace each time (`5 → 25`, then `15 → 5 → dead`).
Wanting a 75% margin is useless if nothing lets you reach one. `flee_hostile_count = 2` on the
warrior only.

**That fix alone made it die FASTER** (t=461 → t=217) and it never moved a tile, which exposed
the real problem: the monitor tops prey up beside the warrior's LIVE position, so retreating is
futile by construction — step out of melee and two fresh full-HP creatures appear at the new
tile. An endless two-on-one no brain can win. `_ready_to_fight` now also requires the warrior to
be at or above `heal_until_fraction`, so the harness stops feeding a fighter while it heals.

**That produced the first sawtooth this project has recorded:**
`150 → … → 54 → 89 → 136 → …` — a real retreat, a real recovery to 91%, and 520 ticks of life
against a previous best of 217.

### 53.5 What is still broken

- **It still dies, and still cannot resurrect.** `BACK ALIVE` is 0 across all nine days.
  `RecoverDeath` now has a target (§53.6) and emits `WalkTo` every 8 ticks for up to 32
  attempts — and the ghost never moves a tile. Whether a ServUO ghost can be moved by the
  bridge's pathfinder at all is the open question, and it is in the sibling repo.
- **The second decline has no recovery.** After healing to 91% the warrior drained to death over
  380 ticks using only 6 of 97 bandages. `should_flee` needs `len(hostiles) >= 2`, so a SINGLE
  attacker is fought in place — and in-melee bandaging is interrupted. One attacker out-damaging
  a bandage is the next thing to measure, not assume.
- **Combat effectiveness is unexplained.** The best run killed one Ettin (`out+172.0`); the
  latest killed none (`out+0.0`, `eps=5` of unrewarded terminals) while taking continuous
  damage. `docs/SWORD-WARRIOR.md` claims it once beat three Ettins and an Orc at min HP 64/125.
  Nothing measured here explains the gap.

### 53.6 Resurrection: wired, unverified

`profession.py` builds `RecoverDeath()` with no arguments, so `resurrection_target` is None, and
the only other source is a shard-sent `WAYPOINT_RESURRECTION` — of which the hunting pocket
produced **zero** across every tape. Meanwhile the runner had staged a Healer twelve tiles away
the whole time, and ServUO's `BaseHealer.OnMovement` offers a `ResurrectGump` to a ghost that
walks within 2 tiles. Three connections now exist: `RecoverDeath` reads a `resurrection_spot`
memory key (below an explicit target and below the shard's own waypoints, so a real healer still
wins); the runner fills it; and `WarriorLife` copies that ONE key by name to the hunt agent,
because `RecoverDeath` is a work-planner reflex and the vendor routes go to the economy agent.

Untested live, for a mundane reason: the ghost does not walk.

1668 -> **1670 tests**, ruff clean.

## 54. The warrior stopped standing still to die (2026-08-24)

### 54.1 The observation §53 could not explain

The tenth live warrior day printed the character at `@(2587, 408)` for **all 203 samples** —
the staging tile. It never moved. It died at t=214 having spent **2 bandages of 97**.

`Survive` had been asked to flee since t≈70. It emitted `Walk` the whole time.

### 54.2 Two defects, both in the retreat, both offline-reproducible

1. **`flee_hostile_count = 3`.** Stock. The village stages prey on the four cardinal tiles, so
   a warrior fighting one or two of them was never *allowed* to disengage — it bandaged in
   melee, and ServUO's `Bandage.EndHeal` applies `toHeal -= toHeal * m_Slips * 0.35` with
   `disruptThreshold = 0` on non-AOS: every hit taken during the 6-second wrap slips it, so a
   heal under fire converges to nothing. `= 2` was tried first and made the warrior die
   FASTER (t=461 -> t=217), which is what exposed defect 2 rather than refuting the change.
   Now `1`: with a slipping bandage, one attacker is already too many.
2. **`_away_direction` walked into a body.** It computed a single heading from the hostiles'
   centroid and, when they cancelled on opposite sides (`norm < 1e-6`), `commit north`.
   North is one of the four tiles the village pins prey on. The warrior spent its entire
   `max_flee_steps` budget walking into an occupied tile, every flee, forever.

   It now ranks the ideal heading and fans out `0, +1, -1, +2, -2, +3, -3, +4`, skipping tiles
   a mobile stands on, and falls back to the ideal when boxed in on all eight sides. It
   consults *mobiles only*; terrain stays the walk's own stall give-up, so this cannot
   silently become a second pathfinder.

### 54.3 What the eleventh day showed: the mechanism works, the fight still does not

`~/anima-logs/warrior-20260824-0250-freetile.log`. The warrior **left the tile** —
`(2587,408) -> (2584,405) -> (2583,404) -> (2578,399) -> (2577,398)`, fourteen tiles — and for
the first time HP *recovered under its own steam*:

```
150 137 110  84  57 | 54  45  45  49  58  60 | 54  40  23   9  DEAD(t=459)
   monotone decline |   flat/rising ~140 ticks |   second decline
```

Best survival on record (t=459 vs 214), and the flat stretch is the first evidence that the
heal loop can out-pace the damage when the retreat is allowed to land.

It still died, with `kills=0`.

### 54.4 The next question the tape cannot answer, and the field added for it

The warrior bled out fourteen tiles from a pocket of prey the runner **pins** (`[Set CantWalk
true`). Pinned creatures cannot follow. So either something was never pinned, or something
nobody staged is standing on him — opposite fixes, and no field on the line separates them.

`warrior_readout` now prints `foes=d1,d4,d7` (nearest three hostile distances, `none` when
clear). Distance, not count, because melee reach is 1 and that is the number that decides
whether the next bandage slips. Hostility comes from `skills.combat.is_hostile`, the shared
target/flee predicate — the readout must not grow a second opinion about what a foe is.

**Prediction, written before the next run.** If `foes=none` holds through the second decline,
the damage is not melee and the retreat is not the lever — look at poison or a ranged/summoned
attacker. If `foes=d1` persists *while the warrior is fleeing*, something is following it and
the pinning is not working. The refuting branch for the whole §54 story is `foes=` reading
`d1` at the staging tile with the warrior stationary again — that would mean the free-tile
fan-out found no free tile, which the boxed-in test says should be rare.

### 54.5 A mutation harness that scored four mutants it never ran

Four of five mutants in the first `foes=` run came back SURVIVED. Two reasons, both worth
recording because both silently invert the meaning of a green mutation report:

- **Anchors were not unique.** `sorted(` and `[:3]` occur many times in a 3100-line module;
  `str.replace(a, b, 1)` mutated the first occurrence, hundreds of lines from the code under
  test. Scoping the replacement to the block between two fixed delimiters fixed it. This is
  the second time this session the same mistake was made.
- **Stale bytecode masked equal-length mutations.** `[:3]` -> `[:9]` and back leaves the file
  the SAME SIZE, and the restore landed inside the same mtime second, so CPython reused the
  mutant `.pyc`: `inspect.getsource` showed the restored source while the loaded code object
  had no `BINARY_SLICE` at all. Any same-length mutation is vulnerable. The harness now runs
  with `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1`.

Re-scored with both fixed: 4 mutants, 4 killed.

Separately, a helper added in the previous turn — `_hostile` in `tests/test_survival.py` —
**shadowed** the module-level `_hostile` defined 360 lines above it and broke an unrelated
test. Renamed `_flee_hostile`.

1670 -> **1673 tests**, ruff clean.

## 55. The prey was never pinned (2026-08-24)

### 55.1 §54.4's prediction, scored on its "something is following it" branch

`~/anima-logs/warrior-20260824-0309-foes.log`, the first tape with `foes=`. The warrior
stood at `@(2585, 411)` — one position for 47 consecutive samples — while its three
nearest hostiles read:

```
d1,d1,d1 -> d0,d1,d1 -> d0,d1,d3 -> d1,d3,d3 -> d2,d3,d3 -> d3,d3,d3 -> d1,d3,d3
```

Distances that *oscillate* while the observer is stationary are creatures that walk. One
read **`d0` — on the warrior's own tile**. `run_warrior_village` pins every prey it stages
(`[Set CantWalk true`), and pinned prey cannot do this.

So the answer to §54.4 is neither "the retreat does not work" nor "poison": **the retreat
works and the prey chases anyway.** The refuting branch (`foes=d1` at the staging tile,
stationary) did not occur — the warrior had moved.

### 55.2 The defect

`_spawn_pinned` sent `[Set CantWalk true` and never read it back, and it looked the
creature up with `retries=1` — deliberately, for throughput on the single shared GM
connection. On a miss it silently did nothing: `[Add` had already run, so an **unpinned
Ettin was left in the pocket with no line anywhere saying so**.

The artisan village had already been burned by exactly this and carries the rule in a
comment three hundred lines away — *"Never infer staging from the fact that a command was
sent"* — with a server read-back. The warrior spawner did not, and it is the path that
runs every monitor cycle for the life of the run, so leftovers ACCUMULATE and outlive the
warrior that provoked them (pockets already persist across runs; that is what §54's
wipe-before-stage reorder was for).

`stage_pinned_prey` is now a module-level single source: `[Add`, find with `retries=2`,
set the pin, **read `CantWalk` back from the server**, retry once, and `[Delete` what still
will not pin. It returns `pinned` / `deleted` / `lost`, and the run's last line prints the
counts **even when both are zero** — a roamer is invisible on every other field, so "no
line" and "nothing went wrong" have to be distinguishable.

Deleting is this path's requirement and not the artisan's: one staged prey can be warned
about, a per-cycle spawner cannot. A deleted spawn is replaced next cycle.

5 mutants, 5 killed. 1673 -> **1674 tests**, ruff clean.

### 55.3 A hypothesis raised and withdrawn in the same hour

Before reading the `foes=` distances, `max_flee_steps` was diagnosed as a *lifetime* cap:
`_FLEE_STEPS` resets only when the hostiles clear or the wound heals, and a warrior at 4 HP
with three Ettins on him can do neither, so after five steps he would never retreat again.
A change was written that charged the budget only for steps which failed to open distance.

Its own test refuted the premise. With prey actually pinned, five steps carry the warrior
past `hostile_scan_range = 6`, the hostile list empties, and the counter resets on its own
— the cap is not a lifetime cap when the retreat is against something that cannot follow.
The observed exhaustion was the chase in §55.2, and it is fixed there. Reverted; recorded
here so it is not re-derived.

### 55.4 What this tape settled that §53.5 listed as unexplained

**"Combat effectiveness is unexplained" is retired.** With `blade=0x13FFr5` and
`plate=6/6`, the warrior killed **two Ettins** and looted them (`gold` 50 -> 212 -> 372,
`out+322.0`) — the best combat result on record, in the first ~150 ticks, before the
roamers wore it down. The earlier `kills=0` days were not a damage problem.

Still open from §53.5: it dies, and `BACK ALIVE` is still 0 (`deaths=1`, the ghost holds
`@(2585, 411)` for the rest of the run).

### 55.5 `res=`, and a bridge accusation withdrawn

§53.5 put the never-resurrecting ghost in the sibling repo: *"Whether a ServUO ghost can be
moved by the bridge's pathfinder at all is the open question, and it is in the sibling
repo."* Two things say that was asserted without evidence:

- `crates/anima-net/src/scene/walk.rs` already threads `player_is_ghost(world)` through the
  blocking checks in three places, so the body's pathfinder is explicitly ghost-aware.
- The evidence offered here was `steps=` frozen after death. `steps=` counts emitted
  `Walk` actions and `RecoverDeath` emits `WalkTo`, so it is structurally blind to this —
  the same class of mistake §37/§38 catalogued for the other alarms.

What the tape does show is `skill=recover` running every sample with the ghost's position
never changing. Two causes remain, with opposite owners, and nothing separated them.

`res=` does: `res=none` means `RecoverDeath` never got a healer (a wiring problem here),
`res=(2575,408)@d12` frozen while `skill=recover` means the brain is asking and the body is
not moving the ghost (the sibling repo). It reads the key through `recovery._first_point`,
the skill's own normaliser — runners store `[(x, y)]` and a second parser here would print
`none` for a healer that IS wired, sending the blame to the wrong repo on the strength of a
field added to prevent exactly that.

**Prediction, before the next death.** `res=(2575,408)@d12` with `d` frozen. If instead it
reads `res=none`, the §53.6 wiring never reached the hunt agent and the fix is one line in
this repo.

## 56. The rule sent a bleeding warrior to the bank (2026-08-24)

### 56.1 The tape

`~/anima-logs/warrior-20260824-0317-pinned.log`, the first run with prey that actually
pins. It is also the best warrior day on record up to the last quarter: **3 kills, 519
gold looted, `out+469.0`**, `foes=d1,d1,d2` stable across 25 samples (§55's fix holding),
`plate=6/6`, `blade=0x13FFr5`.

Then, from t≈490 to t=579:

```
Bram0:economy want=bank_gold admitted=bank_gold@89/120 ... foes=d1,d1,d1 bandages=103
Bram0  swordsman @(2587,409) t=503 hp=36/150  trip=bank_return to=(2587,408) d=1>0 stall=4/6
                             ...    hp=1/150  (ninety ticks, one tile, 103 bandages unused)
```

Both existing alarms fired correctly — `NO PROGRESS for 80/120 ticks`, then `FRAME
OVERDUE` — and the warrior died anyway, holding the gold it had earned.

### 56.2 Two defects, and neither is the exit-edge hold

**(a) The rule itself wanted the errand.** `holding` requires `mode != "economy"` — the
hold only exists to finish a transaction the rule has STOPPED wanting. Here the rule kept
answering `bank_gold` all the way down, because `gold > bank_reserve` was true at 26% HP
with three creatures in melee exactly as it is at full health. `decide_mode`'s docstring
says its order "reflects what actually stops a warrior living", and the thing that stops
one hardest was simply not in the list.

Fixed at the top of `decide_mode`, directly under the `dead` branch: below
`WarriorSurvive.heal_below_fraction` the answer is `("hunt", None)` — the fight-or-heal
window owns him. The threshold is read off the class the profession installs, so the rule
and the reflex cannot drift on the number, and there is deliberately **no second
hysteresis**: `Survive` already owns the recovery ceiling and a copy of it in the
orchestrator would be a second opinion on the same question.

`hits_max == 0` is treated as NO READING rather than a wound — every observation carries
it before the shard's first status packet, and every offline fixture leaves it there.
Without that guard 17 tests turn red, which is the cheap version of stranding a warrior on
a shard.

4 mutants, 4 killed.

**(b) `bank_return` walked at a tile a creature was standing on.** `to=(2587,408) d=1>0
stall=4/6` for the whole ninety ticks: the return leg wants the staging tile at reach 0,
and the village pins prey on that tile's cardinals — one had been staged on the tile
itself. `d=` can never reach 0, so the frame could only ever expire. NOT FIXED; §32's
`trip=` is what made it legible and this is the second time it has paid for itself.

### 56.3 What `Survive` was doing, and the field added to find out

`Survive` sits above the capabilities in the ECONOMY planner too — `test_life_frame_hold`
has a whole fixture built on that (*"a wounded character's economy agent is ticked every
tick while its capability FSM is never stepped"*) — so it should have bandaged him and
demonstrably did not. Offline, `WarriorSurvive` handed the exact live observation (39/150,
three hostiles at d1, 103 bandages) answers `can_run=True` and steps a `Walk`. So the live
divergence is elsewhere, and **no field on the line could say where**: `steps` counts only
`Walk` (not `WalkTo`), `eps` and `out+` only move on rewarded terminals, and the walk
route's own `stall=` only advances when the route code runs. Every one of them is blind in
the same direction — "emitting nothing" and "emitting the same non-`Walk` action forever"
read identically.

`act=<Action>x<run length>` now prints what the ticked agent actually emitted and for how
many consecutive ticks. **Prediction for its first tape:** if the ninety-tick freeze
repeats, `act=` reads a single non-`Walk` name with a run length in the dozens — most
likely `Use`, a bandage FSM re-arming against a cursor that never opens. If instead it
reads `none`, no skill produced an action at all and the fault is above the planner.

Fixing (a) may prevent the state from recurring at all, in which case this prediction goes
unscored — that is the acceptable outcome, and the field stays for the next freeze.

### 56.4 Two readout fields were lying, both mine, both from this week

- `skill=` read `hunt_agent.last_skill_name` and so printed `skill=hunt` through all
  ninety economy ticks. `WarriorLife.last_skill_name` already forwards the LAST-TICKED
  agent and documents why (a carpenter measured 2994 econ ticks against 6 hunt ones). Now
  read through it.
- The corrected reading of `bandages=103 -> 0` in the same tape: that is death emptying
  the pack, not consumption. The first pass through this log recorded "the warrior ran out
  of bandages", which was exactly backwards — it died with all of them.

1674 -> **1675 tests**, ruff clean.

## 57. The warrior survived a full day (2026-08-24)

### 57.1 The first one

`~/anima-logs/warrior-20260824-0335-bleed.log`, 1200 ticks, `--warriors 1`. **`DIED 0`,
`deaths=0`, alive at the budget with `hp=77/150`.** Fourteen live warrior days preceded it
and every single one ended in a corpse.

The HP trace is the sawtooth this profession was supposed to produce, **six times**:

```
150 -> 58(t=228) -> 110 | -> 23(t=666) -> 73 | -> 56 -> 89 | -> 56 -> 88 | -> 52 -> 71 | -> 56 -> 90(t=1162)
```

3 kills, `out+346.0`, and `prey: 0 lost, 0 deleted for not pinning` — §55's counter
reporting an honest zero on a run where nothing roamed.

The lever was §56's one branch: below `heal_below_fraction` the rule stops answering
`bank_gold` and the warrior belongs to the fight-or-heal window. Every earlier death was
some version of being kept on an errand while bleeding.

### 57.2 §56.3's prediction, scored — on its second branch

The prediction named two outcomes for `act=`. The tape gave the second, verbatim:
`act=nonex106` — **no action at all** for a hundred and six consecutive ticks, not a
non-`Walk` action repeating. So the fault is above the planner's action, and the freeze is
not a bandage FSM re-arming.

Read with `skill=`, now fixed to follow the ticked agent, the window says:
`want=None admitted=bank_gold@89/120+hold ... skill=bank_gold act=nonex106`. The RULE had
correctly stopped wanting the errand (§56 working) and the exit-edge hold put the warrior
back into economy mode anyway — which is precisely what the hold is for. `Survive` sits
above the capabilities there and was NOT selected, so `Survive.can_run` returned False,
and its last line is `return ctx.obs.pending_target is None`.

**A hypothesis, and the field to test it before acting on it.** A cursor the work skill
opened and never answers would disable the survival reflex silently and stall the FSM at
the same time — one cause, both symptoms. `ui=tgt`/`ui=g1`/`ui=clear` now prints it.

An override making survival beat the hold was written and **reverted before shipping**: it
turned five tests red, two of them the live-proven bound-3 gate, whose whole mechanism is
wounding a character below the heal trigger to starve the FSM. Breaking a live-proven
bound on an unverified hypothesis is the trade this project does not make. The frame's own
deadline released it in the end — bound 2 doing its job — and the warrior recovered from
23 HP.

### 57.3 What is now the blocker: the economy leg earns nothing

The day ends `admitted=bank_gold@474/120!overdue`, `landed=0/26`, `banked=0`, holding 396
gold. `trip=bank to=(2587,420) d=9>2 stall=3/6` is FROZEN — `d=` never falls and the
stall counter stopped at 3 of 6, so `_market_walk_toward` ran three times and was never
called again. `NO PROGRESS` fired 15 times and was right every time.

So the warrior now lives and fights, and after its first three kills it banks nothing for
the remaining two-thirds of the day. That is the next thing to fix, and `ui=` is in place
to say whether it is the same open surface as §57.2.

1674 -> **1675 tests**, ruff clean.

## 58. Every shop was on the far side of a cliff (2026-08-24)

### 58.1 One cause under three "separate" blockers

`run_warrior_village` staged its four shops at a flat `+/-12` from the hunting pocket and
never asked the map whether that was ground a character can walk. `HUNTING_SPOT`
`(2587, 408)` is a z=15 plateau **ringed by cliffs**:

| ray | z profile out to 12 | round-trip walkable |
|---|---|---|
| weapon E | 15 15 15 10 −15 … | **3** |
| healer W | 15 15 15 15 14 12 25 48 59 62 65 62 | **6** |
| banker S | 15 15 15 15 16 26 31 41 43 56 60 69 | **5** |
| armorer N | 15 15 15 15 15 −2 −15 … | **5** |

ServUO allows a land step only when `startZ + StepHeight >= landZ`
(`Scripts/Services/Pathing/Movement.cs`, `StepHeight = 2`). The banker sat **54 z above**
the warrior behind a `+10` step; the healer behind a `+13`.

That single fact explains three things this file has been carrying as separate open items:

1. **`banked=0` on every warrior day.** The 2026-08-24 tape shows it exactly: every
   `bank_gold` frame gives up at `age=18/120`, having walked `(2587,408) -> (2587,412)`
   — four tiles of twelve — and turned back. `landed=0/17`, 522 gold in the pack.
2. **No vendor purchase has ever happened**, so `buy_bandage`/`buy_weapon`/`buy_armor`
   have never been exercised live.
3. **`BACK ALIVE` = 0 across fifteen days.** §55.5 had narrowed this to "the brain is
   asking and the body is not walking the ghost", with `res=(2575,408)@d12` on the tape.
   The healer at `gx - 12` is behind the `+13` step. The ghost was being sent somewhere
   it cannot go — a runner bug, in this repo, and **not the sibling repo** that §53.5
   blamed and §55.5 was leaning toward.

Descent deserves its own line, because it is the trap in the obvious fix: ServUO does not
cap descent, so the east ray is walkable all the way to `z = -15`. It is a **one-way
trip** — the way home is a +25 climb. `walkable_run` therefore bounds the step in BOTH
directions, and the east shop lands at 3 tiles rather than 12.

### 58.2 The fix

`uomap.walkable_run(map, x, y, dx, dy, max_len, climb=STEP_HEIGHT)` returns how many steps
along a ray stay round-trip walkable. `run_warrior_village` places each shop at the last
tile that answers, prints the four gaps **on every run, even when nothing moved**, and
says loudly when a leg falls under `_VENDOR_MIN_GAP = 3` (one tile outside the ring the
prey is pinned on) — that leg simply cannot work and the tape must not have to be
reverse-engineered to learn it.

5 mutants, 5 killed, including the two that matter: dropping `abs()` (which reinstates the
one-way cliff) and the map-edge guard.

A test pins the finding to the real map files, so if the pocket ever stops being walled in
the suite says so rather than leaving §58 quietly false.

### 58.3 What this does NOT yet show

Nothing here has run on a shard. The prediction for the next day, written before it:
`vendor gaps (walkable, max 12): weapon=3 healer=6 banker=5 armorer=5`, `banked` non-zero
for the first time, and — if the warrior dies at all — the first `BACK ALIVE`. The
refuting branch is `banked=0` with `bank_gold` frames still giving up around age 18, which
would mean the walk fails for a reason the ground does not explain.

1675 -> **1677 tests**, ruff clean.

### 58.4 §58.3's prediction, scored on its main branch

`~/anima-logs/warrior-20260824-0356-reach.log`, 1200 ticks.

The staging line came out verbatim as predicted:
`vendor gaps (walkable, max 12): weapon=3 healer=6 banker=5 armorer=5`.

And the half that mattered:

```
FRAME RETIRED bank_gold#1 age=10/120 -> achieved
FRAME RETIRED bank_gold#2 age=... -> achieved
banked=325   landed=2/2   kills=4   out+651.0   deaths=0   hp=81/150 at BUDGET SPENT
```

**The first gold a warrior has ever banked**, in two trips, both achieved, each closing in
about ten ticks against a 120-tick budget — against sixteen prior days of `banked=0` and,
on the day before, seventeen consecutive `bank_gold` frames giving up at `age=18/120`.
`out+651.0` is the best reward on record; the previous best was `out+469.0`.

Second consecutive day ending alive. The refuting branch — `banked=0` with frames still
giving up around age 18 — did not occur.

**Still 0: `BACK ALIVE`,** for the mundane reason that the warrior did not die. The healer
is now 6 tiles away instead of 12 behind a `+13` step, and `res=(2581,408)@d5` shows
`RecoverDeath` pointing at the moved one, but nothing has tested the ghost walk yet. §58.1's
third claim is therefore *diagnosed and unverified*, not closed.

`NO PROGRESS` fired 13 times and every one was a false positive of a kind now legible:
`act=Attackx21` says the warrior is swinging at pinned prey from a fixed tile, so reward,
steps, speech and position all freeze between kills that are ~200 ticks apart. The alarm's
40-tick threshold is simply far too tight for this profession. Logged, not fiddled with —
§37 already recorded that the obvious fix to that pulse is wrong.

## 59. Nothing walked the warrior into reach (2026-08-24)

### 59.1 The third day alive, and where 831 of its 1200 ticks went

`~/anima-logs/warrior-20260824-0407-repeat.log` reproduced §58: `DIED 0`, `banked=122`,
`landed=1/1` (the `bank_gold` frame achieved), 3 kills. Three consecutive days ending
alive is no longer a fluke.

It also ended `[BUDGET SPENT · STALLED 831]`:

```
foes=d2  act=Attackx831  steps=8  !stalled   @(2587,408) for the whole window
```

**Eight hundred and thirty-one consecutive `Attack` packets at a target two tiles away.**

### 59.2 The defect

`Combat.step` emits `WarMode` once and then `Attack(target)` forever. It never walks, and
`Hunt` extends it without adding a walk either. **The server does not move the player into
range** — an `Attack` from outside reach is a packet that does nothing.

This has been true since `Combat` was written and never showed, because
`run_warrior_village` spawns its prey **adjacent**. The moment the warrior steps off its
stand — for a bank trip, or a retreat — the fight is over for the rest of the day. §58
fixing the bank trip is what made the warrior leave the tile often enough to expose it.

`Combat` now closes: beyond `melee_reach` (1; a ranged profession raises it) it steps
toward the target instead of swinging. Bounded by `approach_stall_limit = 6` consecutive
ticks without the position changing, after which the ordinary `Attack` goes out exactly as
before — a walled-off target must not become an infinite loop, and a target that wanders
back into reach is still engaged.

5 mutants, 5 killed. One of them, `arrival clears`, initially SURVIVED: arrival was
resetting the budget two ways (clearing the counter *and* clearing the remembered
position, whose absence reads as "moved"), so either could be deleted with the tests
still green. Now one statement owns it.

### 59.3 Prediction, before the run

`act=` should stop showing long `Attack` runs — the cadence becomes `Walk`/`Attack`
alternating as the warrior closes — `steps` should climb far above 8, `!stalled` should
not appear, and kills should rise because more of the day is spent in contact.

The refuting branch: repeated `Walk` runs ending at exactly `approach_stall_limit`, which
would mean the approach is being *blocked* rather than being absent — a different problem
(terrain or a body in the way) that this change cannot fix.

1677 -> **1678 tests**, ruff clean.

## 60. The idle band between the two heal thresholds (2026-08-24)

### 60.1 §59.3 scored, and what it exposed next

The approach fix worked on its own terms: `steps` went from 8 to 186, `!stalled` never
appeared, and the run banked 157 with 4 kills by t=494 — better than any full day before
it. Neither branch of the prediction described what actually happened next.

At t=494 the tape reads:

```
skill=wander  foes=d17  @(2594,412)  hp=96/150  act=Walkx169
```

The warrior had **left the hunting pocket**. It had cleared the prey, nothing was inside
`engage_range = 10`, so `Hunt.can_run` was false and `Wander` — the planner's always-true
fallback — took the hands and walked it away.

### 60.2 The defect: a band where nothing can happen

`ready_to_fight` gated the monitor's prey top-up on
`hits / hits_max >= WarriorSurvive.heal_until_fraction` (0.75). But `Survive` only ENTERS
its heal window below `heal_below_fraction` (0.40). Between the two — 40% to 75%, which is
where a warrior spends most of a working day — it is not healing, is perfectly able to
fight, and was fed nothing.

The gate's own comment claimed it "stops feeding it exactly while it is healing and
resumes the moment it is ready". A fraction cannot express that; the reflex's **latch**
can, and it already exists: `Survive._HEAL_LATCH` is set on entering the window and
cleared on reaching the ceiling. The gate now reads it.

`_ready_to_fight` was a closure inside `run_warrior_village` and therefore untestable —
the same shape as `_spawn_pinned` in §55 — so it is now module-level `ready_to_fight`.
The extraction also caught a latent bug written minutes earlier: the new clause read
`w["life"]`, the enclosing loop variable, instead of its own `life` parameter. On a
one-warrior roster that is invisible; on any larger one it consults the wrong warrior.
`ruff` cannot see it, because the closure makes it a legal read.

5 mutants, **4 killed**. `none gate` — deleting the explicit `o is None` check — survives,
and honestly so: the function's catch-all `except` returns False for the resulting
`AttributeError`, so no test can distinguish the two paths by their answer. The explicit
check stays because it is the documented contract; the net is for the unforeseen.

### 60.3 Prediction, before the run

Prey should keep arriving through the mid-HP band, so `skill=wander` should be rare and
`foes=` should stay in single digits; kills should exceed the 4 of the previous run over
the full 1200 ticks, and `banked` should exceed 157.

Refuting branch: the warrior still drifts, which would mean `Wander` is taking the hands
for some reason other than an empty pocket — the leash, or `Hunt.can_run` failing with
prey actually present.

1678 -> **1679 tests**, ruff clean.

## 61. Feeding the mid-HP band killed the warrior nine times (2026-08-24)

### 61.1 §60.3's prediction: right about the drift, silent about what mattered

`~/anima-logs/warrior-20260824-0427-feed.log`. The predicted half held —
`skill=wander` **0 times** all day, `foes=` in single digits throughout, `!stalled` 0,
`kills=5`, `banked=321`, `out+817.0` (a new best). The named refuting branch (still
drifting) did not occur either.

The outcome was a third thing the prediction did not name: **`deaths=9`**, against
`deaths=0` on each of the three preceding days.

### 61.2 Two changes, one run, and no way to attribute it

That run carried BOTH §59's approach step and §60's latch gate, and they interact:

- §60 keeps prey arriving through 40–75% HP, where the warrior used to be left alone. The
  old 0.75 gate was functioning as a **rest window** the profession does not otherwise
  have — `Survive` only enters its heal window below 0.40, so nothing else stops a warrior
  fighting continuously at half health.
- §59 lets the warrior CLOSE on a target, and the top-up spawned beside his LIVE tile. The
  two compose into a random walk: chase the creature two tiles away, get another beside
  the new tile, repeat. He ended at `@(2581,403)`, seven tiles off his stand, and **8 of
  10 `bank_gold` frames gave up** because each trip started somewhere the route was not
  built for (`trip=bank to=(2587,413) d=10>2`).

So `deaths=9` has two candidate causes and this run cannot separate them.

### 61.3 One change, then measure

Top-up now spawns at the **stand**, not at the warrior, and the presence count that drives
it counts creatures in the POCKET rather than near the warrior — otherwise stepping away
from perfectly good prey refills, and then he has both. The approach step is exactly what
makes this safe: a warrior can now walk to anything inside `engage_range`, which is why
the spawner no longer has to put one under his feet.

§60's latch gate is left IN, so the next run isolates the drift.

**Prediction.** If drift was the killer, `deaths` returns to 0 or near it and the
`bank_gold` give-ups collapse toward the 1–2 per day of §58. If `deaths` stays high with
the warrior standing on its own stand, the killer is §60 — continuous prey through the
mid-HP band with no rest window — and the gate goes back to the fraction, accepting the
idle band as the cheaper failure.

### 61.4 The isolation run, and the wall it revealed

`~/anima-logs/warrior-20260824-0438-stand.log`, cut short at t≈790 once the answer was
in. **`deaths=0`** with the warrior standing on `@(2587,408)` — so §61.2's first candidate
is the right one: **the nine deaths were the drift**, not §60's latch gate. The gate stays.

But moving the top-up to the stand traded one failure for another: **11 of 11 `bank_gold`
frames gave up**, several at `d=3>2` — three tiles from a banker the warrior could see.
The prey is PINNED and the spawner offered it the four CARDINAL neighbours, and every shop
this village stages sits due N/S/E/W of the stand (§58). A pinned Ettin at `(2587, 409)`
is therefore a permanent wall across the only southern corridor.

Prey now spawns on the four DIAGONALS. They are still chebyshev 1, so `Combat` engages
them exactly as before and the fight is unchanged; the cardinal exits stay open.

**Prediction.** `deaths` stays at 0 and `bank_gold` returns to achieving — §58's run
retired two frames at `age=10/120`, so anything near that is the target. Refuting branch:
give-ups continue at `d=3>2`, which would mean the corridor is blocked by something other
than the prey and the `_market_walk_toward` greedy step is the real limit.

### 61.5 The diagonal prediction, refuted, and what the tape showed instead

`~/anima-logs/warrior-20260824-0446-diag.log`: **`landed=0/8`, still nothing banked.**
The refuting branch, as written.

The tape says why, and it is not the corridor:

```
Bram0:economy ... skill=bank_gold ... foes=d1,d1,d1
@(2587,408) trip=bank to=(2587,413) d=5>2 stall=4/6 act=Walkx5
```

Five `Walk` actions emitted from the warrior's own stand, position unchanged, three
creatures at chebyshev 1. The warrior is **boxed in**. `[Add` placement is approximate —
§55 already recorded `foes=d0`, a creature standing on the warrior's own tile — so no
choice of "harmless" adjacent tile is safe when the creature is then PINNED there.

`_PREY_GAP = 2` moves the prey off every one of the stand's eight neighbours. §59's
approach step is what makes that possible at all: before it, prey had to be spawned under
the warrior's feet or he never swung. The gap is checked per diagonal with
`walkable_run`, and a pocket where no diagonal reaches it says so rather than quietly
staging nothing.

**Prediction.** `stall=` on a bank trip should stop pegging at 4–6 from the stand, `d=`
should fall, and `bank_gold` should achieve again as it did in §58. Refuting branch: the
warrior still cannot leave `(2587,408)` with all eight neighbours empty, which would put
the fault in `_market_walk_toward` itself rather than in anything standing on the ground.

### 61.6 `BACK ALIVE`, at last — and it closes §53.5's oldest open item

The same refuted diagonal run carried the day's real result, on a line nobody predicted:

```
** Bram0: DIED at (2587,408) — death #1 **
** Bram0: BACK ALIVE at (2583,408) after 10 ticks dead (death #1) **
```

**The first resurrection in this project's history.** `BACK ALIVE` had been 0 across
sixteen live warrior days.

Nothing about `RecoverDeath` changed to make it happen. §58 moved the Healer from
`gx - 12` — behind a `+13` z step the ghost could not climb — to `gx - 6`, and the ghost
walked there and was offered the gump. The tape's `res=(2581,408)@d6` -> `@d2` is the walk
itself, and ServUO's `BaseHealer.OnMovement` resurrects within 2 tiles.

So the chain of attributions ends correctly:

- §53.5 put it in the sibling repo: *"whether a ServUO ghost can be moved by the bridge's
  pathfinder at all is the open question"*. **Wrong**, and asserted on `steps=` — a
  counter structurally blind to `WalkTo` (§57.2).
- §55.5 added `res=` to separate "no healer wired" from "the body will not walk the
  ghost", and predicted the second. **Also wrong**, in a more useful way: the field showed
  `res=(2575,408)@d12`, a healer that WAS wired, and that is what made the third
  possibility visible.
- §58.1 predicted the cliff, from the map rather than from the tape. **Right**, and now
  live-proven.

The body walks ghosts fine. The runner was sending it somewhere no one can go.

### 61.7 §61.5's prediction, confirmed — the best day on record

`~/anima-logs/warrior-20260824-0453-gap.log`, 1200 ticks:

```
banked=630   landed=4/4   kills=5   out+956.0   deaths=0   hp=109/150 at BUDGET SPENT
prey: 0 lost, 0 deleted for not pinning
DIED 0 · BACK ALIVE 0 · !stalled 0 · NOTHING LANDS 0 · WEDGED WALK 0
```

Four `bank_gold` frames, **all four achieved** — the first day with a 100% economy leg.
`banked` doubled the previous best (325) and `out+956.0` beat it by half again. The
warrior finished at 73% HP with prey still pinned two tiles away, holding the retained
reserve of 376.

The refuting branch — still unable to leave the stand with all eight neighbours empty,
which would have put the fault in `_market_walk_toward` itself — did not occur.

`NO PROGRESS` fired 15 times and remains the known false positive for this profession
(§58.4): kills are ~200 ticks apart, the fight happens from one tile, so the pulse freezes
between them. `act=` distinguishes it; the 40-tick threshold does not fit a warrior.

### 61.8 The five days, in order

| day | change under test | deaths | banked | landed | kills |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0335 bleed | §56 bleeding outranks errands | **0** | 0 | 0/26 | 3 |
| 0356 reach | §58 shops on walkable ground | 0 | 325 | 2/2 | 4 |
| 0407 repeat | reproducibility | 0 | 122 | 1/1 | 3 |
| 0419 close→0427 feed | §59 approach + §60 latch gate | 9 | 321 | 2/10 | 5 |
| 0438 stand | §61.3 spawn at the stand (isolation) | 0 | 0 | 0/11 | 3 |
| 0446 diag | §61.4 diagonals | 2 | 0 | 0/8 | 3 |
| 0453 gap | §61.5 prey two tiles off the stand | **0** | **630** | **4/4** | 5 |

The two bad days are both the same lesson from opposite sides: a PINNED creature is a
permanent wall, and where the spawner puts it decides whether the warrior can leave —
either dragged away from its stand (0427, nine deaths) or barricaded onto it (0438/0446,
19 give-ups and nothing banked).

### 61.9 The reproduction run: four deaths, and the variance is the finding

`~/anima-logs/warrior-20260824-0503-confirm.log`, same binary, same command, cut short
once the answer was clear: **`deaths=4`, `banked=0`, `landed=0/0`, `kills=2`.**

§61.7's clean 630 does **not** reproduce. One good day is not a working warrior, and this
run is the reason the goal was not called met on it.

What the tape says:

- **The resurrection loop works and is now the visible failure.** `BACK ALIVE` fired
  **three times** (11, 16 and 17 ticks dead), each time at the Healer — so §61.6 is not a
  one-off. But the warrior comes back naked (gear on the corpse), `Combat.can_run` is true
  for anything inside `engage_range = 10`, the pocket is 6 tiles away, and it walks back
  in unarmoured and dies again. Three deaths at the same tile, `(2583,408)`.
- **The first death was a monotone decline**, 149 -> 12 with no sawtooth, while `Survive`
  ran (21 samples) and spent bandages (103 -> 96 and on). It was losing the arithmetic,
  not failing to try.
- **`foes=d1,d1,d1` — three Ettins in melee, against `prey_target = 2`.** `[Add` placement
  is approximate (§55 recorded `foes=d0`), so a creature that lands outside the tight
  radius the presence count used is invisible to it and the spawner adds another. Three
  attackers is a different fight from two: every hit taken slips the bandage.

The count now uses `WarriorSurvive.hostile_scan_range` — the radius the warrior's own
reflex uses to decide it is in danger — so the top-up asks the question it is actually
about.

**Prediction.** `foes=` should stop showing three at chebyshev 1, and the death rate should
fall toward the 0 of §61.7 rather than the 4 here. Refuting branch: `foes=d1,d1,d1` again,
which would mean the extra creature is not an over-supply at all and the two-Ettin fight
is simply not survivable at these skills.

**Not addressed, and now the top of the list:** a naked resurrected warrior walks straight
back into the fight. `RecoverDeath` recovers the corpse, but nothing stops `Combat`
engaging while unarmoured — and §53's own living-test findings recorded this exact
"remote-death naked loop" years of tape ago.

### 61.10 §61.9's prediction refuted, and the roamers found

`~/anima-logs/warrior-20260824-0511-cap.log`, cut short at t≈741. `foes=d1,d1,d1` still
dominated (39 of the first 45 samples), so widening the presence radius was not the answer
— the refuting branch, as written. `deaths=0` and `banked=195` up to that point, but the
warrior was at `hp=19/150` with three creatures on it.

The clue is WHERE: `@(2593,415)`, six tiles off the stand. `_spawn_pinned` only ever adds
at the stand's own diagonals, so **by construction no PINNED creature can be there.**
Those three were roaming.

`stage_pinned_prey` reads the pin back from the server (§55) and reported `0 lost, 0
deleted` all day, and both statements are true at once because of what it looked up:

```python
mob = gm.find_mobile_near(x, y, exclude={<player serials>})
```

`find_mobile_near` returns the NEAREST match. With only players excluded it happily
returns a creature that was **already** pinned on that tile — re-pins it, reads `CantWalk`
back as `True`, reports `pinned` — while the one just `[Add`-ed is never touched and
roams. Every cycle can add one more. §55's counter cannot see this: it counts the ones it
knows it failed on, and this one it believes it succeeded on.

The caller now excludes every mobile in the current observation, not just the players.

This is the third distinct way the same three lines have gone wrong (§55 the missing
read-back, §61.4/§61.5 the placement, now the lookup), and each one presented as "the
warrior keeps dying" rather than as a staging bug.

**Prediction.** `foes=` should stop showing creatures more than `_PREY_GAP + 1` tiles from
the stand; the count in melee should sit at `prey_target`; and the run-to-run variance
between §61.7's clean 630 and §61.9's four deaths should narrow. Refuting branch: roamers
persist, which would mean `[Add` is placing creatures somewhere other than the tile it is
given and the spawner needs to verify position, not just identity.

### 61.11 §61.10's prediction, confirmed — and the best day by a factor of two

`~/anima-logs/warrior-20260824-0519-roam.log`, 1200 ticks:

```
banked=1297   landed=6/7   kills=9   out+1623.0   deaths=0   hp=82/150 at BUDGET SPENT
skill=warrior_hunt   prey: 0 lost, 0 deleted
DIED 0 · BACK ALIVE 0 · !stalled 0 · WEDGED WALK 0 · NOTHING LANDS 0
```

Against the previous best (§61.7: 630 banked, 5 kills, 4/4 frames) that is roughly double
on every axis. Six of seven `bank_gold` frames achieved; the seventh expired on its
deadline rather than wedging.

Two changes were in this run — the roamer fix and `WarriorHunt` — and they are not
separable from it. What can be said: the roamers were a *staging* bug whose whole
signature was creatures where none could be pinned, and `WarriorHunt` only refuses a state
(bare-handed) that this run never entered, since `deaths=0`. The most likely reading is
that the roamers were most of it.

**Still visible, and now the top of the list:** at t=863 the tape reads
`want=None admitted=bank_gold@84/120+hold ... hp=31/150 foes=d1,d1,d1 act=nonex68`. §56's
branch correctly stopped wanting the errand and the **exit-edge hold put it back**, exactly
as §57.2 described. Bound 2 — the frame's own deadline — released it in time again, and
the warrior recovered. That is luck bounded by 120 ticks, not a design.

§57.2 records why the obvious fix was reverted: a survival override reddens the
live-proven bound-3 gate, whose mechanism is wounding BELOW the heal trigger to starve the
FSM. The resolution is now known and already applied to two sibling tests in
`test_life_frame_hold.py` — wound into the HYSTERESIS BAND instead (`Survive` latches at
0.40 and heals to 0.75, so anywhere between the two still starves the FSM while the
orchestrator's survival branch stays quiet). `anima2/live_frame_overdue_gate.py` and its
two offline tests would need the same two-step wound: drop below the trigger once to set
the latch, then raise into the band.

### 61.12 The hold does not outlive the warrior, and §57.2's revert is resolved

`_being_killed` now releases the exit-edge hold, alongside the death-episode override it
sits next to. It is the same rule one step earlier: a transaction cannot be worth
finishing over the corpse of the character finishing it.

**Both halves are load-bearing**, and that is what makes it shippable where §57.2's
version was not. That one tested only the wound, and reddened the live-proven bound-3 gate
— whose entire mechanism is wounding a TINKER below the trigger by GM fiat to starve its
capability FSM, **with no hostile anywhere on the shard**. A character nobody can reach is
not dying however low the bar reads, and the hold is still the right answer for it. The
attacker clause makes both statements true at once, and the gate's two offline tests pass
untouched.

Thresholds are read off the survival reflex the Life actually installs
(`hunt_agent.planner.skills[0]`), not off `WarriorSurvive` by name, so a profession with a
different reflex gets its own numbers and the rule cannot drift from it. `hits_max == 0`
is an absent reading, not a wound.

5 mutants, 5 killed — including all three that survived the first pass, each of which was
a half of the rule the test could not see: a hostile with no wound, a wound with the
attacker beyond `hostile_scan_range`, and the missing-reading guard.

1680 -> **1681 tests**, ruff clean.

### 61.13 Reproduction of the 1297 day: the earnings hold, the bank leg does not

`~/anima-logs/warrior-20260824-0529-verify.log`, same binary as §61.11 (roamer fix +
`WarriorHunt`, before the hold release):

```
banked=1074   kills=10   out+1566.0   deaths=2   landed=1/7   hp=69/150 at BUDGET SPENT
BACK ALIVE 1  ·  !stalled 0  ·  WEDGED WALK 0
```

So the earnings reproduce — 1074 against 1297, 10 kills against 9, both far above every
day before them — and **the bank leg does not**: 1 of 7 frames achieved here against 6 of
7 there. The remaining variance lives in the trip, not in the fight.

Two things this run showed that no earlier one could:

- **The full death cycle works end to end.** `DIED` -> `BACK ALIVE` -> corpse recovered
  (`plate=0/6(pack 6)` — the suit back in the pack) -> re-equipped (`plate=6/6` by t=1180).
  Nothing was left naked in a loop; `WarriorHunt` held the line while `Survive` had the
  hands, and `EquipArmor` dressed him once the wound closed. That is the "remote-death
  naked loop" `docs/SWORD-WARRIOR.md` recorded years of tape ago, closed.
- **The give-ups are `bank_return`, not `bank`.** `trip=bank_return to=(2588,409) d=6>0`
  — a return leg to a `bs_stand` frozen at wherever the trip opened, the same shape
  `docs/OBSERVATIONS.md` recorded for Pim on 2026-08-18 and §48 fixed for the forge pair.
  The warrior drifts while fighting, so its "home" is stale by the time the trip ends.
  That is the next thing to fix and it is a KNOWN class, not a new mystery.

## 62. The warrior parked four tiles from home and earned nothing (2026-08-24)

### 62.1 The failure §59 created and nothing bounded

`~/anima-logs/warrior-20260824-0539-hold.log`, with every fix through §61.12:

```
deaths=0   kills=6   landed=2/36   out+766.0   @(2591,414) at BUDGET SPENT
DIED 0 · !stalled 0 · WEDGED WALK 0 · NOTHING LANDS 0
```

Perfectly alive, perfectly safe, and **thirty-four `bank_gold` frames given up** — all of
them at `d=4`, from one tile, `@(2591,414)`. Both legs failed there: `trip=bank
to=(2587,413) d=4>2` on the way out and `trip=bank_return to=(2587,411) d=4>0` on the way
back, with a pinned creature at `foes=d1` that `market.py`'s greedy straight-line walk
cannot route around.

§59 taught `Combat` to close on a target. Nothing pulled the warrior back afterwards: it
chased, retreated, chased the next thing from where it landed, and parked. And
`run_warrior_village` turned out to be **the only runner in the file that never called
`set_leash`** — so `wander_home` was unset and even `Wander`'s own leash was inert.

### 62.2 Two bounds, one radius

- `WarriorHunt` refuses to ENGAGE from outside the leash. The clause is deliberately in
  `can_run`, above nothing and below `Survive`/`RecoverDeath`, so fleeing past the leash
  stays legal and only coming back stops being optional. It reads `wander_leash` through
  the same clamped single source `Wander` uses, so a knob moves both.
- The runner sets `wander_home` to the stand, with the radius `min(reach.values())` — the
  SHORTEST walkable shop ray from §58, floored at `_VENDOR_MIN_GAP`. Inside that circle
  every shop this village staged is reachable by the only kind of walk `market.py` has;
  outside it, nothing is guaranteed. The number is derived from the terrain rather than
  chosen.

4 mutants, 4 killed.

### 62.3 Prediction

`bank_gold` give-ups should collapse — §61.11 achieved 6 of 7 when the warrior happened to
stay near its stand, and this makes staying the rule rather than the luck. Kills should
rise back toward 9–10 because the day stops being spent parked. The refuting branch is
give-ups continuing at `d=4` from a tile INSIDE the leash, which would put the fault in
the greedy walk itself and not in where the warrior stands.

1681 tests, ruff clean.

### 62.4 §62.3 scored: deaths back to zero, and one accounting defect left standing

`~/anima-logs/warrior-20260824-0551-leash.log`:

```
banked=904   kills=10   deaths=0   landed=0/6   out+1375.2   hp=58/150 at BUDGET SPENT
DIED 0 · !stalled 0 · WEDGED WALK 0 · NOTHING LANDS 0
```

The warrior held `@(2587..2588, 409..411)` — one to three tiles off its stand — for the
whole day. No parking, and `deaths` back to 0.

Three consecutive days now:

| run | banked | kills | deaths | landed |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0519 roam | 1297 | 9 | 0 | 6/7 |
| 0529 verify | 1074 | 11 | 2 | 1/7 |
| 0551 leash | 904 | 10 | **0** | 0/6 |

Against sixteen preceding days of `banked=0` and a corpse.

**`landed=0/6` with 904 gold in the bank is a defect, and it is in the accounting, not the
warrior.** The deposit lands — `banked=` reads the bank box and climbed 267 -> 580 -> 904
across the day — and then the frame retires `giveup`, because `_market_return_step` walks
home at `final_reach = 0` and a warrior's `bs_stand` is not an anvil it must stand on. The
prediction's refuting branch (give-ups at `d=4` from inside the leash) did not occur; the
outbound leg now reads `d=2<=2` and arrives.

This matters beyond tidiness: `landed=` exists (§37/§38) so that "busy and completing
nothing" is visible. A false ZERO there is exactly as bad as a false alarm, and on this
tape it hides a working economy leg. Next: either the return leg gets a reach a
workbench-less profession can satisfy, or achievement stops being gated on getting home.

## 63. A deposit the warrior walked away from now counts (2026-08-24)

### 63.1 The false zero

§62.4 named it: 904 gold in the bank, `landed=0/6`, every frame `giveup`. The deposit is
real — `banked=` reads the bank box and climbed 267 -> 580 -> 904 across the day — and the
frame retires a failure anyway.

`_bank_achieved` requires `cap_bank_returned_goal_id`, and `BankGold` wrote it only on
`(pos.x, pos.y) == tuple(bs_stand)` — the EXACT tile. Right for a crafter, whose stand is
an anvil it must be standing on to resume work. Wrong for a hunter, which has no such tile
and moves while it fights, so it essentially never lands back on the square its trip opened
from.

Worse, the walk home and the arrival test were **the same constant written twice**:
`_walk_route(..., 0, ...)` and `== tuple(stand)`. That is exactly the drift class
`knobs.py` exists for — widen one and the trip either gives up forever on a journey it has
finished, or claims a homecoming that never happened.

### 63.2 One number, three defaults

- `market.bank_return_reach(memory)` is the clamped single source, and BOTH the walk and
  the arrival test call it through `_return_reach`.
- `BlacksmithMarket._return_reach` returns 0 — every crafter is byte-identical.
- `WarriorLife.DEFAULT_BANK_RETURN_REACH` is **0 as well**, deliberately: the carpenter,
  tinker, mage and woodsman all inherit that class and all have a workbench. It is also a
  real constructor parameter and a member of `KNOBS`, so `--knob bank_return_reach=N`
  reaches it like every other tuned value.
- `village.warrior_village_knobs` fills in 2 for this village only, under anything the
  caller asked for. 2 is `market.BANK_REACH`, so "home" and "at the bank" use one
  tolerance and a trip cannot be closer to arriving than to leaving.

7 mutants, 7 killed, across all three files.

**What is NOT covered offline:** an end-to-end bank trip. `MockBody` has no banker, which
is the same gap follow-up 22 records for the buy FSM, so the walk home is unit-tested at
reach 0/2/3 and the full round trip is live-only.

### 63.3 Prediction

`landed=` should stop reading 0 on a day that banks — the achieved count should track the
number of deposits `banked=` implies. Refuting branch: `landed` still 0 with gold rising,
which would mean the return leg is not what gates achievement and one of the other eleven
clauses in `_bank_achieved` is failing.

1681 -> **1684 tests**, ruff clean.

### 63.4 §63.3 confirmed — and the day it unlocked

`~/anima-logs/warrior-20260824-0947-count.log`, 1200 ticks:

```
banked=2446   landed=16/17   kills=18   out+2772.2   deaths=0   hp=83/150 at BUDGET SPENT
prey: 0 lost, 0 deleted   ·   DIED 0 · !stalled 0 · WEDGED WALK 0 · NOTHING LANDS 0
```

`landed=` tracks the deposits now: sixteen of seventeen frames achieved, most closing at
`age=8..10` of a 120-tick budget. The refuting branch — gold rising with `landed` still 0
— did not occur.

The accounting fix was not supposed to change behaviour, and it did, by a lot: **2446
banked against 904 the run before, and 18 kills against 10.** The reason is that a frame
recorded as `giveup` is a frame the give-up ladder has to walk, and every one of those
burned a trip's worth of ticks the warrior now spends fighting. A false failure is not
free.

**Seventeen live days, and the first eight of the last nine ended alive.** Progression on
the economy, in order: 0 (×16 days) -> 325 -> 122 -> 157 -> 321 -> 630 -> 1297 -> 1074 ->
904 -> **2446**.

### 63.5 Reproduced

`~/anima-logs/warrior-20260824-0958-repro.log`, same binary, same command:

```
banked=2073   landed=13/14   kills=16   out+2399.2   deaths=0   hp=91/150 at BUDGET SPENT
prey: 0 lost, 0 deleted   ·   DIED 0 · !stalled 0 · WEDGED WALK 0 · NOTHING LANDS 0
```

| run | banked | kills | deaths | landed |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0947 count | 2446 | 18 | 0 | 16/17 |
| 0958 repro | 2073 | 16 | 0 | 13/14 |

Two consecutive days within 15% of each other on every axis, both with zero deaths and
~93% of bank frames achieved. §61.9's variance — a clean day followed by four deaths and
nothing banked — is gone, and the causes it was hiding (roaming prey, the unleashed chase,
the false-failure accounting) are each closed with tests.

**This is the point at which the sword-warrior can be called working.** What remains is
listed in `CLAUDE.md` and is about coverage rather than function: no vendor purchase has
run inside a village day, there is no offline reproduction of a bank trip (`MockBody` has
no banker), `NO PROGRESS` still false-fires ~9-15x/day on this profession, and
multi-warrior rosters are untested.

## 64. Three warriors: two of them never landed a blow (2026-08-25)

### 64.1 The roster's first live day

`~/anima-logs/warrior3-20260825-0009-roster.log`, `--warriors 3`. Bram0 worked
(`out+748.3`, `landed=2/5` by t=613). Bram1 and Bram2 did not:

```
Bram1 @(2612,408) hp=150/150 landed=0/0 out+0.2 eps=2 steps=23 act=Attackx547 !stalled
Bram2 @(2637,408) hp=150/150 landed=0/0 out+0.0 eps=0 steps=46 act=Attackx525 !stalled
```

Both frozen at `foes=d2,d2,d2` — the prey exactly `_PREY_GAP` away, never closed on, never
hitting back. `!stalled` fired on both, correctly, and `act=` named the shape immediately:
five hundred consecutive `Attack`s with no walk between them.

### 64.2 The lifetime cap, for the third time

`Combat._approach` charges a stall when the position does not change and gives up at
`approach_stall_limit`. The counter reset ONLY on arriving or on moving — and a warrior
that can do neither does both never, so **six blocked steps ended its day**. Bram0's first
approach happened to land, which reset it, and that is the whole difference between the
three characters.

This is the same shape as §55.3's `max_flee_steps` reading and §61's spawn placement: a
budget that exists to bound a *futile* action, spent once and never returned. It is now
the third time this class has appeared in this profession.

Two resets, both narrow:

- **A cooldown.** `approach_retry_ticks = 30`: once the budget is spent the warrior keeps
  attacking for thirty ticks, then tries walking again. A transient block — a creature in
  the doorway, a tile a neighbour is crossing — must not end the day, and a permanent one
  must not become a walk every tick.
- **A new target is a new approach.** The old target's wall says nothing about this one's,
  and carrying the count over lets one obstacle spend the budget for every creature that
  follows.

4 mutants, 4 killed.

### 64.3 What the roster also proved, on purpose

**§58's "walled in" warning fired for the first time**, and it was right:

```
vendor gaps (walkable, max 12): weapon=12 healer=2 banker=1 armorer=8
** the pocket at (2612,408) is walled in: healer, banker under 3 tiles — that leg cannot work **
```

Bram1's pocket at `(2612,408)` has a banker ray one tile long. `run_warrior_village` stages
its roster along a straight line at `spacing = 25` and nothing has ever asked whether the
25th tile along is usable ground — with one warrior the question never came up. That is a
separate defect from §64.2 and it is NOT fixed: the runner should choose each stand rather
than count tiles, and until it does a multi-warrior roster will keep staging pockets that
cannot bank.

**Prediction for the next roster run.** With the cooldown, `foes=` should fall from
`d2,d2,d2` to `d1` on all three and `act=` should alternate `Walk`/`Attack`; Bram1 should
still fail to BANK, because its pocket is walled in and §64.2 does not touch that.

### 64.4 The real cause: two warriors were standing in a lake

§64.2's cooldown worked — `steps` went from 23/46 to 125/130 and `act=` started alternating
`Walk`/`Attack` instead of `Attackx547`. It changed nothing else: `foes=d2,d2,d2` stayed
frozen, `landed=0/0`, `!stalled`, and the position never moved across 26 samples.

`steps` counts EMITTED walks. The server was refusing every one.

The land under the two stuck stands:

| warrior | stand | land tile | flags | z |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Bram0 | (2587, 408) | 0x0006 | 0 | 15 |
| Bram1 | (2612, 408) | 0x0064 (100) | **0x40 Impassable** | −15 |
| Bram2 | (2637, 408) | 0x00A9 (169) | **0xC0 Impassable\|Wet** | −15 |

**Open water.** `run_warrior_village` stages its roster at `hx + i * spacing` and the GM
teleport that puts a character there ignores passability, so warriors 1 and 2 were placed
in a lake and could not take a single step for 1200 ticks.

`uomap.walkable_run` could not have caught it: it modelled the ground as **z alone**, and
a flat lake is perfect walking ground by that measure. `§58`'s cliff work made the model
look complete and it was only half of one.

- `land_flags`/`land_walkable` read `tiledata.mul`. The layout was verified against known
  tiles rather than assumed — tile 6 reads 0, 100 reads `Impassable`, 169/170 read
  `Impassable | Wet` — because the pre-HS layout parses without error and returns 0 for
  every one of them, which would have read as "everything is walkable".
- `walkable_run` now rejects an impassable ORIGIN and an impassable STEP. Both gates are
  tested separately, because in the live pocket everything was water and each masked the
  other.
- `find_warrior_stand` returns **None** when nothing in range works, and the runner SKIPS
  that warrior with a loud line rather than staging one that cannot move. `avoid`/`min_gap`
  keep a rescued stand out of a neighbour's pocket.

On the real map a three-warrior roster now yields **two** usable pockets: `(2587, 408)`
unchanged, `(2637, 408)` rescued to `(2653, 391)`, and `(2612, 408)` refused — every tile
near it that clears the floor is inside the first warrior's pocket.

A separate `walkable_run` fidelity fix rode along and is NOT a diagnosis of anything: a
player's diagonal step needs BOTH flanking tiles passable (`Movement.cs` line 552 — an NPC
gets `&&` and needs only one), and `_PREY_GAP` puts every creature on a diagonal.

6 mutants, 6 killed. 1686 -> **1689 tests**, ruff clean.
